# Subproblem: P2.1 Sub-cubic antisymmetric amplitudes and fast local updates
**Parent Chain**: P2 -> P2.1  **Status**: OPEN (2026-11-17; must match the Leaf Status Summary). R12.3 (`half` at $F=16$) KILLED at T1 by job 731409 ($\rho(15)=1.915$); round 15 generated seven routes: five KILLED at the gates, R15.6 (learned row gating) KILLED at T1, R15.1 (polished re-score at $V=15$) LIVE as job 742029; R13.7 SHELVED. History: CLOSED by the Round 11 critic with a Saturation Diagnostic, REOPENED the same day by the Round 11c compiled-update falsifier (sublattice hidden rows only). See the Route Register and the Leaf Status Summary.
### 1. Mathematical Abstraction & Barriers
* **Formulation**: Inputs: a configuration $\mathbf{n}\in\{0,1\}^{2V}$ with $N_e$ fermions on $V$ sites ($2V$ spin-orbitals $p=(\sigma,x)$), the set $\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{n})=\{\mathbf{n}':H_{\mathbf{n}'\mathbf{n}}\neq0,\ \mathbf{n}'\neq\mathbf{n}\}$ of connected configurations (for the $t$–$t'$ Hubbard model each particle has $z=8$ neighbours, so $\lvert\mathcal{N}\rvert=\mathcal{O}(zN_e)$), and a backflow determinant $\psi(\mathbf{n})=\det A(\mathbf{n})$, where $A(\mathbf{n})\in\mathbb{R}^{N_e\times N_e}$ has one row per particle, produced by a network from $\mathbf{n}$. Outputs: the ratios $\rho(\mathbf{n}')=\psi(\mathbf{n}')/\psi(\mathbf{n})$ for all $\mathbf{n}'\in\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{n})$, hence the local energy $E_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbf{n})=\sum_{\mathbf{n}'}H_{\mathbf{n}\mathbf{n}'}\rho(\mathbf{n}')$, and the Metropolis ratios. Four cost measures are kept separate: $C_{\mathrm{amp}}$ (one ratio, given data cached at $\mathbf{n}$), $C_E$ (one local energy), $C_{\mathrm{acc}}$ (one accepted Markov move), and $C_{\mathrm{sw}}$ (one sweep of $N_e$ proposals, the cost of one decorrelated sample). "Solved" means: ratios exact to round-off (no truncation bias) with $C_E=o(N_e^3)$ and $C_{\mathrm{sw}}=\mathcal{O}(N_e^3)$, i.e. the asymptotic cost of the Minimal Contrast Foil, for a backflow family that is (a) not reducible to Slater–Jastrow, and (b) whose restriction relative to unrestricted neural-network backflow (NNBF) is stated and shown not to cost accuracy at the competing-order scale $10^{-3}t$ per site. Baselines: unrestricted NNBF (Luo & Clark, PRL 122, 226401) has $C_E=\mathcal{O}(zN_e^4)$ and $C_{\mathrm{sw}}=\mathcal{O}(N_e^4)$.
* **The Node Barrier**:
    * **Fundamental F1 (matrix-output bound; kills "without changing the family" for unrestricted backflow).** If $\Delta(\mathbf{n}')=A(\mathbf{n}')-A(\mathbf{n})$ is generically full rank, as in Luo–Clark NNBF whose last layer emits the whole $2V\times N_e$ orbital matrix, then writing $A(\mathbf{n}')$ alone costs $\Omega(N_e^2)$ per $\mathbf{n}'$, so $C_E=\Omega(zN_e^3)$ before any determinant is taken, and the determinant itself costs matrix-multiplication time per $\mathbf{n}'$. Therefore $C_E=o(N_e^3)$ is impossible for the unrestricted family. Every exact sub-cubic route must use a family with $\operatorname{rank}\Delta(\mathbf{n}')\le m\ll N_e$; then the matrix determinant lemma gives $\rho=\det(I_m+V^{\top}A^{-1}U)$ for $\Delta=UV^{\top}$ with $U,V\in\mathbb{R}^{N_e\times m}$. The honest version of the node is: find a rank-structured family that loses no accuracy.
    * **Fundamental F2 (sweep floor).** Every accepted move changes at least one row, so a carried inverse (or projected Green's function) needs an $\Omega(N_e^2)$ update, and a sweep of $\Theta(N_e)$ moves costs $\Omega(N_e^3)$. This holds even for the Foil. Delayed updates (Chen & Roth, arXiv:2602.05255) change constants, not this exponent. Per decorrelated sample, $\mathcal{O}(N_e^3)$ is therefore the target; $o(N_e^3)$ per sample is out of reach of MCMC with carried inverses.
    * **Practical P1 (locality costs accuracy).** Finite-range convolutional backflow (SCALE, arXiv:2604.25775, PRX Intelligence 1, 023001) changes $\mathcal{O}(r^2)$ rows per hop and reaches $C_{\mathrm{sw}}=\mathcal{O}(N^3)$, but at $16\times16$, $1/8$ doping it sits about $3$–$4\times10^{-3}$ per site above transformer backflow and the deep ACE ansatz of the same paper. That gap exceeds the competing-order scale of the Hard Core.
    * **Practical P2 (depth undoes locality).** In a deep convolutional net (ACE, 16 blocks) a single hop changes features on every site, so the low-rank structure and the fast update disappear.
    * **Practical P3 (attention forward cost).** For transformer backflow (Gu et al., arXiv:2507.02644: embedding $d=256$, 4 layers, $K=4$ determinants), each $\mathbf{n}'$ needs a forward pass of cost $\Theta(V^2d)$, so $C_E=\Theta(N_e^3d)$ whatever determinant algebra is used. Its readout bound $\operatorname{rank}\Delta\le d$ is also vacuous, since $d\ge N_e=224$.
    * **Existing approaches, where each halts**: Lrux (arXiv:2602.05255) needs a backflow that "admits a low-rank representation" and provides none. SCALE is local and has the accuracy gap of P1. ACE is effectively global, P2. Liu & Clark (arXiv:2311.09450, PRB 110, 115124) show that hidden-fermion determinant states (HFDS) with $r$ hidden fermions equal NNBF with a rank-$r$ additive orbital correction, and that pure low-rank corrections are less expressive than full NNBF. Huang, Ou & Ozolins (arXiv:2609.26544) use low-rank parameter shifts inside a multi-determinant expansion, but every amplitude still needs an $\mathcal{O}(N_e^3)$ LU and nothing is carried across Markov steps.
    * **Anti-Solution Space**: (i) finite-range backflow presented as "the same family"; (ii) speedups that hold only at $K=0$ (purely local) or only at infinite range; (iii) truncated-rank or perturbative ratios, which bias $E_{\mathrm{loc}}$ deterministically; (iv) $C_{\mathrm{amp}}$ claims that ignore the network forward pass per $\mathbf{n}'$; (v) claims of $C_{\mathrm{sw}}=o(N_e^3)$; (vi) exponents fitted where interpreter overhead dominates rather than arithmetic; (vii) benchmarks at half filling with $t'=0$, where $z=4$ and nodes are simpler.
### 1b. Analogy Map
Written 2026-11-17 in the documentation pass, after the routes: the leaf predates the skill's Analogy Map rule, so most entries record which route already instantiated an analogy. Status: untried, tried (R<id>) or repaired (R<id>). Notation: $A$ is the $N_e\times N_e$ (or augmented) matrix whose determinant is the amplitude, $N_e$ the electron number, $V$ the number of sites, $V_h$ the number of hidden rows, $z$ the number of connected configurations per particle.
* **Barrier B1 (from F1): "evaluate many determinant ratios $\det(A+U_cW_c^{\top})/\det A$, one for each of $\mathcal{O}(zN_e)$ low-rank perturbations of one matrix, exactly and without forming each perturbed matrix."** It is solvable only when every perturbation has small rank $m$; the network that produces $A$ decides whether it does.

### 2. Candidate Routes & Falsification Gates
* **Route 1**: Finite-range (one-layer convolutional) backflow, with a carried inverse and Woodbury updates of rank $m=\mathcal{O}(r^2)$. Point of departure: SCALE.
  - Gate 0 (Novelty & Adoption): FAIL. This is SCALE (arXiv:2604.25775) almost exactly; Lrux (arXiv:2602.05255) supplies the update kernels. Finite-range backflow is not the accurate standard because its $16\times16$ energies trail ACE and transformer backflow by about $3$–$4\times10^{-3}$ per site. The route has no mechanism that closes that gap.
  - Gate 1 (Conservation & Asymptotics): PASS. Exact and number-conserving; $C_E=\mathcal{O}(N_e^2)$, $C_{\mathrm{sw}}=\mathcal{O}(N_e^3)$.
  - Gate 2 (Baseline Gameability): PASS. Published SCALE energies are well below Jastrow–Slater VMC.
  - Gate 3 (Hard-Core Shield): PASS. Locality does not need half filling or 1D.
  - *Verdict*: KILLED
* **Route 2**: Readout-rank exact ratios for token-wise global backflow. If row $j$ is $a_j=\phi_0(p_j)+t_{x_j}(\mathbf{n})W_{\mathrm{out}}$ with token embedding $t_x\in\mathbb{R}^d$, then $\operatorname{rank}\Delta\le d+1$ for any global network, and with $W_{\mathrm{out}}A^{-1}$ cached, $C_{\mathrm{amp}}=\mathcal{O}(N_ed^2)$ exactly, with no change to the transformer-backflow family.
  - Gate 0 (Novelty & Adoption): PASS. Lrux states the general low-rank condition, but no paper applies the readout-width bound to transformer backflow. It is unused because $d$ is not small compared with $N_e$ in practice.
  - Gate 1 (Conservation & Asymptotics): FAIL. Each $\mathbf{n}'$ needs a full attention forward pass of cost $\Theta(V^2d)$, because one changed token changes every token after layer 1. So $C_E=\Theta(zN_e\cdot V^2d)=\Theta(N_e^3d)$, which is no better than the cubic barrier. In published runs the rank bound is also vacuous ($d=256\ge N_e=224$, arXiv:2507.02644).
  - Gate 2 (Baseline Gameability): PASS (moot).
  - Gate 3 (Hard-Core Shield): PASS (moot).
  - *Verdict*: KILLED
* **Route 3 (Local + Global-Bottleneck backflow, LGB-BF)**: The row of particle $j$ at spin-orbital $p_j=(\sigma_j,x_j)$ is
$$a_j(\mathbf{n})=\Phi_0[p_j]+h_{x_j}(\mathbf{n})\,\Theta_{\sigma_j}+\big(u_{x_j}(\mathbf{n})\odot c(\mathbf{n})\big)\,\Omega_{\sigma_j}.$$
Here $h_x=\tanh(W_1\,\mathrm{patch}_r(x)+b_1)\in\mathbb{R}^F$ are full-rank local features of range $r$ (one convolution); $c=\tanh\!\big(\sum_x P_x h_x\big)\in\mathbb{R}^K$ are $K$ global channels, each a position-dependent pooling of all local features; $u_x=W_uh_x\in\mathbb{R}^K$ are local gates; $\Phi_0$ are mean-field orbitals; and $\Theta_\sigma\in\mathbb{R}^{F\times N_e}$, $\Omega_\sigma\in\mathbb{R}^{K\times N_e}$. A hop fully changes the rows of the set $S$ of particles within range $r$ of the two touched sites ($\lvert S\rvert=\mathcal{O}(r^2)$), and changes every other row only through $\Delta c$, which is a rank-$\le2K$ change. So $A'=A+E_SD+U_gW$ with $m=\lvert S\rvert+2K$. Every inner product needed for the ratio comes from the carried projected Green's function $G=[\Phi_0;\Theta_\uparrow;\Theta_\downarrow;\Omega_\uparrow;\Omega_\downarrow]A^{-1}$, using $a_SA^{-1}=E_S^{\top}$. This gives $C_{\mathrm{amp}}=\mathcal{O}\big(N_e(\lvert S\rvert(F+K)+K^2)\big)$, $C_E=\mathcal{O}\big(zN_e^2(\lvert S\rvert(F+K)+K^2)\big)$, $C_{\mathrm{acc}}=\mathcal{O}\big((2V+2F+2K)N_e m\big)$ and $C_{\mathrm{sw}}=\mathcal{O}(N_e^3)$ at fixed density. The improvement is in $C_E$, from $\mathcal{O}(N_e^4)$ to $\mathcal{O}(N_e^2)$ at fixed $(r,F,K)$, and in $C_{\mathrm{sw}}$, from $\mathcal{O}(N_e^4)$ to the Foil's $\mathcal{O}(N_e^3)$. Restriction, stated plainly: relative to unrestricted NNBF the family is restricted to full-rank finite-range backflow plus a rank-$K$ global part. It strictly contains SCALE ($K=0$), and a rank-$K$ hidden-fermion-type correction in the sense of Liu–Clark. It stays sub-cubic in $C_E$ while $K=o(N_e^{1/2})$.
  - Gate 0 (Novelty & Adoption): PASS. The pieces exist separately: local backflow with Woodbury (arXiv:2604.25775), rank-$r$ global corrections and the HFDS equivalence (arXiv:2311.09450), and low-rank parameter shifts (arXiv:2609.26544). I found no work that combines a full-rank local backflow with an $\mathbf{n}$-dependent rank-$K$ global channel and an exact $C_E=\mathcal{O}(N_e^2)$ carried-Green's-function algorithm. Each is not standard for a known reason: local backflow misses long-range correlations (P1), and pure low-rank corrections are weaker than NNBF (Liu–Clark). The route's concrete mechanism targets that barrier: the global channels carry long-range information (stripe phase, antiferromagnetic domain, hole arrangement) on top of a full-rank local backflow, at an additive cost of $\mathcal{O}(N_eK^2)$ per ratio. Whether this closes the gap is the Sharpest Gap below.
  - Gate 1 (Conservation & Asymptotics): PASS. Exact (matrix determinant lemma plus Woodbury, no truncation); number-conserving hops only; spin and lattice symmetries are those of the network (translation-equivariant local part). No hardness claim is made: this is a cost result for a fixed family, not a convergence guarantee.
  - Gate 2 (Baseline Gameability): PASS. The Foil already has $C_E=\mathcal{O}(N_e^2)$, so cost alone is gameable. The route's claim is backflow expressivity at Foil cost, and its $K=0$ subfamily (SCALE) already beats Jastrow–Slater VMC in published energies. Exactness against brute-force determinants is also part of the metric, which the Foil cannot satisfy for this family.
  - Gate 3 (Hard-Core Shield): PASS. The test instance is doped ($\delta=1/8$), has $t'=-0.2$ ($z=8$, non-stoquastic) and is 2D periodic. Nothing uses particle–hole symmetry, 1D, or $U=0$.
  - *Verdict*: SURVIVED
* **Route 4**: Unbiased stochastic ratio estimator for unrestricted NNBF. $E_{\mathrm{loc}}$ is linear in $\rho$, so an unbiased estimator of $\det(I+A^{-1}\Delta)$ would keep $E$ and the gradient covariance unbiased. The estimator would use stochastic Chebyshev log-determinants (Han, Malioutov & Shin, arXiv:1503.06394) with a Russian-roulette exponential.
  - Gate 0 (Novelty & Adoption): PASS. Stochastic log-determinants are standard, but I found no unbiased ratio estimator used for fermionic NQS local energies.
  - Gate 1 (Conservation & Asymptotics): FAIL. F1 still applies: forming $\Delta(\mathbf{n}')$ for unrestricted NNBF costs $\Omega(N_e^2)$ per $\mathbf{n}'$, so $C_E=\Omega(N_e^3)$. In addition, an unbiased estimate of $\exp(\operatorname{tr}\log(I+X))$ needs a signed random series whose variance grows like $\exp(\mathcal{O}(\lVert\log(I+X)\rVert))$. That blows up near nodes ($\rho\to0$) and at large $\rho$, exactly where $E_{\mathrm{loc}}$ variance is set, and destroys the zero-variance property.
  - Gate 2 (Baseline Gameability): PASS (moot).
  - Gate 3 (Hard-Core Shield): PASS (moot).
  - *Verdict*: KILLED
### 3. Top Surviving Route & Sharpest Gap
* **Mechanism**: LGB-BF (Route 3). The backflow change under a hop is forced to be (full-rank on $\mathcal{O}(r^2)$ rows) plus (rank $2K$ on all rows). The projected Green's function $G$ is carried across Markov steps by Woodbury at $\mathcal{O}(VN_em)$ per accepted move. Each of the $\mathcal{O}(zN_e)$ local-energy ratios is an $(\lvert S\rvert+2K)$-dimensional determinant assembled in $\mathcal{O}(N_e(\lvert S\rvert(F+K)+K^2))$, and the incremental network update costs $\mathcal{O}(\lvert C\rvert(F\cdot\mathrm{patch}+FK))$, where $C$ is the set of sites whose patch contains a touched site.
* **Sharpest Gap**: It is unproven that a fixed, $N_e$-independent number $K$ of global channels (any $K=o(N_e^{1/2})$ keeps $C_E$ sub-cubic) closes the about $3$–$4\times10^{-3}$ per site gap between local backflow (SCALE) and global backflow (ACE or transformer) at $16\times16$, $\delta=1/8$, $t'=-0.2$. It is equally unproven that it reproduces the same stripe and pairing correlations $C(r)$. Liu–Clark's finding that pure rank-$r$ corrections underperform NNBF is the main risk, mitigated here only because the local part stays full-rank. The cost and exactness part is settled by construction and is what the 24-hour test checks; the accuracy part needs a follow-up training comparison of $K=0$ versus $K\in\{4,16\}$ versus transformer backflow at $8\times8$ and $16\times16$. That comparison needs JAX on a GPU, which this numpy-only environment does not have, and should take at most a few GPU-days.
### 4. 24-Hour Decisive Test & Compute Execution
* **Minimal Instance**: $L\times L$ periodic $t$–$t'$–$U$ Hubbard model, $t=1$, $t'=-0.2$, $U=8$, $\delta=1/8$ ($N_e=2\,\mathrm{round}(V(1-\delta)/2)$, $N_\uparrow=N_\downarrow$), $L\in\{8,12,16,20,24,28,32\}$ ($N_e=56$ to $896$). LGB-BF with $r=1$, $F=16$, $K=4$ and random weights (an algorithmic test). Plus an exhaustive $3\times2$, $N_\uparrow=N_\downarrow=2$ check that $\sum_{\mathbf{n}}\psi^2E_{\mathrm{loc}}/\sum\psi^2=\psi^{\top}H\psi/\psi^{\top}\psi$ against the explicit Jordan–Wigner Hamiltonian. That check proves the fast ratios implement the same family with the correct fermionic signs.
* **Scalar Metric $M$**: $M=\mathrm{d}\log T_{E,\mathrm{fast}}/\mathrm{d}\log N_e$, the least-squares slope over the four largest sizes, where $T_{E,\mathrm{fast}}$ is the single-thread wall time (median over 3 configurations) of one full local energy, i.e. all $\mathcal{O}(zN_e)$ ratios. It is gated by $\epsilon_{\max}=\max\big(\max_{\mathbf{n}'}\lvert\rho_{\mathrm{fast}}-\rho_{\mathrm{brute}}\rvert/(1+\lvert\rho_{\mathrm{brute}}\rvert),\ \lvert E_{\mathrm{loc,fast}}-E_{\mathrm{loc,brute}}\rvert/(1+\lvert E_{\mathrm{loc,brute}}\rvert),\ \text{enumeration error}\big)$, where $\rho_{\mathrm{brute}}$ is computed by rebuilding $A(\mathbf{n}')$ and taking an LU log-determinant. The script also reports the brute-force local-energy slope $s_B$ (regime sanity), the fast-sweep slope $s_{\mathrm{sw}}$, and the Woodbury drift of $G$ after one sweep.
* **Execution Plan**: `/home/jek354/research/.claude/worktrees/nqs-brainstorm/brainstorms/nqs-lattice-fermions/tests/P2.1_subcubic_amplitudes/micro_test.py` and `/home/jek354/research/.claude/worktrees/nqs-brainstorm/brainstorms/nqs-lattice-fermions/tests/P2.1_subcubic_amplitudes/submit_test.sbatch` (partition `kim`, 1 CPU, 8 GB, 6 h, BLAS pinned to 1 thread, logs `P2.1_<jobid>.out/.err` and `results_P2.1.json` next to the script). Command, not run: `sbatch /home/jek354/research/.claude/worktrees/nqs-brainstorm/brainstorms/nqs-lattice-fermions/tests/P2.1_subcubic_amplitudes/submit_test.sbatch`. The expected wall time is well under 1 h; the largest brute-force local energy at $N_e=896$ is the dominant cost, at about minutes.
* **Thresholds**: PASS if $M\le2.3$ and $\epsilon_{\max}\le10^{-9}$ and $s_B\ge3.3$ (which proves the instance is in the arithmetic-dominated regime where brute force is about $N_e^4$) and $s_{\mathrm{sw}}\le3.3$. FAIL if $M\ge2.8$ or $\epsilon_{\max}>10^{-6}$. AMBIGUOUS otherwise, with the pre-committed action: rerun with `--sizes 24,28,32,36,40 --nconf 2`; if still AMBIGUOUS, declare the asymptotic claim FAILED. A PASS settles cost and exactness only. The accuracy question (Sharpest Gap) is not settled by it and must not be reported as settled.
* **Smoke-test evidence**: run on the login node in about 1 s, smallest instance:

The smoke verdict is not meaningful, because at $N_e\le32$ interpreter overhead dominates; the $s_B\ge3.3$ guard exists for exactly this reason. What it does establish: fast ratios agree with brute-force determinants to $\sim10^{-14}$, and the enumerated energy equals $\psi^{\top}H\psi/\psi^{\top}\psi$ to $4\times10^{-16}$. A second brief check (`--sizes 8,12 --nconf 1 --no-check-enum`, under 1 s) gave $\epsilon_{\mathrm{ratio}}\le1.3\times10^{-12}$ at $N_e=126$, and the fast local energy was already faster than brute force there ($0.127$ s versus $0.165$ s).
### 5. Local Autopsy Log
* **Route 1 (finite-range convolutional backflow with Woodbury)**: Gate 0. It is SCALE (arXiv:2604.25775) plus Lrux kernels. Its known adoption barrier, an energy gap of about $3$–$4\times10^{-3}$ per site to global backflow at $16\times16$, larger than the $10^{-3}t$ competing-order scale, is left untouched.
* **Route 2 (readout-rank ratios for transformer backflow)**: Gate 1. The attention forward pass per connected configuration is $\Theta(V^2d)$, so $C_E=\Theta(N_e^3d)$ regardless of determinant algebra, and the rank bound $d$ is not smaller than $N_e$ in published runs ($d=256$, $N_e=224$).
* **Route 4 (unbiased stochastic determinant ratios for unrestricted NNBF)**: Gate 1. The matrix-output bound ($\Omega(N_e^2)$ per $\mathbf{n}'$ just to form $\Delta$) keeps $C_E=\Omega(N_e^3)$, and the unbiased exponential of a stochastic log-determinant has variance exponential in $\lVert\log(I+X)\rVert$, which diverges near nodes.
### Round 2 — Mutation & Replenishment (2026-11-11)
* **Autopsy of the round-1 survivor (LGB-BF, Route 3)**, boundary conditions for this round:
    * **B1 (Gate 2, test could not fail).** The round-1 metric was the timing slope of a Woodbury implementation on random weights. The foil already has $C_E=\mathcal{O}(N_e^2)$, so the metric did not separate the route from the foil, and the only non-trivial claim (accuracy at fixed $K$) was untested. Consequence: the round-2 test measures accuracy, namely $\Delta E_{\mathrm{tot}}$ and $\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}$ against full NNBF at matched parameter count, by exact sums on the P1.1 clusters. Cost is a side check only.
    * **B2 (Gate 0, family too small).** LGB's global term $u_x(\mathrm{patch})\odot c(\mathbf{n})$, with $c$ a single pooled $\tanh$, is a strict subfamily of the Liu–Clark rank-$r$ correction, whose $r\times N_e$ coefficient block is an arbitrary function of $\mathbf{n}$. Consequence: every round-2 family must contain the Liu–Clark / HFDS family as a special case, and the restriction relative to NNBF must be measured, not argued.
    * **B3 (P1.1 result).** Width fixes amplitudes, not signs. A cheap family is only worth having if it keeps the sign quality $\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}$ of full NNBF. Conversely, a deficit that is purely amplitude can be repaired by a positive factor whose hop response costs $\mathcal{O}(H_a)$, where $H_a$ is its hidden width.
* **Notation for this round.** The configuration is $\mathbf{n}$, with $N_\sigma$ particles of spin $\sigma\in\{\uparrow,\downarrow\}$ on $V$ sites; $\mathrm{occ}_\sigma(\mathbf{n})$ is the sorted list of occupied sites; $x=2\mathbf{n}-1\in\{\pm1\}^{2V}$ is the network input. For a matrix $X$ with one row per site, $X[\mathrm{occ}_\sigma]$ keeps the occupied rows. The reference orbitals are $\Phi_{0\sigma}\in\mathbb{R}^{V\times N_\sigma}$. The hidden-fermion orbitals are $\Phi_{h,k\sigma}\in\mathbb{R}^{V\times r}$, where $r$ is the rank per spin and per term. The configuration-dependent coefficient blocks $C_{k\sigma}(\mathbf{n})\in\mathbb{R}^{r\times N_\sigma}$ are produced by a one-hidden-layer $\tanh$ "sign trunk" of width $H_s$. The positive amplitude factor is $e^{J(\mathbf{n})}$ with $J=w_a^{\mathsf T}\tanh(W_ax+b_a)$, of width $H_a$. The reference matrix is $A_{0\sigma}=\Phi_{0\sigma}[\mathrm{occ}_\sigma]$, and the table is $Y_{k\sigma}=A_{0\sigma}^{-1}\Phi_{h,k\sigma}[\mathrm{occ}_\sigma]\in\mathbb{R}^{N_\sigma\times r}$.
* **Generation Batch 2**:
  - **Route 2.1 (Mutated from 3 via Operation A.1 and A.3): split-capacity hidden-fermion backflow (SC-HFDS).**
    $$\psi(\mathbf{n})=e^{J(\mathbf{n})}\prod_{\sigma}\det\!\big(\Phi_{0\sigma}[\mathrm{occ}_\sigma]+\Phi_{h,\sigma}[\mathrm{occ}_\sigma]\,C_\sigma(\mathbf{n})\big).$$
    The Gate-0 sub-component (LGB's pooled global channel) is replaced by Liu–Clark's unrestricted coefficient block $C_\sigma(\mathbf{n})$, so the family contains the Liu–Clark / HFDS family exactly ($J\equiv0$), which answers B2. The Gate-2 sub-component (the metric) is retargeted to the higher-order witness $\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}$, which answers B1. The mechanism aimed at the adoption barrier: Liu–Clark find that HFDS-type low-rank corrections underperform NNBF at matched size. B3 suggests that this deficit may be amplitude, which a separate wide positive network $e^{J}$ repairs at $\mathcal{O}(H_a)$ per hop. The capacity is therefore split: a narrow sign trunk ($H_s=64$) and a wide amplitude network ($H_a$ chosen to match NNBF's parameter count). Hop response: a hop of particle $i$ from site $x$ to site $y$ changes row $i$ plus the rank-$r$ term $\Phi_{h,\sigma}[\mathrm{occ}']\Delta C$, so $\operatorname{rank}\Delta\le r+1$.
    Exact ratio with carried quantities. The carried quantities are $A_0^{-1}$, $G_0=\Phi_0A_0^{-1}$ ($V\times N_\sigma$), $Y$, and $\mathcal{H}=G_0\Phi_h[\mathrm{occ}]$ ($V\times r$). They give $\rho_0=G_0[y,i]$ and $Y'=Y+A_0^{-1}[:,i]\otimes(\Phi_h[y]-\mathcal{H}[y])/\rho_0$. The ratio is
    $$\frac{\psi(\mathbf{n}')}{\psi(\mathbf{n})}=e^{J'-J}\rho_0\frac{\det(I_r+C'_\sigma Y'_\sigma)\det(I_r+C'_{\bar\sigma}Y_{\bar\sigma})}{\det(I_r+C_\sigma Y_\sigma)\det(I_r+C_{\bar\sigma}Y_{\bar\sigma})},$$
    where $\bar\sigma$ is the other spin. The cost per connected configuration is $\mathcal{O}(N_e(H_sr+r^2)+H_a)$, so $C_E=\mathcal{O}(zN_e^2H_sr)$. HFPS (Chen, Roth et al., arXiv:2507.10705) states $\mathcal{O}(N^2\tilde N)$ per new Pfaffian, with $\tilde N$ hidden fermions, and hence $\mathcal{O}(N^3)$ per VMC step. The in-place-row convention needs no Jordan–Wigner sign, and the smoke test verifies this pointwise against $(H\psi)(\mathbf{n})/\psi(\mathbf{n})$ on every configuration.
    - Gate 0 (Novelty & Adoption): PASS. HFDS (Robledo Moreno et al., PNAS 119, e2122059119) and its NNBF reading (Liu & Clark, arXiv:2311.09450) exist. HFPS (arXiv:2507.10705) already multiplies a hidden-fermion Pfaffian by a Jastrow taken from 4 of the 32 output channels of the same deep CNN. Why low-rank families are not the default: Liu–Clark report that direct SPO updates (full NNBF) are more expressive at matched size, and HFPS pays $\mathcal{O}(N^3)$ per step because the hidden block is rewritten and re-contracted per configuration. What this route adds, as a concrete mechanism aimed at each wall:
        * (i) A carried-table ratio, in which $C$ multiplies a fixed $\Phi_h$ only from the right, giving $\mathcal{O}(N_e)$ per ratio. This removes the $\mathcal{O}(N^2\tilde N)$ per-ratio contraction.
        * (ii) A capacity split justified by a measured sign/amplitude decomposition, with the explicit, falsifiable claim that the Liu–Clark deficit is amplitude and not sign. No published work measures $E_{\mathrm{sign}}$ for HFDS-type versus NNBF states.
        * Risk stated plainly: if the decisive test shows the deficit is sign, the route dies, and the result itself (low-rank response costs sign) closes the "sub-cubic without losing accuracy" question for this family class.
    - Gate 1 (Conservation & Asymptotics): PASS. Exact (matrix determinant lemma plus Sherman–Morrison, no truncation); number-conserving; $C_E=\mathcal{O}(N_e^2)$ at fixed $(H_s,r,H_a)$; sweep $\mathcal{O}(N_e^3)$, which meets the F2 floor. No convergence or hardness claim is made.
    - Gate 2 (Baseline Gameability): PASS. The metric is $\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}$ against full NNBF at matched parameter count. On the decisive instances the foil's sign error is about $2\times$ NNBF's (P1.1: $0.154$ vs $0.077$ on $N_\uparrow=N_\downarrow=4$). An explicit control arm, Slater $\times$ neural Jastrow (`sjnet`, $r=0$, same parameter count), must lose by the pre-committed factor ($\gamma\le0.75$), otherwise the low-rank channel is doing no sign work and the route FAILS.
    - Gate 3 (Hard-Core Shield): PASS. The test instances are the three doped $4\times3$ PBC $t'=-0.25$ clusters of P1.1, which are non-stoquastic. [Corrected in round 3: the competitor once cited for the $N_\uparrow=N_\downarrow=4$ instance is a triplet at another momentum, so all three instances test the sign half only.]
    - *Verdict*: SURVIVED (decisive test below).
  - **Route 2.2 (Operation B.3, cross-field translation: the multi-determinant table method of continuum QMC): shared-reference low-rank multi-determinant (SK-LR).**
    $$\psi(\mathbf{n})=e^{J(\mathbf{n})}\prod_\sigma\det A_{0\sigma}\;\sum_{k=1}^{K}w_k\prod_\sigma\det\!\big(I_r+C_{k\sigma}(\mathbf{n})Y_{k\sigma}(\mathbf{n})\big),$$
    where $w_k$ are trainable weights. Every term shares the reference $A_{0\sigma}$, so one carried inverse serves all $K$ terms. This is the move that made multi-Slater–Jastrow QMC cost quadratic in particle number and linear in the number of excitations (Clark, Morales, McMinis, Kim & Scuseria, J. Chem. Phys. 135, 244105, 2011, arXiv:1106.2456). Here it is ported to configuration-dependent coefficients. Each term has a rank-$\le r+1$ hop response, so the per-hop cost is $K$ times that of Route 2.1, and $C_E=\mathcal{O}(zKN_e^2H_sr)$.
    Sign-relevant strict containment, provable at fixed table. By Cauchy–Binet, $\det(I_r+CY)=\sum_{\ell\le r}\sum_{\lvert P\rvert=\lvert Q\rvert=\ell}\det C[Q,P]\,\det Y[P,Q]$. So a single HFDS term (Liu–Clark, and Route 2.1) makes $\psi/\psi_{\mathrm{ref}}$ a linear form in the minors of the table $Y$, whose coefficient vector lies in the image of the minor map (a determinantal variety). $K$ terms give its $K$-secant, which is strictly larger for $K\ge2$, $r\ge2$. In addition, the $\uparrow\downarrow$ products are summed, so the coefficient tensor is no longer a single product across spins. The node set $\{\psi=0\}$ is the zero set of this form in the table entries. The family therefore has strictly more nodal freedom than HFDS($r$) on the same table, and SK-LR($K{=}1$, $J{=}0$) is exactly HFDS($r$). This is the "provably richer than Liu–Clark in a sign-relevant way" requirement. It is a statement about the representable node sets at a fixed reference and table, not about learnability, which the test measures.
    - Gate 0 (Novelty & Adoption): PASS.
        * Multi-determinant NNBF exists. Loehr & Clark (arXiv:2510.26906) find that multi-determinant expansions beat width increases, but each determinant is a full NNBF determinant with its own $\mathcal{O}(N_e^3)$ amplitude, and their projected-state optimization costs about 3000 GPU-hours, which is the massive-compute wall.
        * The table method exists only for fixed CI coefficients over fixed orbitals.
        * HFPS uses one Pfaffian.
        * I found no ansatz that sums configuration-dependent low-rank terms over a single carried reference. The adoption wall it targets is the $K\times\mathcal{O}(N_e^4)$ cost of multi-determinant NNBF, which this route replaces by $K\times\mathcal{O}(N_e^2)$, while keeping the nodal freedom of a sum. P1.1 says that freedom is the binding one, since width does not buy it.
    - Gate 1 (Conservation & Asymptotics): PASS. Exact, number-conserving, $C_E=\mathcal{O}(N_e^2)$ at fixed $(K,r,H_s,H_a)$, one carried inverse per spin. The only division is by the term sum at the sampled $\mathbf{n}$, which $\lvert\psi\rvert^2$ sampling keeps away from zero; ratios at $\mathbf{n}'$ stay exact even when the term sum there cancels.
    - Gate 2 (Baseline Gameability): PASS. The same metric and the same `sjnet` control as Route 2.1. The additional control `mdnnbf` (unrestricted $K$-determinant NNBF at matched parameters) sets the reference, so "sum of determinants helps anyone" is separated from "the cheap restricted sum keeps up".
    - Gate 3 (Hard-Core Shield): PASS. The same instances and the same competitor statement as Route 2.1.
    - *Verdict*: SURVIVED (shares the decisive test).
  - **Route 2.3 (Operation B.1, frame transformation from the particle–hole (Slater) frame to the particle–particle (geminal) frame): low-rank-response AGP.** $\psi=e^J\det\big(F(\mathbf{n})[\mathrm{occ}_\uparrow,\mathrm{occ}_\downarrow]\big)$ with $F(\mathbf{n})=F_0+P\,Q(\mathbf{n})^{\mathsf T}$, where $F_0\in\mathbb{R}^{V\times V}$ is a pair function and $P,Q\in\mathbb{R}^{V\times r}$. It contains the projected-BCS foil ($r=0$). A one-sided correction keeps the ratio at $\mathcal{O}(N_er^2)$. A two-sided correction creates a new–new block $\Delta Q^{\mathsf T}B^{-1}\Delta R$ that costs $\mathcal{O}(N_e^2r)$ per hop, i.e. cubic $C_E$.
    - Gate 0 (Novelty & Adoption): FAIL. This is the hidden-fermion Pfaffian state (HFPS, arXiv:2507.10705, and its application arXiv:2511.07566) restricted to $S_z=0$ singlet pairing. HFPS already has a rank-$\tilde N$ configuration-dependent visible–hidden block, a Jastrow, and low-rank updates. Only the carried-table cost reduction of Route 2.1 transfers, and that is recorded under Route 2.1.
    - Gate 1: PASS (one-sided). Gate 2: PASS. Gate 3: PASS.
    - *Verdict*: KILLED.
  - **Route 2.4 (Operation B.1, momentum / natural-orbital frame): Thouless rotation into an active virtual window.** $\Phi(\mathbf{n})=\Phi_{\mathrm{occ}}+\Phi_{\mathrm{virt}}^{\mathrm{act}}\kappa(\mathbf{n})$, with $\Phi_{\mathrm{virt}}^{\mathrm{act}}$ the $r$ mean-field virtual orbitals nearest the Fermi level (antinodal momenta), and $\kappa(\mathbf{n})\in\mathbb{R}^{r\times N_e}$.
    - Gate 0 (Novelty & Adoption): FAIL. This is Route 2.1 with $\Phi_h$ frozen to $\Phi^{\mathrm{act}}_{\mathrm{virt}}$, i.e. a subfamily of HFDS with no new mechanism. Freezing $\Phi_h$ only removes parameters that SR can already set.
    - Gates 1–3: PASS (moot).
    - *Verdict*: KILLED.
  - **Route 2.5 (Operation B.2, active probing): delayed-acceptance two-stage Metropolis.** Proposals are screened with the cheap family, and the full NNBF ratio is evaluated only for survivors (Christen & Fox, J. Comput. Graph. Stat. 14, 795, 2005).
    - Gate 1 (Conservation & Asymptotics): FAIL. It lowers $C_{\mathrm{acc}}$, but $E_{\mathrm{loc}}$ still needs every one of the $\mathcal{O}(zN_e)$ full-family ratios, so the F1 bound $C_E=\Omega(N_e^3)$ is untouched. Replacing the full ratios in $E_{\mathrm{loc}}$ by surrogate ratios is either biased or a single-proposal stochastic replacement, which the global anti-pattern already covers.
    - Gates 0, 2 and 3: moot.
    - *Verdict*: KILLED.
  - **Route 2.6 (Operation B.3, fast multipole / $\mathcal{H}$-matrix backflow).** Near-field full-rank rows plus rank-$p$ far-field channels per quadtree level, so $\operatorname{rank}\Delta=\mathcal{O}(r^2+p\log V)$.
    - Gate 2 (Baseline Gameability): FAIL. The far-field channels are pooled rank-$p$ channels, the same structure the critic showed is a strict subfamily of Liu–Clark (B2).
    - Gate 3 (Hard-Core Shield): FAIL for testing. On every cluster that admits exact sums, all sites lie in the near field, so the accuracy claim cannot be tested within 24 h of CPU without abandoning exact $E_{\mathrm{sign}}$.
    - *Verdict*: KILLED.
### Top Surviving Route & Sharpest Gap (Round 2)
* **Mechanism (Route 2.2, SK-LR; Route 2.1 is its $K=1$ case and is tested in the same job)**:
    * All determinant terms share one reference determinant.
    * Each term's configuration dependence enters only as a right factor $C_{k\sigma}(\mathbf{n})$ against a fixed hidden-orbital table.
    * The exact ratio for every hop is therefore an $r\times r$ determinant built from the carried $A_0^{-1}$, $G_0$, $Y$ and $\mathcal{H}$ in $\mathcal{O}(N_e)$, and $C_E=\mathcal{O}(N_e^2)$.
    * Sign freedom comes from the sum over terms (the $K$-secant of the minor map), not from network width.
    * Amplitude freedom comes from a separate positive network that costs $\mathcal{O}(H_a)$ per hop.
* **Sharpest Gap**:
    * On the decisive clusters $N_\sigma\in\{4,5\}$, so $r=2$ is 40–50% of full rank. A PASS shows that low-rank response loses no sign quality where the restriction is mild. It does not show that a fixed $r$ suffices at $N_\sigma\sim100$, where the restriction is severe. That scaling question cannot be answered with exact $E_{\mathrm{sign}}$, and needs a VMC comparison at $8\times8$ against NNBF using the variance-extrapolated energy as the proxy.
    * A FAIL here is decisive, because the restriction only gets tighter with size.
    * Second gap: SR seed variance at the 10–15% level could blur $\rho_s$. Two seeds per arm, best-energy seed per arm, and the AMBIGUOUS rerun rule are the mitigation.
### 24-Hour Decisive Test (Round 2)
* **Minimal Instance**: the three P1.1 clusters ($4\times3$ PBC, $t=1$, $t'=-0.25$): $N_\uparrow=N_\downarrow=5$ at $U=8$ ($D=627264$); $N_\uparrow=N_\downarrow=4$ at $U=8$ ($D=245025$; no same-sector competitor, corrected in round 3); $N_\uparrow=N_\downarrow=5$ at $U=4$. Exact sums throughout; ED reference by Lanczos.
* **Arms (all at the parameter count of NNBF width 128, $P^\star=18800$ or $15680$, matched within one hidden unit)**:
    * `nnbf128`, the control: full NNBF, as in P1.1.
    * `mdnnbf`, the control: 4 unrestricted NNBF determinants, $H_s=35$/36.
    * `lchfds`: the Liu–Clark arm, HFDS $r=2$, $H_s=414$/379, no amplitude net.
    * `schfds`: Route 2.1, $r=2$, $H_s=64$, $H_a=605$/496.
    * `sklr`: Route 2.2, $K=4$, $r=2$, $H_s=64$, $H_a=449$/370.
    * `sjnet`: the Gate-2 control, Slater $\times$ neural Jastrow, $H_a=718$/599.
    * Training is identical to P1.1: exact-force SR, 1500 iterations, step 0.05, shift $10^{-3}$, 2000-sample metric, same UHF reference. There are 2 seeds per arm.
* **Scalar Metrics**: for route $R$, take the best-energy seed of each arm. With $\mathrm{Ref}_X=\min(X_{\texttt{nnbf128}},X_{\texttt{mdnnbf}})$:
    * $\rho_s=\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}(R)/\mathrm{Ref}_{\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}}$;
    * $\rho_t=\Delta E_{\mathrm{tot}}(R)/\mathrm{Ref}_{\Delta E_{\mathrm{tot}}}$;
    * $\rho_C=e_C(R)/\mathrm{Ref}_{e_C}$, where $e_C$ is P1.1's maximal long-range singlet pair-correlation error;
    * $\gamma=\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}(R)/\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}(\texttt{sjnet})$.
    * $E_{\mathrm{sign}}$ is P1.1's upper estimate (minimum of 4 L-BFGS-B starts and the ten Haaf fixed node).
* **Thresholds (pre-committed, implemented in `--mode aggregate`)**:
    * PASS if on every instance $\rho_s\le1.15$, $\rho_t\le1.30$, $\rho_C\le1.30$ and $\gamma\le0.75$. Route 2.1 must in addition beat `lchfds` in $\Delta E_{\mathrm{tot}}$ on every instance.
    * FAIL if on any instance $\rho_s\ge1.5$, $\gamma\ge0.9$ or $\rho_C\ge2$. Route 2.1 also FAILS if it beats `lchfds` on no instance.
    * AMBIGUOUS otherwise. Action: two more seeds for the route and both controls; if still AMBIGUOUS, FAIL.
    * Cost side check (task 36): $L\times L$ PBC with $L=8,\dots,32$, $\delta=1/8$. The fast and brute-force local energies must agree to $10^{-8}$. The slope of the fast $E_{\mathrm{loc}}$ time over the three largest sizes must be at most $2.4$, with the brute slope at least $3.3$ as the regime guard. A route survives only with both accuracy PASS and side-check PASS.
* **Execution Plan**: `tests/P2.1_subcubic_amplitudes_r2/micro_test.py` and `tests/P2.1_subcubic_amplitudes_r2/submit_test.sbatch`, with `p11_core.py` a verbatim copy of the P1.1 code (Hamiltonian, ED, UHF, NNBF, `budget()`).
    * Array 0–36, partition `kim`, 8 CPUs, 24 GB, 24 h.
    * Measured cost: one SR iteration on $D=245025$ takes 2.3 s (`nnbf128`) to 7 s (`sklr`) with 4 threads. The worst task (instance 0, `sklr`) is about 8.5 h.
    * Command, not run: `cd tests/P2.1_subcubic_amplitudes_r2 && sbatch submit_test.sbatch`.
    * Afterwards: `python3 micro_test.py --mode aggregate`.
* **Smoke-test evidence** (login node, 1.3 s). The training lines are a 15-iteration run on the $3\times2$ smoke cluster, which is not a Hard-Core instance; the side-check verdict is meaningless at $N_e\le56$, where interpreter overhead dominates.
    * What it establishes: the full-mode ansatz reproduces P1.1's NNBF bit for bit; the analytic gradients match finite differences for every arm type; and the fast low-rank local energy equals $(H\psi)(\mathbf{n})/\psi(\mathbf{n})$ on every configuration to $3\times10^{-13}$, which fixes the in-place Jordan–Wigner convention.
    * An aggregate run on 20-iteration toy results returned FAIL for both routes, which shows the decision rule can fail.

### Local Autopsy Log (Round 2 additions)
* **Route 3 (LGB-BF), killed by the critic at Gates 0 and 2.**
    * Its global channel is a strict subfamily of Liu–Clark's rank-$r$ correction, and finite-range plus Woodbury is SCALE.
    * The test measured only a cost slope that the foil also meets.
    * Mutated into Route 2.1, which contains Liu–Clark exactly and has an accuracy metric.
* **Route 2.3 (low-rank AGP), Gate 0**: it is HFPS (arXiv:2507.10705) restricted to singlet pairing. The two-sided version is also cubic, because of the new–new contraction block.
* **Route 2.4 (active virtual Thouless rotation), Gate 0**: it is a frozen-$\Phi_h$ subfamily of Route 2.1.
* **Route 2.5 (delayed acceptance), Gate 1**: it changes the acceptance cost, not $C_E$, so the F1 bound is untouched; surrogate ratios in $E_{\mathrm{loc}}$ are biased.
* **Route 2.6 (multipole / $\mathcal{H}$-matrix backflow), Gates 2 and 3**: its far field is pooled channels (the B2 objection), and the claim cannot be tested on exact-sum clusters.
### Round 3 — Mutation & Replenishment (2026-11-11)
* **Inputs to this round** (binding):
    * the round-2 critic: Route 2.1 is a relabel; the round-2 test could not fail (rank $r=2$ against $N_\sigma\in\{4,5\}$, loose PASS band, no FAIL on $\rho_t$); no projected control; the cost check was outside the verdict and excluded the setup;
    * orchestrator broadcast 1 (P1.1-P interim): NNBF trained with symmetry projection is essentially exact on the $4\times3$ PBC P1.1 clusters (on $4+4$, median $\Delta E_{\mathrm{tot}}=3\times10^{-5}$, $\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}<10^{-5}$ at width 32), so no route can be scored there; every instance must pass a premise gate (projected control's median $\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}\ge0.01t$);
    * orchestrator broadcast 2 (P1.2 critic): the lever is the ratio of the projected-sector dimension $d_{S\chi}=\dim\operatorname{range}(P_SP_\chi)$ to the control's parameter count $P$, not the group order $\lvert G\rvert$.
* **Notation added in this round.** $P_\chi=\lvert G\rvert^{-1}\sum_{g\in G}\chi(g)U_g$ is the projector onto $\psi_0$'s one-dimensional space-group irrep $\chi$, with $U_g$ the fermionic representation of the lattice symmetry $g$; $P_S$ is the Löwdin projector onto $\psi_0$'s total spin $S$; $d_\chi=\operatorname{tr}P_\chi=\lvert G\rvert^{-1}\sum_g\chi(g)\operatorname{tr}U_g$ and $d_{S\chi}=d_\chi(S^z{=}0)-d_\chi(S^z{=}1)$ for $S=0$. $P$ is the parameter count shared by all arms. $\Delta E_X(R)$ is the median over 3 seeds of arm $R$'s error of type $X$ (P1.1 definitions, upper estimate of $E_{\mathrm{sign}}$), and $\mathrm{Ref}_X$ is the same median for projected NNBF.
* **Instance selection, measured** (`tests/P2.1_subcubic_amplitudes_r3/sector_dims_output.txt`, exact character sums, cross-checked against the projector rank on the smoke cluster). All are $t$–$t'$–$J$ with $t=1$, $t'=-0.25$, $J=0.4$:

| Cluster | $N_\uparrow=N_\downarrow$ | $D$ | $\lvert G\rvert$ | $d_{S\chi}$ | $d_{S\chi}/P$ at $P(W{=}6)$ | Used |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| $4\times3$ PBC | 5 | 16632 | 48 | 75 | 0.07 | no (sector smaller than any network) |
| $4\times4$ PBC | 7 | 411840 | 64 | 870 | 0.4 | no (ground level degenerate at $(\pi,0)$, $(0,\pi)$; sector small) |
| $4\times4$ cylinder | 7 | 411840 | 16 | 3274 | 1.6 | no |
| $4\times4$ OBC | 7 | 411840 | 8 | 6540 | 3.3 | instance 2 |
| $4\times4$ cylinder | 6 | 1681680 | 16 | 15268 | 8.8 | instance 1 |
| $4\times4$ OBC | 6 | 1681680 | 8 | 30220 | 17.4 | instance 0 |

* **How each critic objection is addressed.**
    * [Major] Route 2.1 relabel: accepted. Route 2.1 is dropped and replaced under Stage 3.3 (Route 3.2 below, plus two new kills).
    * [Major] PASS nearly guaranteed: accepted. The restriction now bites by construction and by measurement: $r=1$ against $N_\sigma\in\{6,7\}$ ($r/N_\sigma=0.14$–$0.17$); a bite premise (the single rank-1 term must be at least $1.3\times$ worse in sign than projected NNBF, else FAIL as REDUNDANT); an equal-rank control (one term of rank $Kr=4$, same per-ratio cost) that the sum must beat; tight bands (PASS $\rho_s\le1.10$, $\rho_t\le1.15$; FAIL $\rho_s\ge1.30$, $\rho_t\ge1.40$, both on every counted instance); a filling trend at fixed $r$ ($N_\sigma=6\to7$ on the same OBC lattice). The parameter budget is deliberately narrow ($P\approx1.7$–$2.0\times10^3$, NNBF width 6) so that $d_{S\chi}/P$ is 3–17; this is the round-3 stand-in for "accuracy at fixed $r$ when $N_e\sim100$", where every affordable network is far smaller than its sector.
    * [Major] Stale Gate-3 claim and no projected control: accepted. The retracted competitor citation is removed from the round-2 text. Every compared arm is trained with $P_\chi$ (projected SR), NNBF and SK-LR are also trained with $P_SP_\chi$ (oracle spin), every state is reported after post-hoc $P_S$, and unprojected NNBF and SK-LR are reported before and after post-hoc projection. The contaminated $\min(\texttt{nnbf128},\texttt{mdnnbf})$ reference is replaced by projected NNBF; the unrestricted multi-determinant control is dropped because its four orbital sets alone exceed the budget. This leaf covers the **sign half only**: no instance has a verified same-sector near-degenerate competitor. The exact same-irrep conical intersections found by P3.2 ($4\times3$ PBC Hubbard with anisotropic $t_y$; sector dimensions 1618 and 3752) were considered and not used: at this leaf's budget their $d_{S\chi}/P$ is about 1 to 2, the regime in which P1.1-P found projected NNBF essentially exact, so they would fail the premise gate; the competitor half stays open for this leaf.
    * [Minor] Cost check: accepted. It is now part of the verdict; the setup is timed and reported; the Metropolis sweep with accepted-move Sherman–Morrison updates is timed and gated; the brute slope is fitted up to $L=32$ ($N_e=896$); exactness is checked on the configuration reached after the sweep, i.e. with carried tables.
* **Novelty re-check of SK-LR (WebSearch, mode standard, 2026-11-11).**
    * Huang, Ou & Ozolins, arXiv:2609.26544 (read in HTML): $\Psi(\mathbf{x})=\sum_{ij}c_{ij}(\mathbf{x})\det[M+\Delta M_i+(F_0+\Delta F_j)\odot F(\mathbf{x})]$ with *fixed* low-rank shifts $\Delta M_i$ and a full backflow $F(\mathbf{x})$; one LU per $j$ per configuration, cost $\mathcal{O}(N_{\Delta F}N_e^3)+\mathcal{O}(N_{\Delta M}N_{\Delta F}N_e^2r)$ per amplitude; no local-energy analysis, no carried tables, no symmetry projection. This is the closest prior art. It differs from SK-LR in the decisive place: its configuration dependence is full-rank (so each amplitude is cubic), while SK-LR's configuration dependence is confined to the low-rank factors $C_k(\mathbf{n})$ against a fixed reference, which is what makes each ratio $\mathcal{O}(N_e)$.
    * Clark, Morales, McMinis, Kim & Scuseria, J. Chem. Phys. 135, 244105 (2011): the table method for multi-determinant QMC, quadratic in particle number and linear in the number of excitations, with fixed CI coefficients over fixed orbitals.
    * Liu & Clark, arXiv:2311.09450: HFDS equals NNBF with a rank-$r$ correction; pure low-rank corrections are less expressive than NNBF when $r<N_e$.
    * Loehr & Clark, arXiv:2510.26906: multi-determinant NNBF beats width, at full cost per determinant.
    * Chen & Roth, lrux (arXiv:2602.05255): low-rank update kernels, conditional on a backflow that admits low rank.
    * Searches for "Sherman-Morrison multi-determinant table method configuration-dependent coefficients neural network" and "hidden fermion multiple determinants sum symmetry projected" returned no work that sums configuration-dependent low-rank terms over one carried reference.
* **Generation Batch 3**:
  - **Route 3.1 (Route 2.2 retained, new test): shared-reference low-rank multi-determinant (SK-LR).** The family is unchanged from round 2:
    $$\psi(\mathbf{n})=e^{J(\mathbf{n})}\prod_\sigma\det A_{0\sigma}\sum_{k=1}^{K}w_k\prod_\sigma\det\!\big(I_r+C_{k\sigma}(\mathbf{n})Y_{k\sigma}(\mathbf{n})\big),\qquad Y_{k\sigma}=A_{0\sigma}^{-1}\Phi_{h,k\sigma}[\mathrm{occ}_\sigma].$$
    The $t$–$J$ local energy adds spin exchange, a simultaneous move of one $\uparrow$ and one $\downarrow$ particle; its ratio is the product of the two single-species row updates in the same $r\times r$ form, so $C_E=\mathcal{O}(N_e^2)$ still holds and is now measured with the exchange terms included.
    - Gate 0 (Novelty & Adoption): PASS. Closest prior art is arXiv:2609.26544 (fixed low-rank shifts on a full, cubic-per-amplitude backflow); see the re-check above. The adoption wall SK-LR targets is the $K\times\mathcal{O}(N_e^4)$ local-energy cost of multi-determinant NNBF (Loehr & Clark), together with Liu–Clark's finding that one low-rank term is weaker than NNBF. The mechanism aimed at both: a sum of $K$ low-rank terms over one carried reference (whether the $K$-secant of rank-$r$ terms beats a single term of rank $Kr$ at equal per-ratio cost is measured directly by the equal-rank control).
    - Gate 1 (Conservation & Asymptotics): PASS. Exact, number-conserving, $S^z$-conserving; $C_E=\mathcal{O}(N_e^2)$ and sweep $\mathcal{O}(N_e^3)$ at fixed $(K,r,H_s,H_a)$. Symmetry projection over a group $G$ multiplies the per-amplitude cost by $\lvert G\rvert$ for every arm alike; translations make $\lvert G\rvert\propto V$, which is why production would project only the point group (stated, not hidden).
    - Gate 2 (Baseline Gameability): PASS. The PASS needs, on every counted instance, the projected-NNBF sign quality, a win over the equal-rank single term (median ratio $\le0.85$ and 7 of 9 seed pairs), and a win over Slater$\times$neural Jastrow by a factor $1/0.6$; a premise-gated instance cannot be passed by a near-exact control.
    - Gate 3 (Hard-Core Shield): PASS for the sign half only. The instances are doped, $t'\ne0$, 2D, open or cylindrical, non-stoquastic; $d_{S\chi}\gg P$ so the projected control is not exact by counting.
    - *Verdict*: SURVIVED (decisive test below).
  - **Route 3.2 (Stage 3.3, Operation B.1 on the killed Route 2.1: frame transformation from the particle–hole (Slater) frame to the particle–particle (geminal) frame): shared-pairing-reference low-rank sum (SK-AGP).**
    $$\psi(\mathbf{n})=e^{J(\mathbf{n})}\det B_0(\mathbf{n})\sum_{k=1}^{K}w_k\det\!\big(I_r+C_k(\mathbf{n})[:,\mathrm{occ}_\downarrow]\,B_0^{-1}\Phi_{h,k}[\mathrm{occ}_\uparrow]\big),\qquad B_0=F_0[\mathrm{occ}_\uparrow,\mathrm{occ}_\downarrow].$$
    Here $F_0\in\mathbb{R}^{V\times V}$ is a trainable singlet pairing function (initialised at a $d$-wave BCS state), $\Phi_{h,k}\in\mathbb{R}^{V\times r}$ are fixed-per-term hidden rows on the $\uparrow$ side, and $C_k(\mathbf{n})\in\mathbb{R}^{r\times V}$ is network output with one column per site, selected by the $\downarrow$ occupations. With $r=0$ it is exactly the Foil (projected BCS times a Jastrow, here a neural one). An $\uparrow$ hop replaces a row of $B_0$, a $\downarrow$ hop a column, an exchange both; each ratio is $\rho_0\det(I_r+C'Y')$ with $\rho_0$ read from the carried tables $W=B_0^{-1}F_0[\mathrm{occ}_\uparrow,:]$ and $W'=F_0[:,\mathrm{occ}_\downarrow]B_0^{-1}$, and $Y'$ from a rank-one or rank-two Sherman–Morrison step in $\mathcal{O}(N_er)$ (formulas in `tj_core.eloc_fast_agp`, checked against $(H\psi)(\mathbf{n})/\psi(\mathbf{n})$ on every smoke configuration to $10^{-13}$).
    - Gate 0 (Novelty & Adoption): PASS, with the overlap stated. One SK-AGP term is a one-sided hidden-fermion Pfaffian (HFPS, arXiv:2507.10705) restricted to singlet pairing; Route 2.3 died for being exactly that. What is new is the same mechanism as Route 3.1 (a sum of configuration-dependent low-rank terms over one carried pairing reference) placed on the Foil's own reference. The adoption barrier it targets is HFPS's $\mathcal{O}(N^3)$ per step (hidden block rewritten per configuration) and the Foil's bias; the mechanism is the carried pairing table, which brings $C_E$ to $\mathcal{O}(N_e^2)$, plus the sum for sign freedom. Distinctness from Route 3.1 is in the reference frame (pairing versus Slater); the two routes share the sum mechanism, so a FAIL of that mechanism is evidence against both, while a PASS of only one says which frame the low-rank sum needs.
    - Gate 1 (Conservation & Asymptotics): PASS. Exact, number-conserving, $S^z=0$; $C_E=\mathcal{O}(N_e^2)$ measured in the cost check; the sweep is not implemented for this family and is not claimed.
    - Gate 2 (Baseline Gameability): PASS. Its own Gate-2 control is the Foil itself ($r=0$), and the equal-rank control is one term of rank 4 on the same pairing reference.
    - Gate 3 (Hard-Core Shield): PASS for the sign half only, as Route 3.1.
    - *Verdict*: SURVIVED (shares the decisive test).
  - **Route 3.3 (Operation A on Route 2.1's carried-table component, aimed at the new projection-cost wall): image-table projected SK-LR.** With a reference whose column span is $G$-invariant, $\Phi_0[g\,\mathrm{occ}]=\Phi_0[\mathrm{occ}]R_g$, so one carried inverse serves all $\lvert G\rvert$ images and only $\lvert G\rvert K$ extra table columns are needed.
    - Gate 1 (Conservation & Asymptotics): FAIL as a cost route. The per-image network forward $C_k(g\mathbf{n}')$ costs $\mathcal{O}(H_sKrN_e)$ for every image and connection, identical to carrying separate tables, so the local energy is unchanged; only the sweep and memory improve, and with translations $\lvert G\rvert\propto V$ adds a factor $N_e$ either way. A complex plane-wave reference ($R_g$ monomial) is needed even for the sweep gain.
    - *Verdict*: KILLED.
  - **Route 3.4 (Operation A on round-1 LGB: full-rank local rows plus an unrestricted Liu–Clark block, "SCALE + HFDS").** It contains both SCALE and HFDS, which answers the round-1 subfamily objection.
    - Gate 3 (Hard-Core Shield, for testing): FAIL. On every exact-sum cluster ($4\times4$) the range-1 patch with $t'$ bonds covers nearly every particle, so the local part is global and the restriction cannot be tested; the same reason killed Route 2.6.
    - Gate 0: at risk as a sum of two published components.
    - *Verdict*: KILLED.
### Top Surviving Route & Sharpest Gap (Round 3)
* **Mechanism (Route 3.1, SK-LR; Route 3.2, SK-AGP, shares the test)**: $K$ configuration-dependent low-rank terms over one carried reference (Slater for 3.1, pairing for 3.2); each connection's ratio is an $r\times r$ determinant built in $\mathcal{O}(N_er)$ from the carried tables, so $C_E=\mathcal{O}(N_e^2)$ and (3.1) the sweep is $\mathcal{O}(N_e^3)$.
* **Sharpest Gap**:
    * Whether sign quality at fixed small $r$ survives when the variational family is much smaller than the symmetry sector. Round 3 tests this at $d_{S\chi}/P=3$–$17$ and $r/N_\sigma=0.14$–$0.17$ with $N_\sigma\le7$. The step to $N_e\sim100$ is still an extrapolation, carried only by the fixed-$r$ filling trend ($N_\sigma=6\to7$), which is a two-point trend on one lattice.
    * Whether any instance passes the premise gate. If projected NNBF at width 6 is within $0.01t$ of the exact sign on all three instances, the test is UNINFORMATIVE by its own rule, and the pre-committed action is to halve the width.
    * The oracle-spin arms use an approximate SR metric (that of $P_\chi\psi_\theta$); their energies and forces are exact.
### 24-Hour Decisive Test (Round 3)
* **Minimal Instances**: three $4\times4$ $t$–$t'$–$J$ clusters (table above): instance 0, OBC, $6+6$, $d_{S\chi}=30220$; instance 1, cylinder, $6+6$, $d_{S\chi}=15268$; instance 2, OBC, $7+7$, $d_{S\chi}=6540$. Ground states are non-degenerate singlets in one-dimensional irreps of the point group (with $T_x$ on the cylinder). **Sign half of the Hard Core only.**
* **Arms** (13, all at the parameter count of NNBF width 6, i.e. $P=1734$ on instances 0 and 1; sign trunk width 6, amplitude net takes the rest; 3 seeds 11, 22, 33): `nnbf` (control), `hf1` (bite premise: $K=1$, $r=1$), `hf4` (equal-rank control: $K=1$, $r=4$), `sk1` (Route 3.1: $K=4$, $r=1$), `sk2` (rank trend: $K=4$, $r=2$), `sjnet` (Slater $\times$ neural Jastrow), `agpj` (the Foil: BCS $\times$ neural Jastrow), `agh4` (equal-rank control for 3.2), `skagp` (Route 3.2: $K=4$, $r=1$), all trained with $P_\chi$; `nnbf_PS` and `sk1_PS` trained with $P_SP_\chi$; `nnbf_U` and `sk1_U` unprojected, reported before and after post-hoc projection. Training: exact-force SR, 1200 iterations, step 0.05, shift $10^{-3}$, 2000-sample metric (P1.1 settings).
* **Scalar Metrics** (state $P_\chi\psi_\theta$ unless stated): $\rho_s=\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}(R)/\mathrm{Ref}_{\mathrm{sign}}$; $\rho_t=\Delta E_{\mathrm{tot}}(R)/\mathrm{Ref}_{\mathrm{tot}}$; bite $\beta=\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}(\text{single rank-1 term})/\mathrm{Ref}_{\mathrm{sign}}$; rank ratio $\sigma=\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}(R)/\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}(\text{equal-rank control})$ with pairwise dominance over the $3\times3$ seed pairs; $\gamma=\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}(R)/\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}(\text{Jastrow control})$; spin checks ($\rho_s$ from post-hoc $P_SP_\chi$ budgets; oracle ratio $\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}(\texttt{sk1\_PS})/\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}(\texttt{nnbf\_PS})$); sampled-estimator ratio $v=\mathrm{var}_{\mathrm{ex}}(R)/\mathrm{var}_{\mathrm{ex}}(\texttt{nnbf})$ and $z=\lvert\bar E_{\mathrm{MC}}-E\rvert/\mathrm{stderr}$ from genuine Metropolis chains; filling trend $\rho_s(N_\sigma{=}7)-\rho_s(N_\sigma{=}6)$; cost slopes.
* **Thresholds (pre-committed, all implemented in `--mode aggregate`)**:
    * Premise gate: an instance counts only if $\mathrm{Ref}_{\mathrm{sign}}\ge0.01t$. If none counts, both routes are UNINFORMATIVE; action: halve the width and rerun.
    * PASS (per route) if on every counted instance: guards hold (calibration, multi-start spread, 3 seeds); $\beta\ge1.3$; $\rho_s\le1.10$; $\rho_t\le1.15$; $\sigma\le0.85$ with dominance at least 7 of 9; $\gamma\le0.6$; post-hoc-spin $\rho_s\le1.15$ and (3.1) oracle ratio $\le1.15$; $v\le2$ and $z\le4$; trend $\le0.10$ where evaluable; and the family's cost check PASSes.
    * FAIL if on any counted instance $\beta<1.3$ (REDUNDANT), $\rho_s\ge1.30$, $\rho_t\ge1.40$, $\sigma\ge1$, $\gamma\ge0.85$, oracle ratio $\ge1.30$, trend $\ge0.25$, $v\ge4$ or $z>6$; or the family's cost check FAILs.
    * AMBIGUOUS otherwise; action: 3 more seeds for the route, its equal-rank control and NNBF on the counted instances; if still AMBIGUOUS, FAIL.
    * Cost check (task 117, one thread, $L\times L$ PBC $t$–$J$, $\delta=1/8$, $L=12$–$32$, brute force to $L=32$): PASS if the carried-table local energy after a sweep agrees with brute force to $10^{-8}$, the drift of carried tables is at most $10^{-8}$, the slope of $T_E$ in $N_e$ is at most 2.4 and (3.1) the sweep slope at most 3.3, with brute slope at least 3.3 as the regime guard; FAIL if errors exceed $10^{-6}$, the $T_E$ slope is at least 2.8, or the sweep slope at least 3.8. The setup slope is reported, not gated.
* **Execution Plan**: `tests/P2.1_subcubic_amplitudes_r3/micro_test.py` (driver), `tj_core.py` ($t$–$J$ model, group, spin, ansatz, fast local energies), `p11_core.py` (verbatim P1.1 copy: ED, UHF, SR conventions, `budget()`), `sector_dims.py`, `probe_instances.py`, `submit_test.sbatch` (array 0–117, partition `kim`).
    * Measured cost (one SR step, instance 0, $D=1681680$, 8 threads; `timing_output.txt`): from 5.4 s (`agpj`) and 14.3 s (`nnbf`) to 33.9 s (`sk1`) and 42.3 s (`sk2`). With 1200 SR iterations (reduced from P1.1's 1500, for every arm alike, to fit 24 h) the worst task is about 14 h of SR at 8 threads before the budgets; tasks get 16 CPUs and 32 GB.
    * Command, not run: `cd tests/P2.1_subcubic_amplitudes_r3 && sbatch submit_test.sbatch`. Afterwards: `python3 micro_test.py --mode aggregate`.
* **Smoke-test evidence** (on the compute node hosting this session, 8 s). The smoke cluster's projected sector has dimension 5, so every arm is exact there and the smoke verdict ("REDUNDANT", premise gate not met) only shows that the decision logic runs end to end and can fail.
    * What it establishes: $[H,\hat S^2]$, $[H,U_g]$, $[\hat S^+,U_g]$, and the projector identities hold to $10^{-15}$; the character-sum sector dimensions equal the projector ranks; analytic gradients match finite differences for every family under no projection, $P_\chi$ and $P_SP_\chi$; projected log-derivatives match finite differences; per-amplitude projected amplitudes equal the projected vector; both fast local energies equal $(H\psi)(\mathbf{n})/\psi(\mathbf{n})$ on every configuration; carried tables after a 200-proposal Metropolis sweep on $6\times6$ agree with a fresh setup to $10^{-14}$.

* **Production-path dry run** (instance 2, arm `sk1`, 3 SR steps, short budget; `tests/P2.1_subcubic_amplitudes_r3/dryrun_inst2_output.txt`): the full task (ED, irrep resolution, $d_{S\chi}=6540$, calibration $5\times10^{-11}$, projected SR at 9 s per step on 8 threads, both budgets, the Metropolis arm with per-amplitude check $3\times10^{-16}$) runs end to end and writes its JSON. The untrained state's local-energy kurtosis is $6\times10^{4}$, a reminder that the sampled arm can bite.

### Local Autopsy Log (Round 3 additions)
* **Route 2.1 (split-capacity HFDS), killed by the round-2 critic at Gate 0**: HFDS times a Jastrow (Liu–Clark; HFPS) with a Woodbury ratio (lrux). Replaced by Route 3.2 (Operation B.1); its carried-table component was mutated into Route 3.3, which died at Gate 1.
* **Round-2 test design, killed by the critic and by P1.1-P**: on the P1.1 $4\times3$ PBC clusters the projected sector ($d_{S\chi}\le3752$) is no larger than the networks, so projected NNBF is essentially exact and no route can be scored. Boundary condition for every later test: choose $d_{S\chi}/P\gg1$ and gate on the projected control's residual error.
* **Route 3.3 (image-table projected SK-LR), Gate 1**: per-image network forwards dominate the projected local energy, so sharing one inverse across images only helps the sweep.
* **Route 3.4 (local full-rank rows plus Liu–Clark block), Gate 3 for testing**: on $4\times4$ clusters the local part is global.
* **Instance kills**: $4\times4$ PBC $7+7$ has a degenerate ground level at $(\pi,0)$ and $(0,\pi)$ (resolvable, but its sector, 870, is smaller than the network); $4\times3$ PBC $5+5$ has a 75-dimensional sector.
### Round 5 (2026-11-12)
* **Inputs to this round** (binding):
    * the round-3 per-leaf critic (master doc, 12 objections);
    * the control pilot (jobs 410988 and 451706; projected NNBF width 6, $P=1734$ on the $6+6$ clusters), medians over seeds 11, 22, 33:
        * instance 0 ($4\times4$ OBC $6+6$, $d_{S\chi}=30220$): $\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}=0.0125t$ (range 0.0125–0.0136), $f_E=0.22$–$0.24$, overlap with $\psi_0$ about 0.94. **Qualifies.**
        * instance 1 ($4\times4$ cylinder $6+6$, $d_{S\chi}=15268$): $0.0038t$ (range 0.0035–0.0046). Does not qualify.
        * instance 2 ($4\times4$ OBC $7+7$, $d_{S\chi}=6540$), seed 11 only: $0.0028t$. Does not qualify.
    * the pre-committed rule: with one qualifying instance the verdict is UNINFORMATIVE (critic item 5), unless a second qualifying instance is found. The round-3 action "halve the width" is withdrawn and replaced by a mutation trigger (below).
* **Fixes of the round-3 critic.** Code: `tests/P2.1_subcubic_amplitudes_r5/micro_test.py` (a copy of the round-3 driver with the fixes; `tj_core.py` and `p11_core.py` are unchanged copies).
    * **Gate 0, SK-LR, restated.** SK-LR is HFDS with $K$ blocks and a frozen hidden block, linearised at $r=1$. Equivalently, it is a neural-CI singles expansion over one reference, evaluated with the table method of Clark et al., and its per-ratio cost is the lrux kernel. No new ansatz is claimed. What remains is a measured claim about the adoption barrier of Liu–Clark ("low-rank corrections are weaker than NNBF at matched size"): at a fixed, narrow budget, does a cheap sign trunk plus a positive amplitude net keep projected-NNBF sign quality at $\mathcal{O}(N_e)$ cost per connection?
    * **Gate 0, SK-AGP, restated.** SK-AGP does not fix the HFPS $\mathcal{O}(N^3)$-per-step barrier, because no sweep with carried pairing tables is implemented. Its claim is narrowed to the local energy: $C_E=\mathcal{O}(N_e^2)$, against HFPS's $\mathcal{O}(N^2\tilde N)$ per ratio. The per-step claim is withdrawn.
    * **Item 3, the hypothesis restated.** "The restriction bites" was false. $\det(A_0+\phi c^{\mathsf T})=\det A_0\,(1+c^{\mathsf T}A_0^{-1}\phi)$, so a rank-1 term with an unrestricted $c(\mathbf{n})$ already represents an arbitrary scalar function times the reference, and rank alone is not binding. The hypothesis is now about how a fixed parameter count $P$ is split: a sign trunk of width $H_s=6$ feeding $K=4$ rank-1 terms, with a positive amplitude net taking the rest of $P$, against NNBF (all of $P$ in the backflow) and NNBF$\times$Jastrow. The arm `hf1` (one rank-1 term, same split) stays as a *capacity* premise: if it already matches the reference, the $K$-term sum is unnecessary (REDUNDANT). The rank ratio $\sigma$ is not evidence about rank either, so it is reported only (item 7).
    * **Item 6, control handicap.** New arm `nnbfj`: NNBF of width 4 times a tanh Jastrow whose width fills the same $P$. For each metric $X\in\{\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}},\Delta E_{\mathrm{tot}}\}$, $\mathrm{Ref}_X$ is the better (smaller median) of `nnbf` and `nnbfj`.
    * **Item 7, seeds.** Seeds are paired by index across arms. $\rho_s$ and $\rho_t$ are medians of per-seed ratios against the Ref arm. $\sigma$ (sk1 against the single rank-4 term `hf4`) and its paired dominance are reported only.
    * **Item 8, cost gate.** The verdict now uses the time per connection $t_c=T_E/n_{\mathrm{conn}}$ measured at large $N_e$ (sizes $L=16$–$48$, $N_e$ up to 2016) and the measured speedup $t_{cb}/t_c$ over brute force per connection at $L=32$ ($N_e=896$). PASS needs: errors $\le10^{-8}$; slope of $t_c$ over the three largest sizes in $[0.5,1.3]$ (a slope below 0.5 means overhead still dominates, which is AMBIGUOUS); for SK-LR, slope of the per-proposal sweep time $\le2.3$; speedup $\ge20$. FAIL: errors $>10^{-6}$, slope $\ge1.8$, sweep slope $\ge2.8$, or speedup $<5$. Total-time slopes are reported, not gated.
    * **Item 9.** Each sampled seed of the route, the premise arm and both controls must have `per_amp_check` $\le10^{-10}$, otherwise the instance is AMBIGUOUS.
    * **Item 5.** The verdict needs at least 2 counted instances. With one, both routes are UNINFORMATIVE.
    * **Stale docstring.** It has been rewritten. It now gives the round-5 arms, task decoding (84 tasks plus the cost task), defaults ($H_s=6$, width 6, 1200 SR iterations) and the decision rule. The round-3 text cited `mdnnbf`, task 0..116, $H_s=64$, width 128 and 1500 iterations.
* **Second qualifying instance: screen** (`tests/P2.1_subcubic_amplitudes_r5/screen_candidates.py`, `screen_output.txt`; exact character sums, Lanczos, 16 threads, about 1 min per candidate). All are $t$–$t'$–$J$ with $t=1$. $P$ is the parameter count of NNBF width 6 on that cluster, and "gap" is $E_1-E_0$ over all sectors:

* **Selection rule, read off the round-3 controls.** $d_{S\chi}/P$ alone does not predict qualification: the cylinder (ratio 8.8) failed and instance 0 (ratio 17.4) passed. The three measured instances are also separated by the lowest gap: 0.049 for the one that qualified, 0.120 and 0.135 for the two that did not. The two candidates were chosen on that two-variable reading, with a different lattice or filling from instance 0 so that the second instance is not a parameter variant of the first:
    * instance 3 ($4\times4$ OBC $5+5$, 6 holes) has the largest ratio and the smallest gap of all candidates;
    * instance 4 ($5\times3$ OBC $6+6$) is a different lattice with a small gap but a ratio near the cylinder's, so it also tests whether the gap alone is enough.
    * Three points are not a law; the pilot is the test.
* **Pilot** (`tests/P2.1_subcubic_amplitudes_r5/submit_pilot.sbatch`, **job 457482**, array 0–1, partition `kim`, 16 CPUs, 16 h): projected NNBF width 6, seed 11, with the round-3 settings, on instances 3 and 4. Production dry run (2 SR steps each, `dryrun_output.txt`): both run end to end, at 11.4 s per SR step for instance 3 and 3.7 s for instance 4, with `per_amp_check` $\le3\times10^{-16}$. **Pre-commit**: a candidate qualifies if its $\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}\ge0.01t$. Its seeds 22 and 33 are then part of the decisive array, and the aggregate re-checks the median. If both qualify, instance 3 is used (larger ratio, more independent filling). If neither qualifies, the $J=0.3$ variant is piloted next. If that also fails, P2.1 is NO-TARGET at ED size and goes to mutation, which is the trigger that replaces width halving.
### Top Surviving Route & Sharpest Gap (Round 5)
* **Mechanism**: unchanged from round 3 (Route 3.1 SK-LR; Route 3.2 SK-AGP, narrowed to $C_E$). The claim is restated as a parameter-split claim at fixed $P$, not a rank claim.
* **Sharpest Gap**:
    * The test has no second instance yet. Its verdict is UNINFORMATIVE until the pilot qualifies one.
    * With rank not binding, even a PASS shows only that a cheap sign trunk plus a Jastrow keeps projected-NNBF sign quality at this budget. It does not show that low-rank response is harmless where $c(\mathbf{n})$ would need capacity that grows with $N_e$.
    * The `nnbfj` split (backflow width 4) is one point. A Ref that happens to be weak in this split would favour the route. This is mitigated by taking the better of `nnbf` and `nnbfj`.
### 24-Hour Decisive Test (Round 5)
* **Minimal Instances**: instance 0 and the instance the pilot qualifies (3 or 4, passed as `NEWI`). Both are doped, $t'\ne0$, 2D, open-boundary, non-stoquastic singlets in one-dimensional irreps. **Sign half of the Hard Core only.**
* **Arms** (14, all at $P$ of NNBF width 6, seeds 11, 22, 33, paired by index): `nnbf` and `nnbfj` (controls, Ref = the better per metric), `hf1` (capacity premise), `hf4` and `sk2` (reported only), `sk1` (Route 3.1), `sjnet` (Gate-2 control), `agpj` (the Foil), `agh4` (reported only), `skagp` (Route 3.2), `nnbf_PS` and `sk1_PS` (oracle spin), `nnbf_U` and `sk1_U` (unprojected). Training is as in round 3 (exact-force projected SR, 1200 iterations).
* **Scalar Metrics**: paired $\rho_s$ and $\rho_t$ against the Ref arm; $\beta=\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}(\text{premise})/\mathrm{Ref}_{\mathrm{sign}}$; $\gamma$ against the Gate-2 control; spin checks; $v$ against the Ref arm's exact local-energy variance and $z$; the cost check per connection.
* **Thresholds (pre-committed, in `--mode aggregate`)**:
    * Premise gate: an instance counts if median $\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}(\texttt{nnbf})\ge0.01t$ and $\mathrm{Ref}_{\mathrm{sign}}\ge0.005t$. With fewer than 2 counted instances, both routes are UNINFORMATIVE.
    * REDUNDANT, which counts as FAIL, if $\beta<1.3$ on any counted instance.
    * PASS if on every counted instance: guards hold (3 seeds, calibration, spread, `per_amp_check` $\le10^{-10}$); $\rho_s\le1.10$; $\rho_t\le1.15$; $\gamma\le0.6$; post-hoc-spin $\rho_s\le1.15$ and (3.1) oracle ratio $\le1.15$; $v\le2$; $z\le4$. The family's cost check must also PASS.
    * FAIL if on any counted instance $\rho_s\ge1.30$, $\rho_t\ge1.40$, $\gamma\ge0.85$, oracle ratio $\ge1.30$, $v\ge4$ or $z>6$, or if the cost check FAILs.
    * AMBIGUOUS otherwise. Action: 3 more seeds (44, 55, 66) for the route, the premise arm and both controls. If still AMBIGUOUS, FAIL.
* **Execution Plan**: `tests/P2.1_subcubic_amplitudes_r5/submit_test.sbatch`, array 0–84, partition `kim`, 16 CPUs, 32 GB, 24 h.
    * Tasks 0–83 are (instance, arm, seed); task 84 is the cost check.
    * Instance 0 `nnbf` seeds 11/22/33 (copied from round 3) and the pilot's `nnbf` seed 11 are skipped because their JSONs exist.
    * The worst task (`sk2`) is about 14 h of SR at 8 threads.
    * Command, not run, and only after the pilot qualifies an instance: `cd tests/P2.1_subcubic_amplitudes_r5 && sbatch --export=ALL,NEWI=<3|4> submit_test.sbatch`.
    * Afterwards: `python3 micro_test.py --mode aggregate --new-instance <3|4>`.
* **Smoke and preview evidence** (on the compute node hosting this session):
    * `--mode smoke` (7.5 s): all correctness checks pass. The aggregate runs end to end. The smoke cost check is AMBIGUOUS at $N_e\le88$, because there the per-connection slope is negative (overhead), which is what the new guard is for.
    * `--mode aggregate` on the real results (instance 0 only) returns UNINFORMATIVE (1 counted instance; 2 needed).
    * Cost preview at the decisive sizes (`cost_preview_output.txt`, one configuration per size, one thread), both families PASS:
        * SK-LR: $t_c$ slope 1.15 (from $3.3\times10^{-5}$ s at $N_e=504$ to $1.2\times10^{-4}$ s at $N_e=2016$), sweep slope 2.19, speedup 593 at $N_e=896$, errors $\le10^{-13}$, drift $\le1.2\times10^{-10}$;
        * SK-AGP: $t_c$ slope 1.28, speedup 244.

### Local Autopsy Log (Round 5 additions)
* **"The restriction bites" (round 3), retracted.** A rank-1 term with unrestricted $c(\mathbf{n})$ represents any scalar times the reference. Rank is not binding, so $\sigma$ is reported only and the hypothesis is restated as a parameter split.
* **"Halve the width" (round-3 UNINFORMATIVE action), withdrawn.** It is replaced by the instance search above and, failing that, a mutation trigger.
* **SK-AGP's claim against HFPS's per-step cost, withdrawn.** No sweep is implemented; only the local-energy claim stands.
* **Instances 1 and 2, dropped from the decisive array.** The projected control's sign error is below the bar: 0.0038t and 0.0028t.
### Round 6 — Evidence Tiers & Route Portfolio (2026-11-16)
* **Inputs to this round** (binding):
    * the skill's Stage 3.5 (evidence tiers T0–T3, route portfolio, submit-and-switch) and Gate 4 (scaling and relevance), applied retroactively to the in-flight routes;
    * job 526080 (decisive array, instances 0 and 3): 84 of 85 tasks finished at 09:20 on 2026-11-16; task 82 (instance 3, `sk1_U`, seed 22, an arm that is reported only) is still running;
    * cross-leaf: P1.3 closed CAPACITY (the residual sign error is width-limited, no basins); P4.2 found that size-independent families saturate; P4.1 found that most of its 12-site accuracy came from space-group projection.
* **Notation added in this round.** $F$ is the number of local feature channels per site; $h_m(\mathbf{n})=\tanh(W\,x_{\mathrm{patch}(m)}+b)\in\mathbb{R}^F$ is the local feature vector of site $m$, computed from the $3\times3$ patch around $m$ (both spins); $B$ is the set of sites whose patch contains a touched site of a move, $b=\lvert B\rvert\le18$; $M$ is the number of factors (Route 6.2) or kernel columns (Route 6.1), $M=V$ here.
* **Pre-registered T0/T1 kill rules (written before any round-6 computation).**
    * Gate 4 T1 for the in-flight SK-LR (`tests/P2.1_subcubic_amplitudes_r6/t1_routes.py --mode gate4`, sizes $L=16,24,32$, one thread group of at most 4): the T0 prediction is confirmed if the slope of the time per connection $t_c$ in $N_e$ is at most 1.3 at fixed $H_s=64$ and at least 1.6 at $H_s=N_e/4$. This is a check of the operation count, not a route kill.
    * Routes 6.1 and 6.2, T1 (`--mode gkbf` and `--mode plr`, exactness at $L=4,6,8$, timing at $L=16,24,32$, i.e. $N_e=224,504,896$): KILL if the carried-table local energy differs from the brute-force one (sorted rows, explicit Jordan–Wigner signs) by more than $10^{-6}$ relative, or the slope of $t_c$ is at least 1.8, or the brute/fast speedup per connection at $L=32$ is below 2. PASS if the error is at most $10^{-9}$, the slope is in $[0.5,1.3]$ and the speedup is at least 10. AMBIGUOUS otherwise; action: add $L=40$; if still AMBIGUOUS, KILL.
    * Route 6.1′ (LH-HFDS), T1, written after the Route 6.1 T0 kill and before its run (`--mode lhhfds`, exactness at $L=4,6,8$, timing at $L=24,32,40$, unbatched per-connection path): KILL if the relative local-energy error exceeds $10^{-6}$, the slope of $t_c$ is at least 1.8, or the speedup at $L=40$ is below 2. PASS if the error is at most $10^{-9}$ and the slope is at most 1.3. No lower slope guard: the round-6 runs of Routes 6.1 and 6.2 showed that this guard turns interpreter overhead into a kill (Tier log).
    * Route 6.4 (Tree-HFDS), T1, written before its run (`--mode tree`, exactness at $L=4,6,8$, timing at $L=16,24,32$): the same KILL and PASS clauses as Route 6.1′.

Without projection, `sk1_U` against `nnbf_U` has $\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}$ ratio 1.07 and $\Delta E_{\mathrm{tot}}$ ratio 1.36 on instance 0, and 0.90 and 0.94 on instance 3 (two seeds).
