#### Retroactive Gate 4 for the in-flight routes (3.1 SK-LR and 3.2 SK-AGP)
* **Cost claim, T0 (operation count).** Per connection, SK-LR evaluates the $K$ terms $\det(I_r+C_k'Y_k')$: the table update and contraction cost $\mathcal{O}(KrN_e+Kr^2N_e)$, the sign-trunk output layer costs $\mathcal{O}(H_sKrN_e)$ (its output has $Kr(N_\uparrow+N_\downarrow)$ entries), and the amplitude net costs $\mathcal{O}(H_a)$. Hence $C_{\mathrm{amp}}=\mathcal{O}(N_e(H_sKr+Kr^2)+H_a)$ and $C_E=\mathcal{O}(zN_e^2H_sKr)$. This is $\mathcal{O}(N_e^2)$ only at fixed $(H_s,K,r)$; with $H_s\propto N_e^a$ it is $\mathcal{O}(N_e^{2+a})$. SK-AGP has the same form with $V$ in place of $N_e$ in the output layer. Verdict: the measured round-5 exponent matches the count at fixed width.
* **Cost claim, T1 (timing at three sizes, `t1_routes.py --mode gate4`, `t1_output_run1.txt`, `t1_output_run2.txt`).** Pre-registered prediction: slope of $t_c$ at most 1.3 at $H_s=64$ and at least 1.6 at $H_s=N_e/4$.
    * $L=16,24,32$: slopes 0.54 (fixed) and 0.84 (growing).
    * $L=32,48,64$ ($N_e$ up to 3584, $H_s$ up to 896): slopes 0.80 and 1.30, i.e. $C_E$ exponents 1.80 and 2.30.
    * Verdict: **prediction NOT CONFIRMED** at these sizes. The growing-width slope exceeds the fixed-width one by 0.30, then 0.50, in the predicted direction, but per-connection overhead still hides the asymptotic $H_sKrN_e$ term. The cost verdict rests on the T0 count, not on this timing.
* **Accuracy claim, T0 (extensivity lemma).** Take $M$ decoupled blocks with block-diagonal reference $A_0$ and hidden rows supported per block, so the exact state is $\prod_b\det A_{0b}\,(1+g_b)$ with independent sign-capable $g_b$. With $r=1$, each SK-LR term is $\prod_\sigma(1+c_{k\sigma}(\mathbf{n})^{\mathsf T}y_{k\sigma})$ and $c^{\mathsf T}y=\sum_bc_b^{\mathsf T}y_b$.
    * If the trunk is block-separable ($c_b$ depends on block $b$ only), every term is a multilinear polynomial of degree at most 2 in the block functions, and so is any sum of $K$ terms. The product $\prod_{b=1}^M(1+g_b)$ has a degree-$M$ component, so for $M\ge3$ it is not representable at **any** $K$.
    * With a shared trunk, $c_b$ must equal $\hat c_b(\mathbf{n}_b)\prod_{b'\ne b}(1+g_{b'})$, an $(M-1)$-fold product. A one-hidden-layer network with a smooth nonlinearity needs $2^{M-1}$ hidden units to multiply $M-1$ inputs exactly (Lin, Tegmark & Rolnick, J. Stat. Phys. 168, 1223, 2017). The positive factor $e^J$ cannot supply signs.
    * NNBF is not size-consistent at fixed width either, but its determinant multiplies block factors by itself, so block-local hidden units (width $\propto M$) suffice.
    * Consequence: at matched fixed widths, SK-LR's sign quality relative to NNBF is predicted to degrade as $N$ grows. Keeping it requires $H_s$ (or a deeper trunk, whose forward is no longer incremental) to grow with $N_e$, and then $C_E$ reaches at least the Foil's exponent $N_e^3$. The advantage over NNBF remains a factor of $N_e$ in $C_E$, but the leaf's objective, $C_E=o(N_e^3)$ with no accuracy loss at scale, is not met.
* **Gate 4 verdict for Routes 3.1 and 3.2: FAIL (T0, retroactive).** The fixed-$P$ ED-size test measures a finite-size regime ($N_e\le12$, $P\approx1.5$–$1.7\times10^3$, point-group projection doing most of the work). Its outcome is recorded as a finite-size result and cannot promote the routes. Boundary condition for every new route: **sign capacity must be extensive at fixed per-site width**, i.e. size-consistent by construction.
* **Decision tree for 526080 (pre-planned, executes on landing).**
    * AMBIGUOUS (expected for both): the pre-registered action (seeds 44, 55, 66) is **not recommended**, since even a resolved PASS is a finite-size bonus under Gate 4. The routes become KILLED(T0, Gate 4), with the 526080 numbers above as the record.
    * If the orchestrator overrides: the pre-registered arm list has a defect. Route 3.1 is ambiguous through the oracle ratio, and the listed arms (`sk1`, `hf1`, `nnbf`, `nnbfj`) do not include `sk1_PS` and `nnbf_PS`, so that rerun would end in FAIL by construction. The override array must add those two arms; with `skagp` and `agpj` for Route 3.2 that is 8 arms, 2 instances and 3 seeds, i.e. 48 tasks of at most about 14 h each at 16 CPUs.
    * PASS (either route): recorded as "a width-6 sign trunk with $K=4$ rank-1 terms keeps projected-NNBF sign quality at ED size"; KILLED(T0, Gate 4) for the sub-cubic claim.
    * FAIL: KILLED(T3), consistent with Gate 4.
#### Generation Batch 6 (Stage 3.3 refill, gated against the round-6 boundary condition)
* **Route 6.1 (Operation A on Routes 2.6 and 3.4, whose far field was a pooled rank-$p$ channel and whose test could not separate local from global): global-kernel linear-readout backflow (GK-BF).** $a_j=\Phi_{0\sigma}[x_j]+(K_\sigma[x_j,:]H(\mathbf{n}))\,\Omega_\sigma$, with local features $H\in\mathbb{R}^{V\times F}$, a trainable kernel $K_\sigma\in\mathbb{R}^{V\times V}$ and a shared readout $\Omega_\sigma\in\mathbb{R}^{F\times N_\sigma}$. A hop changes $H$ on $B$ only, so each spin's matrix changes by rank at most $1+b$. The ratio is a $(1+b)$-dimensional determinant from carried $A^{-1}$, $T=A^{-1}K[\mathrm{occ},:]$, $R=\Omega A^{-1}$, $Q=\Omega T$ and $KH$, at $C_{\mathrm{amp}}=\mathcal{O}(N_eb+Fb^2)$.
    - Gates 0–3: PASS (no prior art found in the quick searches listed under Route 6.1′; exact; contains Slater and one-layer SCALE; same instances as round 5).
    - Gate 4, T0: **FAIL**, found after its T1 run 1 (logged below). By the determinant lemma, $\det(A_0+G\Omega)=\det A_0\det(I_F+\Omega A_0^{-1}G)$ with $G=K[\mathrm{occ},:]H$, so the whole family is a single $F\times F$ hidden-fermion factor, i.e. HFDS with $F$ hidden fermions. Two decoupled blocks share the same $F$-dimensional readout space, so the factor does not multiply, and $F$ must grow with $M$. The extensivity boundary condition fails.
    - *Verdict*: KILLED(T0, Gate 4). Mutated into Route 6.1′.
* **Route 6.1′ (Operation A on Route 6.1): local-hidden-row HFDS (LH-HFDS).** One hidden fermion per site $m$, with a patch-local hidden row $C_m(\mathbf{n})=h_m(\mathbf{n})\,\Omega_m$ ($\Omega_m\in\mathbb{R}^{F\times N_\sigma}$, site-dependent) and hidden orbitals $K_\sigma\in\mathbb{R}^{V\times V}$:
$$A_\sigma(\mathbf{n})=\Phi_{0\sigma}[\mathrm{occ}_\sigma]+K_\sigma[\mathrm{occ}_\sigma,:]\,C_\sigma(\mathbf{n}),\qquad\psi=e^{J}\prod_\sigma\det A_\sigma.$$
    - The correction has rank up to $V\ge N_\sigma$, so there is no rank cap. A hop changes only the $b$ hidden rows in $B$, so $\Delta A_\sigma$ has rank at most $1+b$. The ratio is a $(1+b)$-dimensional determinant from carried $A^{-1}$, $T=A^{-1}K[\mathrm{occ},:]$, $R_m=\Omega_mA^{-1}$ and $Q_m=\Omega_mT$. The row-change term needs $\sum_m\kappa_mh'_m\cdot Q_m[:,B]$ with $\kappa=K[y,:]-K[x,:]$, so $C_{\mathrm{amp}}=\mathcal{O}(VFb)$ and $C_E=\mathcal{O}(zN_e^2Fb)$. Memory is $\mathcal{O}(N_e^2F)$, setup $\mathcal{O}(N_e^3F)$ once per chain, an accepted move $\mathcal{O}(N_e^2Fb)$ and a sweep $\mathcal{O}(N_e^3Fb)$ (analytic; the sweep is not implemented).
    - Gate 0 (Novelty & Adoption): PASS. HFDS (Robledo Moreno et al.; Liu & Clark, arXiv:2311.09450) takes the hidden rows from a global network, so a hop changes all of them and each amplitude is cubic. SCALE (arXiv:2604.25775) is local only. Route 3.4 (killed) was SCALE plus a global Liu–Clark block. Here the hidden-fermion count is extensive and each hidden row is local, which gives a rank-$b$ hop response with global coupling through $K$. Quick searches (2026-11-16: "neural backflow convolutional local features global kernel low-rank update", "product of determinant ratios rank-one local factors hidden fermion", "hierarchical hidden fermion determinant tree fast update") found no such construction. The adoption barrier it targets is P1 (local backflow trails global backflow by about $3$–$4\times10^{-3}$ per site), through global linear coupling at quadratic $C_E$.
    - Gate 1 (Conservation & Asymptotics): PASS. Exact (determinant lemma), number- and $S^z$-conserving, no hardness claim.
    - Gate 2 (Baseline Gameability): PASS. It contains Slater ($\Omega=0$) and one-layer SCALE ($K=I$), so it must beat Slater $\times$ neural Jastrow and match projected NNBF at matched $P$ under the round-5 premise gate.
    - Gate 3 (Hard-Core Shield): PASS for the sign half (round-5 instances). On $4\times4$ the patch covers 9 of 16 sites, but the restriction relative to NNBF (nonlinearity only within patches, global coupling linear) does not depend on $b$, so it is testable at ED size; the cost advantage is tested separately at large $N_e$.
    - Gate 4 (Scaling & Relevance), T0: PASS. For decoupled blocks with block-local $K$ and $\Omega_m$, $A$ is block-diagonal and $\psi$ factorizes, so capacity is extensive ($VF$ local features, $V$ hidden fermions) at fixed $F$ while $C_E=\mathcal{O}(N_e^2)$.
    - *Verdict*: SURVIVED to T1.
* **Route 6.2 (Operation B.3, cross-field: the size-extensivity of coupled cluster over configuration interaction, applied to the sum-of-terms structure of Routes 3.1 and 3.2): product of local rank-1 determinant-ratio factors (PLR).**
$$\psi=e^{J}\prod_\sigma\det A_{0\sigma}\prod_{m=1}^{V}\prod_\sigma\big(1+h_m(\mathbf{n})\cdot Z_{\sigma}[:,m]\big),\qquad Z_\sigma=\Omega_\sigma A_{0\sigma}^{-1}\Phi_{h\sigma}[\mathrm{occ}_\sigma].$$
    - Each factor equals $\det(A_0+\phi_m[\mathrm{occ}]\,c_m^{\mathsf T})/\det A_0$ with $c_m=\Omega^{\mathsf T}h_m$: a sign-capable, reference-aware local factor. The product replaces SK-LR's sum, which the Gate 4 lemma showed is not size-consistent. A hop updates $Z$ by a rank-1 term and $h$ on $B$, so all $V$ factors are refreshed in $\mathcal{O}(VF)$ and $C_E=\mathcal{O}(zN_e^2F)$.
    - Gate 0: PASS. A Liu–Clark determinant Jastrow is a single $r\times r$ factor, and CPS times Slater (killed in P1.2) has factors that depend on local occupations only. PLR's factors are local in input but depend on the global reference response through $A_0^{-1}$, and there are $V$ of them. Gates 1–3: PASS, as for Route 6.1′.
    - Gate 4, T0: PASS. With block-diagonal $A_0$ and block-supported $\phi_m$, each factor depends on its own block, so $\psi$ factorizes exactly.
    - *Verdict*: SURVIVED to T1.
* **Route 6.3 (Operation A on Route 3.3, image-table projection, killed at Gate 1 by per-image network forwards): translation-equivariant trunk with shared image tables.** For an equivariant trunk, the image forwards $C(g\mathbf{n})$ are permutations of one forward, so projection costs no extra network evaluations.
    - Gate 4, T0: **FAIL**. The carried tables are still per image, so $C_E=\mathcal{O}(\lvert G\rvert N_e^2)$, which is $\mathcal{O}(N_e^3)$ with translations ($\lvert G\rvert\propto V$). With the point group only, $C_E$ stays quadratic, but the gain is a single global quantum number worth $\mathcal{O}(1/V)$ per site: a finite-size bonus, the case Gate 4 names. A fully equivariant ansatz with an invariant reference is already symmetric, and projecting it does nothing.
    - *Verdict*: KILLED(T0, Gate 4).
* **Route 6.4 (Operation A on Route 6.1′'s sharpest gap, nonlinearity only within patches; also a repair of Route 2.6's pooled far field): hierarchical local-hidden-row HFDS (Tree-HFDS).** Route 6.1′ plus one hidden fermion per block of a $2^\ell\times2^\ell$ block hierarchy, with nonlinear block features $g_\beta=\tanh(W_\ell\,\overline{g}_{\mathrm{children}(\beta)}+b_\ell)$ and their own $\Omega_\beta$ and $K[:,\beta]$.
    - A hop changes $B$ plus the ancestors of $B$, i.e. $b'=b+\mathcal{O}(\log V)$ hidden rows, so $C_E=\mathcal{O}(zN_e^2F(b+\log V))$. Coarse rows are nonlinear in whole-block content, which P1.2 and P4.2 indicate the residual correction needs (power-law tails), and which Route 2.6 lacked (its far field was linear pooled channels, the B2 objection).
    - Gates 0–3: PASS, as for Route 6.1′; it contains Route 6.1′ (coarse $\Omega_\beta=0$).
    - Gate 4, T0: PASS. It contains the size-consistent Route 6.1′, so blocks not aligned with the tree can switch their coarse couplings off; the hidden-fermion count is about $4V/3$.
    - *Verdict*: SURVIVED to T1.

In a T1 rule whose only failure mode is super-linear growth, "AMBIGUOUS twice, then KILL" turns a flat, overhead-bound curve into a kill. No measurement here points toward the kill region (slope at least 1.8): per-connection time is flat while brute-force time grows tenfold. A report-only run at $F=64$ (`t1_output_run3_F64_reportonly.txt`, $L$ up to 48) gave slopes 0.32 (PLR) and 0.58 (GK-BF), with speedups 1862 and 198.

### Round 6 per-leaf critic (2026-11-16)
Scope: the round-6 section, `tests/P2.1_subcubic_amplitudes_r6/t1_routes.py` (read in full for the PLR, LH-HFDS and Tree-HFDS paths and the verdict logic), its four output files, and one new critic check, `tests/P2.1_subcubic_amplitudes_r6/critic_plr_poles.py` with output `critic_plr_poles_output.txt` (single thread, under 10 s). Job 526080 was not touched.
* **Persistence (mandate item 1)**: satisfied. Round 6 mutated every kill (6.1 into 6.1′, 2.6 and 6.1′ into 6.4, the SK sum into the PLR product) and gated the replacements.
* **Critic check output** (`critic_plr_poles_output.txt`):

```
  eps=1e-04  log10|psi_PLR,up|=   +26.64  log10|psi_LH,up|=  -5.39
  eps=1e-05  log10|psi_PLR,up|=   +62.22  log10|psi_LH,up|=  -5.39
  eps=1e-06  log10|psi_PLR,up|=   +96.85  log10|psi_LH,up|=  -5.39
check1 asymptotic slope (eps=1e-4..1e-6): PLR=-35.11, predicted 1-M=-35
check2 L=6 (V=36, 800 spin blocks): ... log10|prod f| median -0.02, max +22.41; log10|det A0| min -8.77
```

#### Major objections
1. **PLR (Route 6.2) has a pole of order $M-1$ at every reference node (Gate 1, T0; confirmed at T1).** Each factor is $f_m=\det(A_0+\phi_m[\mathrm{occ}]c_m^{\mathsf T})/\det A_0$, so per spin $\psi_{\mathrm{PLR}}=\det(A_0)^{1-M}\prod_m\det(A_0+\phi_m[\mathrm{occ}]c_m^{\mathsf T})$. When the smallest singular value $\varepsilon$ of $A_0$ goes to zero, $\lvert\psi\rvert\propto\varepsilon^{1-M}$: the check measures a slope of $-35.11$ against the predicted $-35$ at $M=V=36$, while LH-HFDS with the same weights stays at $\log_{10}\lvert\psi\rvert=-5.39$. With the T1 weights, $\max\log_{10}\lvert\prod_mf_m\rvert$ over 800 random spin blocks is $+22$ at $L=6$ and $+78$ at $L=8$, growing with $V$. The worker's own T1 log shows the same symptom ($E_{\mathrm{loc}}\approx-9\times10^{20}$ at $L=8$, against $\lvert E\rvert\lesssim10^2$ for the other families). Under $\lvert\psi\rvert^2$ sampling the chain is drawn to the nodes of the reference and the local energy has unbounded variance, and the order of the pole is extensive. This kills PLR independently of the timing rule.
2. **PLR is dominated by LH-HFDS (Gate 0 inside the portfolio).** With $\phi_m=K[:,m]$ and $c_m^{\mathsf T}=h_m\Omega_m$, LH-HFDS is $\det(A_0+\sum_m\phi_mc_m^{\mathsf T})$ and PLR is $\det A_0\prod_m(1+c_m^{\mathsf T}A_0^{-1}\phi_m)$. The two agree to first order in the corrections. PLR is the independent-factor product of the same $M$ single-hidden-fermion ratios, while LH-HFDS puts them in one determinant. Both are size-consistent for decoupled blocks and both have $\mathcal{O}(N_e^2)$ local energy. Only LH-HFDS is a polynomial in the matrix entries and therefore free of poles. PLR's only advantage is a constant factor $b\approx18$ per connection. Is PLR a valid fermionic amplitude? Yes. Every factor is invariant under row reordering, because $(PA_0)^{-1}P\phi=A_0^{-1}\phi$, so the sign is $\mathrm{sgn}\det A_0\prod_m\mathrm{sgn}f_m$, and the brute-force check with explicit Jordan–Wigner signs confirms the convention. As a construction it is a Slater determinant times a symmetric Jastrow that can change sign, whose factors are one-hidden-fermion HFDS ratios. Quick searches found no published form of it.
3. **Tree-HFDS (Route 6.4) cannot be tested at exact-sum sizes and repeats the Route 2.6 anti-pattern (Gate 3 for testing; mandate item 6).** `TreeHFDS.build_tree` adds coarse levels only while $2^\ell$ divides a square side $L$. On instance 4 ($5\times3$) there is no coarse level, so the proposed T2 would test plain LH-HFDS under another name. On the $4\times4$ instances the levels are four $2\times2$ blocks and one $4\times4$ root, so the root row is a global nonlinear hidden unit. A PASS would then show that one global hidden unit helps, which is NNBF's mechanism and not the hierarchy's. The worker already concedes that a null result is uninformative. Route 2.6 was killed for this exact situation ("all sites lie in the near field on every exact-sum cluster"). **Verdict: KILLED(T0, Gate 3 for testing).** Reopen only with a test that does not use exact sums, for example VMC at $8\times8$ or larger against NNBF, with variance-extrapolated energies, after LH-HFDS has VMC code.
4. **LH-HFDS's T1 covered only one of the leaf's two cost conditions (Gate 4; anti-solution item iv).** "Solved" requires $C_E=o(N_e^3)$ *and* $C_{\mathrm{sw}}=\mathcal{O}(N_e^3)$. The accepted-move update of the carried $Q$ (size $V\times F\times V$ per spin) costs $\mathcal{O}(V^2Fb)$, so a sweep costs $\mathcal{O}(N_eV^2Fb)$. That meets the F2 floor, but with a constant of about $Fb(V/N_e)^2\approx190$ relative to an $N_e^3$ sweep. At $16\times16$, $\delta=1/8$, $F=8$, $b=18$, with NNBF width 128, the operation counts are: LH-HFDS sweep about $2\times10^9$, NNBF sweep about $1.3\times10^9$, NNBF local energy about $10^{10}$, LH-HFDS local energy about $7\times10^7$. The gain per decorrelated sample is therefore about 5 times at the target size, not the $N_e^2$ suggested by the per-connection slope, and it is set by the sweep, which is neither implemented nor checked for drift. Required before any T3 (cheap T1, can run alongside the T2 code): implement accepted-move updates of $A^{-1}$, $T$, $R$ and $Q$; check drift after one sweep against a fresh setup at $L=8$, $16$ and $24$; and report cost per decorrelated sample against an NNBF timing at $L=16$.
5. **The proposed T2 for LH-HFDS is gameable at ED size (Gate 2 and Gate 3 for testing; mandate items 4 and 6).** On instance 4 a $3\times3$ patch covers 9 of 15 sites, so "nonlinearity only within patches" barely restricts anything compared with NNBF, whose hidden units see 15 sites. The worker's own Gate-3 argument ("the restriction does not depend on $b$") is the round-2 failure mode ("PASS nearly guaranteed") in a new form. The T2 also has no foil that isolates the global linear coupling $K$. Redesign (see the T2 proposal below): the route arm uses a patch that is small relative to the cluster, and the foil is LH-HFDS with one-site features. The one-site arm is a lattice backflow of the Tocchio–Becca–Parola–Sorella type (PRB 78, 041101, 2008) with a long-range, spin-resolved $\eta$ coupling. It is a known class and is the right Minimal Contrast Foil.
#### Minor objections
1. **The SK-LR and SK-AGP Gate-4 lemma reaches the right conclusion with a wrong citation.** Lin–Tegmark–Rolnick's $2^{M-1}$ bound is about *exact multiplication* of real inputs. The sign question needs less, so the bound overstates the requirement. A sound version has three steps.
    * Sign suffices, so $e^J$ is irrelevant. Restrict each block to two states with opposite block sign $s_b$. The target sign is the parity $\prod_bs_b$, and parity on $M$ bits has polynomial-threshold degree $M$ (Minsky–Papert).
    * With a block-separable trunk, or with any trunk whose hidden units are local (the obvious rescue that keeps the forward pass incremental), each term $\prod_\sigma(1+c^{\mathsf T}y)$ is a polynomial of degree at most 2 (at most 4 for local trunks) in block functions. A sum of $K$ terms has the same degree, so parity is out of reach for $M\ge5$ at **every** $K$.
    * A dense trunk must compute $M$ independent nonlinear block corrections, so $H_s=\Omega(M)=\Omega(N_e)$, which gives $C_E=\Omega(N_e^3)$.
    * The FAIL stands, and so does the recommendation: do not run the rerun with seeds 44, 55 and 66, and kill both routes on landing. The size-consistency requirement does apply to the summed part, because $\det A_0$ (product over blocks) and $e^J$ (positive) cannot supply the block-parity sign, so all sign structure beyond the reference must come from the sum. The note on the arm defect of the override array is moot.
2. **The low-slope guard is defective, and the PLR timing kill is voided.** A lower bound on a timing slope protects against a false PASS. It cannot be evidence for a kill, because the only failure the T1 tests is super-linear growth. The worker also applied it inconsistently: the SK-LR timing "NOT CONFIRMED" was correctly left to the T0 count. Rule for this leaf: a timing slope below the operation-count prediction is recorded as "overhead-bound, cost rests on T0", never as AMBIGUOUS-then-KILL. PLR stays killed, but on Major 1 and 2, not on this guard.
3. **The script prints verdicts the doc does not use.** `run_family` hard-codes the Route 6.1 and 6.2 rule (`0.5 <= sl <= 1.3 and spd >= 10`) for every family, so the Tree-HFDS log says AMBIGUOUS while the doc records PASS. Under `backing-claims`, the printed verdict must be each route's pre-registered rule. Fix this before the output is cited again.
4. **The LH-HFDS slope of 1.06 is not asymptotic evidence.** Its local slopes are 1.56 (from $N_e=504$ to 896) and 0.36 (from 896 to 1400). This is non-monotone and consistent with cache effects: $Q$ is about 67 MB at $L=32$ and 164 MB at $L=40$ per spin. The $\mathcal{O}(VFb)$ claim rests on the operation count, which the code matches (`ratio` gathers `Q[:, :, B]` and forms `kh` in $\mathcal{O}(VF)$). State it that way.
5. **The T2 budget text is inconsistent with the data.** "No smaller cluster qualifies: projected NNBF essentially exact wherever $d_{S\chi}/P\lesssim15$" contradicts instance 4, which qualifies at $d_{S\chi}/P=9.3$, while the cylinder fails at 8.8. The gap matters, not the ratio alone. The "10 s per step" figure is also unmeasured: the NNBF dry run on instance 4 took 3.7 s per step on 16 CPUs. The pre-flight must measure the route's own step time.
6. **Gate 0 for LH-HFDS must cite the lattice-backflow class and SCALE's caching.** A quick WebSearch on 2026-11-16 ("hidden fermion determinant state local hidden rows fast update", and Tocchio et al. lattice backflow) found the HFDS papers (Robledo Moreno et al. PNAS 2022; Liu–Clark arXiv:2311.09450; the $t$–$J$ application arXiv:2411.10430), SCALE's affected-site caching with Woodbury updates (arXiv:2604.25775), and the Tocchio–Becca lattice backflow (PRB 78, 041101; PRB 83, 195138). None of them combines local hidden rows, a factorised output $K\otimes\Omega_m$ and a rank-$b$ carried-table ratio. Gate 0 is PASS, but the mechanism is "a nonlinear local generalisation of long-range lattice backflow with fast updates", and the Gate-0 text should say so.
7. **Parameter count is not fixed per site.** $K$ ($V^2$) and $\Omega_m$ ($VFN_\sigma$) give $P=\mathcal{O}(N_e^2)$ at fixed $F$. This is the same order as $\Phi_0$ and harmless for cost, but "fixed per-site width" should read "fixed $F$".
8. **The PLR exactness lines at $\lvert E\rvert\sim10^{21}$** passed a relative-error test on a pole-dominated energy. Future exactness checks should use weights for which $\lvert E_{\mathrm{loc}}\rvert$ is $\mathcal{O}(N_e)$.
#### Cheapest valid T2 for LH-HFDS (answer to the T2 blocker)
* **Instance.** The critic found no smaller qualifying instance. Every smaller exact-sum candidate in the round-3 and round-5 screens failed the premise, and the parameter floor of LH-HFDS ($2(V^2+VN_\sigma+VFN_\sigma)$, about $10^3$ at $V=15$) prevents shrinking $P$ to restore $d_{S\chi}/P$ on a smaller sector. Instance 4 ($5\times3$ OBC $6+6$) stays, and the budget exception is accepted on that ground. The cost is cut by changing the design, not the instance.
* **Arms** (2 tasks, seed 11, matched $P=1626$ with the remainder in a Jastrow $e^J$):
    * the route arm uses a plus-shaped five-site patch, which covers 5 of 15 sites and gives $b\le10$;
    * the foil arm uses one-site features (lattice backflow with global $K$), which isolates whether nonlinearity within patches carries sign.
    * The $3\times3$ arm is reported only.
    * The reference is the existing NNBF pilot trajectory (job 457482, seed 11), which uses the same protocol, so no control is rerun.
* **Budget.** The T2 decision is taken at the pre-registered 25% checkpoint (75 projected SR steps). At the measured 3.7 s per step on 16 CPUs for NNBF that is about 1.2 CPU-hours per task, within the 2-hour cap if the pre-flight shows the route's step is no slower than $1.5\times$ NNBF's. If it is slower, run on 8 CPUs and record the exception.
* **Kill rule (pre-register before the run).**
    * KILL if, at step 75, the route's projected $\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}$ is at least $1.5\times$ NNBF's at step 75 of its pilot.
    * KILL if the route does not beat the one-site foil by a factor of at least $1/0.85$ in $\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}$. Otherwise, global linear coupling of on-site features already does the work, and that is the known class.
    * PROMOTE to the full 300-step T3 set (instances 0 and 3, three seeds, power-sized from this spread) if the ratio to NNBF is at most 1.15 and the foil is beaten.
    * AMBIGUOUS otherwise; action: a second seed.
* **Pre-flight:** gradients against finite differences, and the fast local energy against $(H\psi)/\psi$ on every configuration of the smoke cluster, both with the OBC patch indexing. The T1 code uses PBC indexing (`lattice` takes the patch mod $L$), which is wrong for instance 4.
#### Per-route verdicts
* **3.1 SK-LR**: KILL on landing (T0, Gate 4), upheld with the corrected lemma (Minor 1). No rerun.
* **3.2 SK-AGP**: KILL on landing (T0, Gate 4), same argument with the pairing reference.
* **6.1′ LH-HFDS**: KEEP at T1. Before T2: the sweep and drift T1 (Major 4), the redesigned arms (Major 5) and the corrected Gate-0 text (Minor 6).
* **6.4 Tree-HFDS**: KILL (T0, Gate 3 for testing; Major 3). Reopen only with a VMC-scale design.
* **6.2 PLR**: the T1 guard kill is voided (Minor 2), but the route is not reinstated: KILL (T0, Gate 1 poles; Major 1), dominated by 6.1′ (Major 2).
* **Portfolio:** one live route, so refill now. Candidate seeds for the mutation worker, each to be gated for extensivity and for freedom from poles:
    * LH-HFPS, the pairing frame of LH-HFDS (local hidden rows on the Foil's own pairing reference, after the round-3 SK-AGP frame change);
    * a two-layer local trunk, which trades $b=\mathcal{O}(r^2)$ for nonlinear range at fixed cost class;
    * Tree-HFDS reopened with a VMC test.

* **The pre-committed AMBIGUOUS action (rerun with seeds 44, 55 and 66) is not run.** It is superseded by the Round 6 Gate 4 FAIL, upheld by the per-leaf critic. Each summed term has bounded polynomial degree, while the block sign is a parity. Keeping accuracy at scale therefore needs a sign trunk whose width grows with $N_e$, which makes the local energy at least cubic. That defeats the leaf's objective.

### Round 7 — Portfolio refill and LH-HFDS T2 preparation (2026-11-16)
* **Pre-registered T1 kill rules (written before any round-7 computation; moved here unchanged when the 526080 section was inserted above).** Script `tests/P2.1_subcubic_amplitudes_r7/t1_r7.py`; random weights scaled so that $\lvert E_{\mathrm{loc}}\rvert=\mathcal{O}(N_e)$ (round-6 Minor 8); at most 4 threads; CPU cap 10 minutes per route, set with `ulimit -t 600` before the run.
    * Routes 7.1 (LH-HFPS) and 7.3 (SH-HFDS): exactness of the carried-table local energy against brute force (sorted rows and columns, explicit Jordan–Wigner signs, every amplitude rebuilt) on $L=4,6,8$ PBC and on the $5\times3$ OBC instance-4 geometry; timing per connection at $L=16,24,32$; operation count per connection printed from the implemented algorithm. KILL if the relative error exceeds $10^{-6}$, or the slope of the time per connection in $N_e$ is at least 1.8, or the printed operation count grows faster than linearly in $N_e$. PASS if the error is at most $10^{-9}$ and the operation count is linear in $N_e$. A timing slope below the operation-count prediction is recorded as overhead-bound (round-6 Minor 2), never as a kill.
    * The same script re-runs LH-HFDS (6.1′) through the same engine (Route 7.3 with the hidden–hidden coupling switched off), with the OBC patch indexing, as a regression check of the round-6 result.
* **Inputs to this round** (binding): the round-6 per-leaf critic (one live route, so refill to at least 3; seeds LH-HFPS and a two-layer local trunk; the pole trap of PLR; the bounded-degree parity trap of the SK sum; the redesigned LH-HFDS T2), and the round-6 extensivity boundary condition.
* **Boundary conditions for every new route.**
    * (B7.1) Size-consistent sign capacity: for decoupled blocks the amplitude must factorize into block amplitudes, each sign-capable, so that the block-parity sign of round-6 Minor 1 is representable at fixed $F$.
    * (B7.2) Pole-free: the amplitude is a polynomial in matrix entries that are bounded functions of the configuration, with no division by a reference determinant.
    * (B7.3) Testable at exact-sum size: the added mechanism must bite on instance 4, against a foil that isolates it.
#### Generation Batch 7
* **Route 7.1 (Operation B.1, frame change to the Foil's pairing frame; critic seed): local-hidden-row hidden-fermion pairing state (LH-HFPS).** With $N_\uparrow=N_\downarrow=N$, a singlet pairing function $F_0\in\mathbb{R}^{V\times V}$, hidden orbitals $K\in\mathbb{R}^{V\times V}$ and $C(\mathbf{n})[m,d]=h_m(\mathbf{n})\cdot\Omega_m[:,d]$ ($\Omega_m\in\mathbb{R}^{F\times V}$):
$$\psi(\mathbf{n})=e^{J(\mathbf{n})}\det\begin{pmatrix}F_0[\mathrm{occ}_\uparrow,\mathrm{occ}_\downarrow]&K[\mathrm{occ}_\uparrow,:]\\-C(\mathbf{n})[:,\mathrm{occ}_\downarrow]&I_V\end{pmatrix}=e^{J}\det\big(F_0[\mathrm{occ}_\uparrow,\mathrm{occ}_\downarrow]+K[\mathrm{occ}_\uparrow,:]\,C(\mathbf{n})[:,\mathrm{occ}_\downarrow]\big).$$
    - Hop response: an up hop replaces one row and a down hop one column; both change the $b$ hidden rows whose patch contains a touched site. The augmented matrix $M$ (size $n=N+V$) therefore changes by rank at most $2+b$. The exact ratio is a two-stage determinant-lemma formula (rows first, then the column) from the carried $M^{-1}$, $G_r=[F_0[:,\mathrm{occ}_\downarrow],K]M^{-1}$, $G_c=M^{-1}[F_0[\mathrm{occ}_\uparrow,:];-C]$ and $X=G_rG_c'$ (formulas in the docstring of `LHHFPS.ratio`). Cost per connection $\mathcal{O}(bFN+nb^2)$, so $C_E=\mathcal{O}(zN_e^2(bF+b^2))$.
    - Gate 0 (Novelty & Adoption): PASS. HFPS (arXiv:2507.10705; Hubbard application arXiv:2511.07566) uses $\tilde n\ll N_e$ hidden fermions whose block comes from a global network, so every hidden entry changes per hop and low-rank updates help only while $\tilde n$ is small. Tocchio–Becca backflow (PRB 78, 041101; PRB 83, 195138) corrects the BCS pairing function with one-site, linear-in-occupation terms. A quick WebSearch on 2026-11-16 ("hidden fermion Pfaffian local hidden rows pairing backflow lattice fast update"; "backflow BCS pairing function lattice Tocchio Becca Gros") found no construction with an extensive number of patch-local hidden rows in the pairing frame. The mechanism is LH-HFDS's (a nonlinear local generalisation of long-range lattice backflow with a rank-$b$ update) moved into the Foil's own frame. It is not a new class beyond that.
    - Gate 1: PASS. Exact (determinant lemma, no truncation), number- and $S^z$-conserving, singlet pairing.
    - Gate 2: PASS. It contains the Foil (projected BCS times Jastrow, $\Omega=0$) and the Tocchio–Becca pairing backflow (one-site features), which is its T2 foil.
    - Gate 3: PASS for the sign half on the round-5 instances (doped, $t'\ne0$, 2D, singlet).
    - Gate 4 (Scaling & Relevance), T0: PASS, with one stated requirement.
        * Cost: $C_E=\mathcal{O}(N_e^2)$ at fixed $(F,b)$ (operation count above, T1 below).
        * Accuracy: for decoupled blocks with block-local $F_0$, $K$ and $\Omega_m$, $M$ is block-diagonal whenever every block has $n_\uparrow=n_\downarrow$. The amplitude is then a product of block amplitudes, so the block-parity sign is representable (B7.1).
        * AGP's known size-consistency defect is the pair-number fluctuation between fragments. It is an amplitude effect that a density Jastrow removes exactly (Neuscamman, PRL 109, 203001, 2012). The requirement is therefore a two-body density Jastrow, which costs $\mathcal{O}(V)$ per connection.
        * Pole-free (B7.2): $\psi$ is a determinant of bounded entries.
    - *Verdict*: SURVIVED to T1.
* **Route 7.2 (Operation A on the sharpest gap of 6.1′; critic seed): two-layer local trunk (LH2).** $h^{(2)}_m=\tanh\big(W_2\,[h^{(1)}_{m'}]_{m'\in\mathrm{patch}(m)}+b_2\big)$ feeds the hidden rows of LH-HFDS.
    - Gates 0–2 against the literature: PASS, as for 6.1′. Gate 4 (cost): PASS, since $b$ becomes the number of radius-$2r$ receptive fields containing a touched site, still a constant.
    - Gate 0 inside the portfolio: **FAIL**. The number of changed hidden rows, and with it the cost class and constant, depends only on the receptive field, and a one-layer patch of the same radius has the same receptive field. LH2 is a parameter-efficiency variant of LH-HFDS's feature map, a point on the patch-size axis of 6.1′, not a separate route.
    - Gate 3 for testing (B7.3): **FAIL**. Two plus-shaped layers reach Manhattan radius 2, which covers 11 of the 15 sites of instance 4 from its centre. This is the Tree-HFDS situation (round-6 Major 3): a PASS would show only that near-global nonlinear features help.
    - *Verdict*: KILLED(T0, Gate 0 in portfolio and Gate 3 for testing). Reopen only with a VMC-scale test, together with Tree-HFDS.
* **Route 7.3 (Operation B.3, cross-field translation from Gaussian Markov random fields, where a sparse precision matrix gives dense correlations; Rue & Held 2005): sparse hidden–hidden HFDS (SH-HFDS).** LH-HFDS plus a configuration-dependent sparse hidden–hidden block, with $\hat d$ the four nearest-neighbour directions:
$$\psi=e^{J}\prod_\sigma\det\begin{pmatrix}\Phi_{0\sigma}[\mathrm{occ}_\sigma]&K_\sigma[\mathrm{occ}_\sigma,:]\\-C_\sigma(\mathbf{n})&I+L_\sigma(\mathbf{n})\end{pmatrix},\qquad L_\sigma[m,m+\hat d]=h_m(\mathbf{n})\cdot\lambda_{\sigma,m,\hat d}.$$
    - By the Schur complement, $\psi_\sigma=\det(I+L)\det\big(\Phi_0[\mathrm{occ}]+K[\mathrm{occ}](I+L)^{-1}C\big)$. The resolvent $(I+L)^{-1}=\sum_k(-L)^k$ sums products of local features along lattice paths. The effective backflow is therefore non-local *and* nonlinear (string-like products, the structure of a hole moving through a spin background), which LH-HFDS lacks. A hop still changes only $1+b$ rows of the augmented matrix.
    - Gate 0: PASS. HFDS (Robledo Moreno et al., PNAS 119, e2122059119; $t$–$J$ application arXiv:2411.10430) has a dense hidden–hidden block from a global network. A quick WebSearch on 2026-11-16 ("hidden fermion determinant sparse hidden-hidden block resolvent string backflow t-J neural") found no sparse, local, configuration-dependent hidden–hidden block. The adoption barrier it targets is P1 (local backflow trails global backflow) without paying P2 (depth undoes locality): non-local nonlinearity enters through the resolvent, not through depth.
    - Gate 1: PASS. Exact, number- and $S^z$-conserving.
    - Gate 2: PASS. It contains LH-HFDS ($\lambda=0$), which is its T2 foil, so the test isolates the hidden–hidden coupling.
    - Gate 3 for testing (B7.3): PASS. On instance 4 the resolvent couples every pair of sites through paths, while the foil with the same patch has no non-local nonlinearity, so the comparison bites at exact-sum size.
    - Gate 4, T0: PASS. Cost $C_E=\mathcal{O}(zN_e^2(bF+b^2))$, as for 7.1. Accuracy: with block-local $\lambda$, $M$ is block-diagonal for decoupled blocks (B7.1). Pole-free (B7.2): $\psi=\det M$ is a polynomial in bounded entries, and the factor $\det(I+L)$ cancels the pole of $(I+L)^{-1}$ in the Schur form.
    - *Verdict*: SURVIVED to T1.
* **Route 7.4 (Operation B.3 from machine learning): linear-attention backflow with a global summary.** Rows $a_j=\Phi_0[x_j]+\phi(q_j)^{\mathsf T}S\,W$ with $S=\sum_k\phi(k_k)v_k^{\mathsf T}$, which a hop changes by rank $\mathcal{O}(1)$.
    - Gate 4, T0: **FAIL**. A linear readout of $S$ makes the global correction rank at most $\dim\phi$: one fixed-size hidden-fermion factor shared by all blocks, the GK-BF trap of round 6, not size-consistent. A nonlinear readout changes every row per hop, which is the F1 bound ($C_E=\Omega(N_e^3)$).
    - *Verdict*: KILLED(T0, Gate 4).
#### Tier log (round 7)
* **Implementation note.** One augmented-matrix engine (`t1_r7.py`) serves all three local-hidden-row families. It carries $M^{-1}$ and the Green's functions $G_r$ (and $G_c$, $X$ for the pairing frame) and forms the changed rows explicitly, so the per-connection cost is $\mathcal{O}(bFN+nb^2)$. The round-6 table $Q$ ($V\times F\times V$) is no longer needed. The first small-size debug run found a wrong term in the LH-HFPS exchange move: $D_iG_c$ used $G_c[i,y]$ instead of $F_0[x_u,y]$ (since $M[i]M^{-1}=e_i$, the column table maps back to raw column entries). It was fixed before the registered run, which checks every move type.
* **T1, Routes 7.1, 7.3 and the 6.1′ regression** (`tests/P2.1_subcubic_amplitudes_r7/t1_r7_output.txt`; 4 threads; 34 CPU-seconds in total):

Analytically an accepted move costs $\mathcal{O}(n^2b)$, so a sweep costs $\mathcal{O}(N_en^2b)$ with $n=N_e/2+V$. That meets the F2 floor with a constant of about $b(1+2V/N_e)^2\approx46$ relative to $N_e^3$ at doping $1/8$ and $b=10$. This is required before any T3, not before T2.
#### LH-HFDS (6.1′) T2: training code, pre-flight and rule
* **Code** (`tests/P2.1_subcubic_amplitudes_r7/`):

### Round 8 — Accepted-move drift (Major 4), LH-HFPS training code and the 7.1 and 7.3 T2 designs (2026-11-16)
* **Pre-registered T1 rule for the accepted-move update (written before the registered run).** Script `tests/P2.1_subcubic_amplitudes_r7/accept_update.py`, using the round-7 engines unchanged, with random weights as in round 7; at most 4 threads; `ulimit -t 600` per run, one run per route.
    * Algebra check: after one accepted move, the carried tables equal a fresh rebuild in the same slot order to at most $10^{-11}$ (normalised max error). Otherwise the result is BUG, not a verdict.
    * Drift metric $\delta$: the maximum over checkpoints (every 1000 accepted moves) of the normalised max error of $M^{-1}$ and $G_r$ (and $G_c$ and $X$ for 7.1), and of $\lvert E_{\mathrm{loc}}^{\mathrm{carried}}-E_{\mathrm{loc}}^{\mathrm{fresh}}\rvert/(1+\lvert E_{\mathrm{loc}}\rvert)$.
    * PASS if $\delta\le10^{-8}$ after at least $10^4$ accepted moves with no refresh, on every geometry (the $5\times3$ OBC instance-4 geometry and $L=6$, 8 and 16 PBC).
    * Otherwise $K^*$ is the number of accepted moves until $\delta$ first exceeds $10^{-8}$. A refresh costs $n^3$ against $n^2b$ per update, so it keeps the cost class if $K^*\ge n_t/b$, where $n_t=368$ and $b=10$ at $16\times16$ and doping $1/8$. KILL if $K^*<37$; else PASS with a refresh every $K^*/2$ moves.
    * Cost: KILL if the slope of the operation count per accepted move in $N_e$ exceeds 2.15 (timing at $L=8$, 16 and 24). A lower timing slope is overhead-bound (round-6 Minor 2).
#### Tier log (round 8): accepted-move update and drift (critic Major 4)
* **Implementation** (`accept_update.py`, Woodbury updates on top of the round-7 engines). In the Slater frame (6.1′ and 7.3), per spin, $Z=S^{-1}DM^{-1}$, then $M^{-1}\leftarrow M^{-1}-M^{-1}[:,R]Z$ and $G_r\leftarrow G_r-G_r[:,R]Z$. The pairing frame (7.1) uses two stages: the changed rows first (with $G_c$ and $X$ updated alongside), then a Sherman–Morrison update for the down-particle column. The cost per accepted move is $\mathcal{O}(n^2b+Vnb)$, plus $\mathcal{O}(bnV)$ in the pairing frame.
* **Debug finding before the registered run (7.1 only).** The first form of the column stage took its vectors from the identities $G_c[:,x_d]=e_j$ and from columns of $X$. It is algebraically exact (every single move agrees with a rebuild to $10^{-15}$), but it feeds the errors of $G_c$ and $X$ back into $M^{-1}$ and $G_r$, and it is numerically unstable: the drift is $10^{-7}$ after 200 accepted moves and $10^{16}$ after 600 at $L=6$ (`accept_update_hfps_feedback_output.txt`, reproducible with `--hfps-form feedback`). The registered form computes $u=M^{-1}dc$, $q=G_rdc$ and row $j$ of $M^{-1}$ times the column table from the primary tables only, at the same cost order, so $G_c$ and $X$ are slaves of $M^{-1}$ and $G_r$. Any production pairing-frame code must use the slaved form.
* **Registered results** (`accept_update_{hfps,sh,lh}_output.txt`; 4 threads; about 7 CPU-minutes per route). Every one-move algebra check is at most $2.4\times10^{-13}$.

| Route | max drift over $10^4$ accepted moves without refresh ($5\times3$ OBC; $L=6$; $L=8$; $L=16$) | ops per accepted move, slope in $N_e$ | rebuild time over update time at $L=24$ | verdict |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| 7.1 LH-HFPS | $1.6\times10^{-11}$; $7.0\times10^{-12}$; $5.6\times10^{-11}$; $1.6\times10^{-11}$ | 2.00 | 3 | PASS (no refresh needed) |
| 7.3 SH-HFDS | $1.3\times10^{-13}$; $3.7\times10^{-13}$; $8.4\times10^{-13}$; $1.7\times10^{-12}$ | 2.00 | 12 | PASS (no refresh needed) |
| 6.1′ LH-HFDS | $8.8\times10^{-14}$; $4.2\times10^{-13}$; $1.0\times10^{-12}$; $7.2\times10^{-12}$ | 2.00 | 12 | PASS (no refresh needed) |

* The drift does not grow with the number of moves (SH-HFDS at $L=16$: $5\times10^{-13}$ after 1000 moves, $8\times10^{-13}$ after $10^4$). It stays at least two orders of magnitude below the $10^{-8}$ threshold, so $K^*$ is never reached. A sweep therefore costs $\mathcal{O}(N_e\cdot n^2b)=\mathcal{O}(N_e^3)$, the F2 floor, with no refresh term. The timing slopes (1.1 to 1.6) are overhead-bound. At $L=24$ the update is 12 times cheaper than a rebuild in the Slater frame, and 3 times cheaper in the pairing frame, where the stage-1 product of $Z$ with the column table costs $knV$.
* **Still open from Major 4:** the cost per decorrelated sample against an NNBF timing at $L=16$. It is required before any T3, not before T2.

$$\psi=e^{J_d+J_t}\det\big(F_0[\mathrm{occ}_\uparrow,\mathrm{occ}_\downarrow]+K[\mathrm{occ}_\uparrow,:]\,C(\mathbf{n})[:,\mathrm{occ}_\downarrow]\big),\qquad J_d=\sum_{i\le j}v_{ij}\tilde n_i\tilde n_j,$$

All are within $2V+2$ of $P=1626$. $F=5$ does not fit, because its fixed blocks alone have 1630 parameters.

* **Pairing-frame column update in the feedback form**: algebraically exact, but feeding the slaved tables ($G_c$, $X$) back into $M^{-1}$ and $G_r$ makes the error grow exponentially (about $10^{16}$ after 600 moves). ### Round 8b: cost per decorrelated sample (critic Major 4) (2026-11-16)
* **Pre-registered rule (written before any round-8b measurement).** Script `tests/P2.1_subcubic_amplitudes_r8b/cost_per_sample.py`, which imports the round-7 and round-8 engines (`t1_r7.py`, `accept_update.py`) read-only; nothing that jobs 698229 and 699118 import is edited.
    * Setting: $t$–$t'$–$J$ ($t'=-0.25$, $J=0.4$), $L\times L$ PBC with $L=8$, 12 and 16, doping $1/8$ ($N_e=56$, 126 and 224). Same symmetric Metropolis proposal for every arm (a random site and one of its 8 hopping neighbours; a hop if the target is empty, a spin exchange on nearest-neighbour up–down pairs, otherwise a null attempt), acceptance $\min(1,\lvert\rho\rvert^2)$. One sweep is $4V$ attempts, with the local energy evaluated once per sweep.
    * Arms at the T2 widths: 6.1′ LH-HFDS (plus patch, $F=5$), 7.3 SH-HFDS (plus patch, $F=5$, $\lambda$ scale 0.3), 7.1 LH-HFPS (plus patch, $F=4$), and NNBF with backflow width $H=6$ (the T2 `nnbf` arm). Jastrow factors are omitted from all arms (an $\mathcal{O}(V)$ term per connection).
    * Metric per arm and size: $c=t_{\mathrm{sw}}\,\tau_{\mathrm{int}}$, with $t_{\mathrm{sw}}$ the wall time per sweep (proposals, accepted-move updates and the local energy) and $\tau_{\mathrm{int}}$ the integrated autocorrelation time of $E_{\mathrm{loc}}$ in sweeps (Sokal window, $c_{\mathrm{w}}=6$; error $\tau\sqrt{2(2W+1)/n}$). Ratio $R(L)=c_{\mathrm{NNBF}}/c_{\mathrm{route}}$, error from the two $\tau$ errors in quadrature.
    * KEEP-FOR-T3 if $R(16)\ge3$ and $R$ grows with $L$ ($R(16)>R(12)>R(8)$ on central values). DOWNGRADE (constant-factor tool only) if $R(16)\ge1$ but $R$ does not grow. KILL-COST if $R(16)<1$. A verdict whose $1\sigma$ interval at $L=16$ straddles 3 or 1 is flagged "uncertain".
    * Budget: one process per arm, single-threaded, `ulimit -t 290` each, so at most 19.3 CPU-minutes in total.
* **Parameters.** None of the arms is trained. The route arms use the round-7 random initialisation: $\Phi_0$ is the lowest orbitals of $-T$ plus $0.1/\sqrt V$ noise, $K\sim0.5/\sqrt V$ and $\Omega\sim0.5/\sqrt{Fn}$, so the backflow term $KC$ is about 20% of the reference entries. NNBF uses the same $\Phi_0$, $W_1\sim\mathcal N(0,1/2V)$, and $W_2$ scaled so that the backflow output has RMS $0.2/\sqrt V$, which is the same 20% relative strength. All four arms give $\langle E_{\mathrm{loc}}\rangle/N_e$ between $-0.48$ and $-0.63$ and acceptance between 0.29 and 0.33. **Caveat:** a trained state can have a different $\tau_{\mathrm{int}}$, and so can a trained NNBF with a stronger backflow.
* **Pre-flight** (`check_output.txt`): the NNBF fast local energy (slot-ordered rows, batched determinants) equals `t1_r7.eloc_brute` (sorted rows, explicit Jordan–Wigner signs) on $4\times4$ and $6\times6$ to at most $2.8\times10^{-15}$ relative. The proposal adjacency is symmetric. The route engines are the ones already verified in rounds 7 and 8.
* **Result** (`run_{lh,sh,hfps,nnbf}.log`, `aggregate_output.txt`; single-threaded; 270 CPU-seconds per arm, about 19 CPU-minutes including the checks). A sweep has about $1.6N_e$ valid proposals.

| Arm | $t_{\mathrm{sw}}$ (s) at $L=8$, 12, 16 | $\tau_{\mathrm{int}}$ at $L=8$, 12, 16 | sweeps at $L=16$ | $R=c_{\mathrm{NNBF}}/c_{\mathrm{route}}$ at $L=8$, 12, 16 | $t_{\mathrm{sw}}$ ratio at $L=16$ | verdict |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| NNBF ($H=6$) | 0.018; 0.093; 0.461 | $1.14\pm0.14$; $1.12\pm0.23$; $1.00\pm0.33$ | 344 | — | — | reference |
| 6.1′ LH-HFDS | 0.056; 0.145; 0.325 | $1.22\pm0.29$; $0.99\pm0.23$; $1.48\pm0.43$ | 498 | $0.31\pm0.08$; $0.73\pm0.22$; $0.96\pm0.42$ | 1.42 | **KILL-COST (uncertain)** |
| 7.3 SH-HFDS | 0.058; 0.147; 0.318 | $0.98\pm0.22$; $0.73\pm0.16$; $0.98\pm0.23$ | 507 | $0.37\pm0.09$; $0.98\pm0.28$; $1.47\pm0.60$ | 1.45 | **DOWNGRADE (uncertain)** |
| 7.1 LH-HFPS | 0.038; 0.120; 0.358 | $0.92\pm0.16$; $0.87\pm0.20$; $1.23\pm0.36$ | 449 | $0.59\pm0.13$; $1.00\pm0.30$; $1.04\pm0.46$ | 1.29 | **DOWNGRADE (uncertain)** |

* **Reading.**
    * At these parameters every $\tau_{\mathrm{int}}$ is about one sweep, equal within error across arms. The ratio $R$ is therefore set by the sweep time, and the noise in $\tau$ (30–45% at $L=16$) dominates its error bar.
    * The sweep-time ratio grows with $L$ for all three routes (from about 0.3–0.5 at $L=8$ to 1.3–1.45 at $L=16$), in the predicted direction. But at $L=16$ it is far below the pre-registered 3 and below the critic's op-count estimate of about 5. Two reasons. First, the NNBF here has the T2 width $H=6$, so its output layer is negligible and its cost is the two $N\times N$ determinants per proposal; the critic's estimate assumed width 128. Second, the route sweep is set by the $\mathcal O(n^2b)$ accepted-move updates on the augmented matrix ($n=N+V=368$), as Major 4 predicted, plus per-connection Python overhead, while the NNBF local energy is batched.
    * All three verdicts are flagged uncertain: the $1\sigma$ interval at $L=16$ contains 1 for every route. LH-HFDS is KILL-COST on its central value of 0.96. SH-HFDS and LH-HFPS reach $R(16)\ge1$, and both grow strictly with $L$, but both stay below 3. Under the rule they are therefore DOWNGRADE.
* **Consequence for T3.** At the matched T2 width, no route has a cost-per-sample advantage over NNBF large enough to justify T3 on cost grounds at $L\le16$. A positive T2 outcome now buys at most a constant-factor tool, about $1.5\times$ at $16\times16$. A route's cost case at T3 would need either a wider NNBF reference (the op-count crossover grows with $H$) or $L\ge24$. Neither is pre-registered here, and either would be a new rule.
* **Code:** `tests/P2.1_subcubic_amplitudes_r8b/cost_per_sample.py` (`--mode check`, `run`, `aggregate`).

* **Orchestrator note on Round 8b (2026-11-16).** The verdicts stand as pre-registered: 6.1′ KILL-COST; 7.1 and 7.3 DOWNGRADE; all flagged uncertain (the $1\sigma$ interval of $R(16)$ contains 1). The comparison width $H=6$ was set by the orchestrator's brief ("same backflow width as the T2 arms"). At that width NNBF's network cost is negligible, so the test measured determinant-update cost, not the regime the leaf targets (production NNBF widths, which the Round 6 critic's estimate of about 5× assumed at $H=128$). This is recorded as a brief defect, not used to overturn the verdicts.
* **Pre-planned follow-up (Round 8c, pre-registered here before any measurement).** Measure $R(L,H)$ for $L=8,12,16$ and NNBF widths $H=6,32,64,128$, with the same metric and caps. The scored width is the *accuracy-matched* one: the smallest $H$ at which NNBF's $\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}$ on instance 4 is within the route's T2 value (from jobs 698229 and 699118). Rule at that $H$: KEEP-FOR-T3 if $R(16)\ge3$ and $R$ grows with $L$; DOWNGRADE if $R(16)\ge1$ but flat; KILL-COST if $R(16)<1$. A route that does not PROMOTE at T2 is not measured.

| arm | parameters | $\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}$ | $\Delta E_{\mathrm{tot}}$ | s per step |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| `lh_plus` (route) | 1617 | 0.0655 | 0.186 | 12.8 |
| `lh_site` (one-site foil) | 1641 | 0.0755 | 0.208 | 9.2 |
| `nnbf` (reference) | 1626 | 0.0880 | 0.248 | 3.7 |
| `lh_3x3` (reported only) | 1625 | 0.0388 | 0.136 | 16.1 |

* **Reported-only arm.** The $3\times3$ patch gives $\rho_N=0.44$ and $\rho_F\approx0.51$ at matched parameters. It cannot be promoted after the fact. It becomes **route 6.1″ (LH-HFDS with a $3\times3$ patch)**, which needs its own pre-registered T2 on a new seed, its own Gate 4 cost check (16.1 s per step against NNBF's 3.7 at this width), and a foil that isolates patch size (the plus patch and the one-site arm).

### Round 9 — Route 6.1″ (LH-HFDS, $3\times3$ patch): Gate 4 at T0, T2 design and pre-flight (2026-11-16)
* **Inputs** (binding): the 698229 result above (6.1′ KILL by the foil clause; the $3\times3$ arm reported only, $\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}=0.0388$ at $P=1625$ and 16.1 s per step against NNBF's 3.7); the Round 8b verdicts and the pre-registered Round 8c. Jobs 699118 and 699305 are in flight, so `lh_train.py`, `t2_routes.py` and their imports are imported read-only. New code lives in `tests/P2.1_subcubic_amplitudes_r9/`.
* **Notation.** $p$ is the number of sites in a patch ($p=1$, 5, 9 for `site`, `plus`, `3x3`); $H$ is the NNBF hidden width; $H_c$ is the compute-matched width defined below; $t_{\mathrm{st}}$ is the wall time of one projected SR step on instance 4 at 4 threads; $\rho_C=\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}(\text{route})/\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}(\texttt{nnbf\_c})$.
#### Gate 4 at T0 for 6.1″
* **Changed hidden rows.** $b=\lvert B\rvert$, with $B$ the union of the patches that contain a touched site. Counted with `lh_geom.patch_index` on a $16\times16$ torus: $b=12$ for a nearest-neighbour hop or exchange and $b=14$ for a $t'$ hop with the $3\times3$ patch, against $b=8$ for both with the plus patch. The patch size enters only through $b$, which stays a lattice constant.
* **Local energy.** Per connection, $C_{\mathrm{amp}}=\mathcal{O}(bFN+nb^2)$ with $n=N+V$ (round-7 engine), so $C_E=\mathcal{O}(zN_e^2(bF+b^2))$. The $\mathcal{O}(N_e^2)$ class is unchanged. The constant grows by $14/8=1.75$ in the $bF$ term and by $(14/8)^2\approx3.1$ in the $nb^2$ term.
* **Accepted-move update.** A Woodbury update of rank $1+b$ costs $\mathcal{O}(n^2b)$, so a sweep stays $\mathcal{O}(N_e^3)$ with a constant 1.75 times the plus patch's. The algebra does not depend on the patch, but the drift was measured only for the plus patch. It is re-measured below for the $3\times3$ patch (T1 rule written before the run).
* **Extensivity.** The decoupled-block argument of 6.1′ (block-local $K$ and $\Omega_m$ give a block-diagonal $A$) does not depend on $p$. Gate 4 accuracy: PASS, unchanged.
* **Is the gain "patch size = more effective width"?** Not in parameters: the patch enters only $W_l$ ($2pF$ entries, 90 at $p=9$ against 50 at $p=5$), and the arms are matched within one Jastrow unit. In compute it can be. The operation count per amplitude on instance 4 is about $VF(2p+N_\uparrow+N_\downarrow)+N_\uparrow VN_\uparrow+N_\downarrow VN_\downarrow\approx3.3\times10^3$ for `lh_3x3`, against $H(2V+V(N_\uparrow+N_\downarrow))\approx1.3\times10^3$ for NNBF at $H=6$. The measured $t_{\mathrm{st}}$ ratio is 4.4. An NNBF of width about 15 to 30 spends the same compute per amplitude or per step. At matched compute NNBF has more parameters, so a matched-$P$ comparison cannot tell whether the $3\times3$ gain comes from the patch mechanism or from spending more compute. **Foil decision:** the T2 adds an NNBF at matched wall time per step, `nnbf_c` (width $H_c$), and its KILL clause is set against it.
* **What the compute match does not settle.** On instance 4 a $3\times3$ patch covers 6.07 of 15 sites on average (9 at the centre); the plus patch covers 3.93 and the one-site patch 1. The round-6 critic (Major 5) flagged exactly this: at this cluster size a $3\times3$ patch is close to a global feature. The T2 can kill 6.1″ (if near-global patch features plus linear $K$ do not beat a compute-matched NNBF here, they will not at scale). A PROMOTE cannot show that the gain survives once the correlation length exceeds the patch. That needs a VMC-scale accuracy test, written into the decision tree below.
* **Exact-sum step time is a proxy.** $t_{\mathrm{st}}$ is the cost of the batched exact-sum forward and gradient over all 420420 configurations, not the VMC cost at scale. Round 8c measures the VMC cost per decorrelated sample, and it runs only after a PROMOTE.
* **Gate 4 verdict (T0): PASS**, pending the drift T1 below.
#### Pre-registered T1 rule: accepted-move drift for the $3\times3$ patch (written before the run)
* Script `tests/P2.1_subcubic_amplitudes_r7/accept_update.py` (unchanged, run read-only) with `--route lh --patch 3x3 --F 5 --geoms 5x3obc,8,16 --accepts 10000 --sizes 8,12,16`, 4 threads, `ulimit -t 600`. The Round 8 rule applies unchanged: BUG if the one-move algebra check exceeds $10^{-11}$; PASS if the drift $\delta\le10^{-8}$ after $10^4$ accepted moves with no refresh on every geometry; KILL if $K^*<37$ or the slope of the operation count per accepted move exceeds 2.15. If the CPU limit stops the run, the completed geometries are reported and the verdict is INCOMPLETE.
#### Pre-registered T2 rule, Route 6.1″ (written 2026-11-16, before any round-9 computation)
* **Setting.** Instance 4 ($5\times3$ OBC $6+6$), **seed 22** (new), 75 $P_\chi$-projected SR steps, pilot settings (step 0.05, shift $10^{-3}$, 2000 samples, 4000 L-BFGS and 10 fixed-node iterations). Metric: $\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}$ of the projected state at the end (P1.1 upper estimate).
* **Arms** (one task each, 4 CPUs):
    * `lh_3x3`: route, $3\times3$ patch, $F=5$, $P=1625$;
    * `lh_plus`: patch-size foil, $P=1617$;
    * `lh_site`: one-site arm, $P=1641$, reported (it gives the patch-size trend for the autopsy);
    * `nnbf`: NNBF at matched parameters ($H=6$, $P=1626$);
    * `nnbf_c`: NNBF at matched wall time per step, width $H_c$.
* **Definition of $H_c$** (fixed before the run by the pre-flight, one process, one node, 4 threads): $t_{\mathrm{st}}$ is the mean over 2 SR steps at seed 22. $H_c$ is the smallest $H$ in the grid 8, 12, 16, 20, 24, 32, 48 (scanned upward) with $t_{\mathrm{st}}(\texttt{nnbf},H)\ge t_{\mathrm{st}}(\texttt{lh\_3x3})$; if no width matches, $H_c=48$. $H_c$ and every arm's step-0 energy are written to `r9/preflight_config.json`.
* **Ratios.** $\rho_N=\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}(\texttt{lh\_3x3})/\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}(\texttt{nnbf})$, $\rho_C=\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}(\texttt{lh\_3x3})/\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}(\texttt{nnbf\_c})$, $\rho_F=\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}(\texttt{lh\_3x3})/\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}(\texttt{lh\_plus})$.
* **Rule, in order.**
    1. INCOMPLETE if `lh_3x3`, `lh_plus`, `nnbf` or `nnbf_c` is missing.
    2. INVALID (fix and rerun the named task, no verdict): calibration error above $10^{-3}\Delta E_{\mathrm{tot}}$; multi-start spread at least 0.05; an arm's step-0 energy differs from the pre-flight value by more than $10^{-6}$; `nnbf_c` was not trained at the configured $H_c$; `lh_3x3`, `lh_plus` or `nnbf` is not within $2V+2$ of $P=1626$; an NNBF arm stopped early; or the in-job $t_{\mathrm{st}}$ ratio `nnbf_c`/`lh_3x3` is below 0.5 (the compute match failed grossly).
    3. Early stop: at SR step 50, KILL if $E(50)-E_0>2(E_{\mathrm{pilot}}(50)-E_0)=0.681t$ for `lh_3x3` (the 6.1′ threshold, from NNBF seed 11).
    4. UNINFORMATIVE if $\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}(\texttt{nnbf})<0.005t$, or if both $\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}(\texttt{nnbf\_c})$ and $\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}(\texttt{lh\_3x3})$ are below $0.005t$.
    5. KILL if $\rho_C\ge1$ (a compute-matched NNBF is at least as good) or $\rho_F>0.85$ (the $3\times3$ patch does not beat the plus patch).
    6. PROMOTE if $\rho_N\le0.75$ and $\rho_C\le0.75$ (with $\rho_F\le0.85$ from step 5).
    7. AMBIGUOUS otherwise; action: one more seed (33) for the five arms.
* **Power.** On seed 11 the route sat at $\rho_N=0.44$ and $\rho_F=0.59$ against the plus patch, so both PROMOTE margins are about 0.3 wide. The seed-to-seed spread is unknown; the AMBIGUOUS band absorbs it and costs one seed.

#### Route 7.1 T2 Result (job 699118, 2026-11-16): PROMOTE

Aggregate: `python3 t2_routes.py --mode aggregate --route hfps` (saved as `tests/P2.1_subcubic_amplitudes_r7/aggregate_699118_output.txt`). Instance 4, seed 11, 75 projected SR steps, no early stop; reference `nnbf` from job 698229.

| arm | parameters | $\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}$ | $\Delta E_{\mathrm{tot}}$ | s per step |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| `hfps_plus` (route) | 1642 | 0.0126 | 0.080 | 7.9 |
| `hfps_site` (one-site foil, pairing frame) | 1610 | 0.0170 | 0.097 | 5.4 |
| `agpj` (projected BCS × J, reported only) | 1633 | 0.0537 | 0.209 | 2.1 |
| `nnbf` (reference, Slater frame) | 1626 | 0.0880 | 0.248 | 3.7 |

* **Verdict: PROMOTE** ($\rho_N=0.143\le1.15$ and $\rho_F=0.738\le0.85$; $\rho_t=0.324$).
* **Attribution caution, before any T3.** Most of the gain over NNBF comes from the pairing frame, not from the sub-cubic patch mechanism. The one-site foil in the same frame already reaches $\rho_N=0.19$, and projected BCS × Jastrow without any network reaches 0.61. The patch contributes the factor 0.74 over the foil. The leaf's question (sub-cubic amplitudes at no accuracy loss) therefore needs a cubic baseline *in the pairing frame* (a Pfaffian NNBF or hidden-fermion Pfaffian at matched cost), which the pre-planned T3 arm list lacks.
* **Other caveats:** one seed; 75 SR steps is early training, where per-step learning speed and expressivity are confounded; Round 8b cost (DOWNGRADE, uncertain) was measured at $H=6$, and the pre-registered Round 8c at the accuracy-matched width is now due.
* **Next:** per-leaf critic on the PROMOTE, then the T3 build (pre-planned arms plus a cubic pairing-frame baseline), with Round 8c and power sizing.

#### Round 9 (continued): Route 6.1″ tier log, amendment A1 and decision tree
* **T1, accepted-move drift for the $3\times3$ patch** (`tests/P2.1_subcubic_amplitudes_r9/accept_update_lh3x3_output.txt`; 4 threads; 5.9 CPU-minutes). The one-move algebra check passes on every geometry (at most $3.9\times10^{-14}$). Over $10^4$ accepted moves with no refresh the maximum drift is $8.7\times10^{-14}$ ($5\times3$ OBC), $4.9\times10^{-13}$ ($L=8$) and $1.8\times10^{-12}$ ($L=16$), so $K^*$ is never reached. The operation count per accepted move has slope 1.99 in $N_e$ ($6.1\times10^5$, $3.1\times10^6$ and $9.7\times10^6$ at $L=8$, 12 and 16); the timing slope of 0.85 is overhead-bound. **Verdict: PASS (no refresh needed).** Gate 4 for 6.1″ is PASS at T0 and T1.
* **Pre-flight, correctness** (`check_output.txt`): analytic energy gradients of `lh_3x3` match central finite differences to at most $1.7\times10^{-9}$ relative, with and without $P_\chi$, on toy instances 9 ($3\times2$) and 8 ($3\times3$ OBC). The same holds for an NNBF of width 24 (at most $3.0\times10^{-9}$). Projected per-sample log-derivatives of `lh_3x3` match to $2.8\times10^{-8}$. The fast carried-table local energy equals $(H\psi)/\psi$ on all 1680 configurations of instance 8 to $2.9\times10^{-13}$.
* **Pre-flight, instance-4 validity** (`calibrate_output.txt`): $E_0=-9.7918583301$ (the pilot's), $\lvert G\rvert=4$, $S=0$, $d_{S\chi}=15080$, non-degenerate, and gap $E_1-E_0=0.0249t\ge0.02t$. `lh_3x3`, `lh_plus` and `nnbf` are within $2V+2$ of $P=1626$. The step-0 energies at seed 22 are stored in `preflight_config.json`.
* **Pre-flight finding that changes the design: there is no wall-time-matched NNBF on instance 4.** Measured $t_{\mathrm{st}}$ (mean of 2 steps, 4 threads, one node): `lh_3x3` 10.34 s; NNBF 3.09, 3.13, 3.16, 3.23, 3.26, 3.34, 3.47 and 3.91 s at $H=6$, 8, 12, 16, 20, 24, 32 and 48 ($P$ up to 10488). The step time is nearly flat in $H$, because the exact-sum step is dominated by work that does not depend on the width (determinants over 420420 configurations, the $P_\chi$ projection, the SR solve). Matching it would need $H\approx280$, i.e. $P\approx6\times10^4$, four times the sector dimension: a foil that can fit the sector and therefore kills by construction. The pre-registered fallback $H_c=48$ would trip the rule's own guard (in-job $t_{\mathrm{st}}$ ratio $0.38<0.5$), so the rule as written returns INVALID by construction. The Gate 4 sentence "an NNBF of width about 15 to 30 spends the same compute per amplitude or per step" is right for the operation count per amplitude and wrong for the step time.
* **Amendment A1 (written after the calibration and before any T2 data; no seed-22 accuracy has been seen).**
    * $H_c$ is the smallest width in the grid whose operation count per amplitude of the implemented forward is at least the route's. Route: $VF(2p+N_\uparrow+N_\downarrow)+N_\uparrow VN_\uparrow+N_\downarrow VN_\downarrow=3330$. NNBF: $2VH+HV(N_\uparrow+N_\downarrow)=210H$. Both add the two $6\times6$ determinants (144). This gives 3474 for the route and 3504 at $H=16$, so **$H_c=16$** ($P=3736$, 2.3 times the matched count; `opmatch_output.txt`). At scale the per-connection costs differ in class ($\mathcal{O}(bFN+nb^2)$ against $\mathcal{O}(HVN+N^3)$); Round 8c measures that.
    * The INVALID guard on the in-job $t_{\mathrm{st}}$ ratio is dropped; the ratio is reported.
    * A sixth arm, `nnbf_w` ($H=48$, $P=10488$, the widest width of the scan), is **reported only**. It answers the wall-time question as far as an NNBF that does not exceed the sector can go.
    * The AMBIGUOUS action becomes seed 33 for all six arms. Everything else in the rule is unchanged. `nnbf_c` keeps its INVALID guards (step-0 energy, configured width, stopped early).
* **Pre-flight, synthetic aggregate** (`synthetic_output.txt`): all 20 cases return their intended verdict. They cover PROMOTE; KILL by $\rho_C=1$, by $\rho_C>1$, by $\rho_F$ and by the early stop; AMBIGUOUS through $\rho_N$ and through $\rho_C$; both UNINFORMATIVE clauses; INCOMPLETE for a missing compute foil and a missing patch foil; PROMOTE with either reported-only arm missing; and seven INVALID guards.
* **Pre-flight, toy controls** (`controls_output.txt`; instance 7, $4\times3$ OBC $4+4$, 30 SR steps; the round-7 toy arms are read from `r7/results_preflight`, read-only, and `nnbf_c` at $H=16$ is trained here):
    * positive control: route := the sign-exact state $P_\chi[\psi_0(1+0.5u)]$ with $u\sim U(0,1)$ ($\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}=-1.8\times10^{-14}$, $\Delta E_{\mathrm{tot}}=0.027$). Verdict PROMOTE, as intended. It is a perfect partner in every branch, since all three ratios are 0.
    * negative control A: route := Slater $\times$ Jastrow. Verdict KILL ($\rho_C=4.87$, $\rho_F=1.98$).
    * negative control B: route := `nnbf_c` itself. Verdict KILL ($\rho_C=1$ exactly), so the compute clause fires on an identical partner.
    * Toy pipeline, not a verdict: `lh_3x3` $\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}=0.0486$ against `nnbf_c` 0.0219 ($\rho_C=2.2$) and `lh_plus` 0.0540 ($\rho_F=0.90$). On the toy, the op-count-matched NNBF is twice as good as the $3\times3$ route. This is a warning for the T2, not evidence: the toy has a smaller sector and a different $P$ balance.
* **CPU used locally in round 9:** about 15 CPU-minutes (calibration 5.9, drift 5.9, controls 1.4, checks and amendment 0.6), under the 30-minute cap. Every run was under `ulimit -t 600` at 4 threads.
* **T2 budget.** At 4 CPUs, 75 steps plus ED and two budgets: `lh_3x3` at most about 1.75 CPU-hours (16.1 s per step on the slower node of 698229, 10.3 s on the pre-flight node); `lh_plus` about 1.5; `lh_site` about 1.2; `nnbf`, `nnbf_c` and `nnbf_w` about 0.6–0.9 each. That is about 7 CPU-hours in total, and every task is within the 2-hour cap. No exception is needed.
* **Decision tree, Route 6.1″ T2 (pre-planned).**
    * PROMOTE: 6.1″ reaches T2. First, Round 8c at the accuracy-matched width, as pre-registered: the smallest NNBF width whose instance-4 $\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}$ is at most `lh_3x3`'s seed-22 value. The seed-22 arms give three points ($H=6$, 16 and 48); intermediate widths, if needed, come from one extra 75-step task each. Then measure $R(L)$ for $L=8$, 12 and 16 with the $3\times3$ engine ($b\le14$) under the 8b metric and caps; KEEP-FOR-T3 if $R(16)\ge3$ and $R$ grows with $L$. Before any T3 claim, run a VMC-scale accuracy test (Gate 3 for testing, above), because on instance 4 a $3\times3$ patch sees 40% of the cluster: $8\times8$ or larger, variance-extrapolated energies, `lh_3x3` against `lh_plus` and an NNBF at the width that 8c sets. That needs VMC code (engineering, not compute). A $3\times3$ patch in the pairing frame (7.1, which PROMOTEd at seed 11 with the plus patch) becomes the natural mutation.
    * KILL by $\rho_C\ge1$: autopsy. At matched operations per amplitude a wider NNBF is at least as good, so the $3\times3$ gain at matched $P$ in 698229 was the effective compute that the larger patch buys, not a better use of it. The class "patch-local hidden rows with linear global coupling" is dead in the Slater frame at fixed compute. Log the anti-pattern "a matched-$P$ win at ED size that a matched-operations NNBF erases". It also flags 7.1: its T3 needs the same op-count-matched foil (a wider cubic baseline in its own frame).
    * KILL by $\rho_F>0.85$: the seed-11 patch-size gain does not reproduce; it was within seed-to-seed spread. 6.1″ is KILLED and the patch-size axis is closed in the Slater frame.
    * KILL by the early stop: as for $\rho_F$; record the step-50 energy.
    * AMBIGUOUS: seed 33 for all six arms (about 7 CPU-hours; run `calibrate` and `opmatch` at seed 33 first to fix the step-0 energies).
    * UNINFORMATIVE: resume all arms to 150 steps from the saved parameters (the 50% checkpoint).
    * INVALID: fix the named guard and rerun the affected task only. INCOMPLETE: wait for the missing task, or rerun it if it failed.
* **Command (submitted by the orchestrator as job 699528):** `cd /home/jek354/research/.claude/worktrees/nqs-brainstorm/brainstorms/nqs-lattice-fermions/tests/P2.1_subcubic_amplitudes_r9 && sbatch submit_t2_3x3.sbatch`. Verdict after all six tasks: `python3 t2_3x3.py --mode aggregate`.

* **Sharpest gap, 6.1″:** whether the matched-$P$ advantage of the $3\times3$ patch ($\rho_N=0.44$ on seed 11) survives an NNBF at matched operations per amplitude ($H_c=16$). The toy pipeline points the other way ($\rho_C=2.2$ on instance 7). Even a PROMOTE leaves open whether the gain survives once the cluster is much larger than the patch.

### Local Autopsy Log (Round 9 additions)
* **Wall-time matching at exact-sum size**: the projected-SR step time on instance 4 hardly depends on the NNBF width (3.1 s at $H=6$, 3.9 s at $H=48$), because work that does not depend on the width dominates it. A foil "matched in wall time" would need $P$ above the sector dimension and would kill by construction. Rule: match compute through the operation count per amplitude of the implemented forward, and leave cost at scale to the VMC cost test (Round 8c).
### Round 8 per-leaf critic on 7.1 PROMOTE (2026-11-16)
* **Inputs read:** the leaf from Round 6 on; `t2_routes.py` and `lh_train.py` (read, not edited, since job 699305 imports them); `aggregate_699118_output.txt`; the round-5 results and logs of job 526080 (`tests/P2.1_subcubic_amplitudes_r5/results`, 1200 SR steps, 3 seeds on instances 0 and 3); the Round 8b harness.
* **Gate 0 search** (quick WebSearch, abstracts only):
    * hidden-fermion Pfaffian states (HFPS): Chen, Wan, Sengupta, Georges and Roth, [arXiv:2507.10705](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.10705). A Pfaffian whose hidden-fermion rows come from a dense group-convolutional network, state of the art on attractive and repulsive Hubbard models. Gutzwiller-projected HFPS has been used for mixed-dimensional $t$–$J$ bilayers ([arXiv:2602.10091](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.10091)). This is the cubic pairing-frame class that 7.1 belongs to.
    * Local convolutional backflow with low-rank determinant updates (SCALE): [arXiv:2604.25775](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.25775), PRX Intelligence 2026. It reports $\mathcal{O}(N^4)\to\mathcal{O}(N^3)$ per sample for Hubbard and $t$–$J$ backflow. This is the Slater-frame form of the "finite receptive field gives low-rank updates" mechanism of 6.1′ and 6.1″, and is not cited in the leaf.
    * Low-rank updates of determinants and Pfaffians in JAX (lrux): [arXiv:2602.05255](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.05255). Pairing-frame backflow on a lattice (Tocchio, Becca and Gros): [arXiv:1102.3017](https://arxiv.org/abs/1102.3017), which is the `hfps_site` foil.
* **What is new in 7.1** beyond "local hidden rows in a pairing frame": only the combination of a finite receptive field for the hidden rows with a *learned linear long-range* row block $K$, in the pairing frame. This gives $\mathcal{O}(N_e)$ per connection and $\mathcal{O}(N_e^2)$ per local energy (drift-free carried tables, Round 8). The novelty residue is therefore a **cost claim at equal accuracy against the dense HFPS of the same frame**. Accuracy against Slater-frame NNBF is not the leaf's claim. Whether SCALE's per-sample $\mathcal{O}(N^3)$ already equals our per-sweep $\mathcal{O}(N_e^3)$ must be checked in its full text before any write-up (Gate 0: PARTIAL, not FAIL).
#### Major objections
1. **Training length confounds expressivity (decisive).** $\rho_N=0.143$ compares states after 75 SR steps. The same NNBF arm (instance 4, seed 11, same protocol) reaches $\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}=0.01585$ at 1200 steps (pilot 457482, `r5/results/inst4_nnbf_s11.json`), 5.6 times below its 75-step value of 0.0880. Against converged NNBF the route's 75-step value 0.0126 is $\rho\approx0.80$, not 0.14. The pairing arms also start 0.37$t$ lower ($-8.431$ against $-8.063$). The reported-only `agpj` shows the reversal: $\rho_N=0.61$ at 75 steps on instance 4, but 1.80 and 1.33 at 1200 steps on instances 0 and 3 (aggregate 526080). **Fix:** the T3 trains every arm for 1200 steps, the round-5 horizon, so the round-5 `nnbf`, `agpj` and `skagp` results on instances 0 and 3 are reused unchanged. 300 steps, the pre-planned T3 length, is not enough.
2. **Attribution: no cubic pairing-frame baseline.** The one-site foil already gives $\rho_N=0.19$ and projected BCS × J gives 0.61, so the frame carries the gain. The leaf's claim is sub-cubic cost *with no accuracy loss*, so it needs a scored cubic arm in the same frame. **Fix:** `hfps_dense` uses the route's ansatz, $F$, $J_d$ and tanh Jastrow unchanged, but every site's feature vector is computed from the whole configuration: $C(\mathbf n)=\sum_f g_f(\mathbf n)\Omega_f$ with $g=\tanh(Wx+b)$. This is a dense hidden-fermion pairing state, or pairing-frame NNBF of width $F$. Every move changes every row of $C$, so a ratio needs a fresh $N\times N$ determinant. Its parameter count is the route's or up to 4% above (never below), which is conservative for the route. Its operation count per amplitude equals the route's within the $2VF$ input layer. That is the matching rule of Round 9 amendment A1, since exact-sum step time cannot be matched. The *deployment* wall-time match is the VMC cost per decorrelated sample at $L=16$, measured in Round 8c. **PASS at T3 must show** a pooled $\rho_D=\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}(\texttt{hfps\_plus})/\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}(\texttt{hfps\_dense})\le1.10$, no instance above 1.35, and a cost advantage against `dense` from Round 8c.
3. **The cost case is unproven at reachable sizes.** Round 8b gave $R(16)=1.04\pm0.46$ against NNBF at $H=6$. If the route is also not cheaper than `dense` at $L=16$, a T3 accuracy PASS buys nothing for this leaf. **Fix:** Round 8c is extended with the dense arm, and its verdict gates the T3 submission (below).
4. **Gate 4 (accuracy size-consistency) cannot be tested on 15–16 sites.** The plus patch covers 3.9 of 15 sites on instance 4, so "local" and "dense" features are nearly the same object there (the round-6 critic's Major 5). T3 parity with `dense` is necessary, not sufficient. The parameters also grow as $V^2F$ ($\Omega$ is $V\times F\times V$, $K$ is $V\times V$). The per-sample SR cost therefore grows as $V^2F$, although the local energy is $\mathcal{O}(N_e^2)$. **Fix:** a T3 PASS leads to a VMC size-trend test (accuracy per site of route against `dense` at $6\times6$ and $8\times8$, matched $P$), not to a leaf closure.
5. **One seed, so the T2 spread is unknown.** Power is sized from the round-5 three-seed spreads at 1200 steps instead. Seed CV is 4–12% for the network arms (`nnbf`, `skagp`, `sk1`) and up to 30% for `agpj`.
#### Minor objections
1. The acronym "LH-HFPS" collides with the published HFPS, so the leaf should call the route "local-feature HFPS". The literature HFPS is the `dense` arm.
2. On instance 3 ($P_{\mathrm{target}}=1478$) the route cannot be matched at $F=4$, because its fixed blocks alone have 1716 parameters. The T3 uses $F=3$ there for route, foil and dense alike.
3. The pre-registered Round 8c scores NNBF at the "accuracy-matched" width. The T2 data contain only $H=6$, and NNBF at $H=6$ does not reach the route's value even at 1200 steps (0.0159 against 0.0126). That width is therefore undetermined, and 8c reports the bracket $H=6$ to $H=128$ instead of inventing one.
4. The 8b and 8c timings use untrained parameters. The scored $\tau_{\mathrm{int}}$ are about one sweep for every arm, so $R$ is set by the sweep time.
* **Verdict: proceed to T3 (redesigned), not redo T2 and not kill.** The redesign uses 1200 steps, adds `hfps_dense` as the scored attribution arm, and is gated on Round 8c against `dense` not being KILL-COST. A plain re-run of the T2 at 75 steps would answer the wrong question (Major 1).
#### Round 8c extension (pre-registered 2026-11-16, before any 8c measurement)
* Script `tests/P2.1_subcubic_amplitudes_r10/cost_8c.py` imports the Round 8b harness read-only. Arms: route `hfps` (re-measured under the current node load), `dense` (dense HFPS, $F=4$; $K\Omega_f$ precomputed, so a ratio costs $FN^2$ for the gather plus one batched $N\times N$ determinant, the cheapest form of the cubic baseline), and `nnbf` at $H=6$, 32, 64 and 128. Sizes $L=8$, 12 and 16; CPU budgets of 30, 50 and 110 s per size; one single-threaded process per arm, at most 4 at a time, `ulimit -t 200`. The total is at most 6 × 190 s = 19 CPU-minutes.
* $R_D(L)=c_{\mathrm{dense}}/c_{\mathrm{route}}$ and $R_N(L,H)=c_{\mathrm{NNBF}(H)}/c_{\mathrm{route}}$. The verdict thresholds are those of Round 8b: KEEP-FOR-T3 if $R(16)\ge3$ and $R$ grows with $L$; DOWNGRADE if $R(16)\ge1$ but not growing; KILL-COST if $R(16)<1$; "uncertain" if the $1\sigma$ interval at $L=16$ contains 1 or 3.
* **Scored:** $R_D$. NNBF: the accuracy-matched width is undetermined (Minor 3). $R_N$ at $H=6$ is the lower bound and $R_N$ at $H=128$ the upper bound. KEEP at $H=6$ means KEEP; KILL-COST at $H=128$ means KILL-COST; otherwise the crossover width is reported.
* **Gate on the T3:** KILL-COST on $R_D$ (central value) means the T3 is not submitted and 7.1 is DOWNGRADED to "pairing-frame accuracy tool without a cost case". A DOWNGRADE on $R_D$ still allows the T3, but a T3 PASS then counts as a constant-factor result only. KEEP allows the T3 with the full sub-cubic claim at stake.
* **Correctness of the dense engine** (`check_8c_output.txt`): the fast slot-ordered batched local energy equals `t1_r7.eloc_brute` after random accepted moves on $4\times4$ and $6\times6$ PBC to at most $5.2\times10^{-15}$ relative.

* **Scored verdict, $R_D$: KILL-COST (uncertain).** At $L=16$ the dense pairing baseline costs the same per decorrelated sample as the route ($R_D=0.99\pm0.56$). Its sweep is even 10% faster (0.38 s against 0.42 s). The low-noise part of $R$, the sweep time, agrees with the central value, so the "uncertain" flag comes only from $\tau$. Reason: with $K\Omega_f$ precomputed, a dense ratio is an $FN^2$ gather plus one batched $N\times N$ determinant, about the same as one round-7 ratio plus the $\mathcal{O}(n^2b)$ accepted-move update on the augmented $n=N+V=368$ matrix. The $\mathcal{O}(N_e^4)$ against $\mathcal{O}(N_e^3)$ class difference per sweep has not set in by $L=16$.

At $H=64$ and 128 the sweep-time ratio at $L=16$ is 10.8 and 23. With $\tau\approx1$, as for every arm where it was measured, that is KEEP; but $\tau$ was not measured there (too few sweeps), so it is reported, not scored. The route has a cost case against NNBF only if NNBF needs a width of order 64 or more to match it in accuracy.

The matched-VMC-wall-time dense arm (`hfps_dense_w`) is not needed in any design, since dense at $F=4$ is already as fast as the route at $L=16$.
