#### T3 rule, Route 7.1 (pre-registered 2026-11-16, before any T3 data)
* **Metric:** $\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}$ of the $P_\chi$-projected state after 1200 SR steps (best energy), from the round-5 budget. Per instance $i$, take the seed median of each arm. $\rho_{D,i}$, $\rho_{F,i}$ and $\rho_{N,i}$ are `hfps_plus` over `hfps_dense`, `hfps_site` and `nnbf`. The pooled values $\bar\rho$ are geometric means over the three instances.
* **Rule, in order:**
    1. INCOMPLETE if any of the 36 scored results is missing.
    2. INVALID if any calibration exceeds $10^{-3}\Delta E_{\mathrm{tot}}$, any multi-start spread is at least 0.05, a non-route arm stopped early, or a task did fewer than 1200 steps.
    3. UNINFORMATIVE if the dense median is below $0.005t$ on at least 2 instances.
    4. KILL if $\bar\rho_D\ge1.30$ (accuracy loss against the cubic pairing baseline), or $\bar\rho_F>0.90$ (the patch does not beat one-site pairing backflow), or $\bar\rho_N\ge1.15$ (worse than Slater-frame NNBF).
    5. PASS if $\bar\rho_D\le1.10$, $\max_i\rho_{D,i}\le1.35$ and $\bar\rho_N\le1.00$.
    6. AMBIGUOUS otherwise.
* **Futility (Stage 3.5.5)**, run by the orchestrator at steps 300 and 600 with `--mode checkpoint`: KILL-EARLY if, on at least 2 instances, the seed median of $E_{\mathrm{route}}-E_0$ exceeds 1.5 times that of `hfps_dense`. The round-5 NNBF trajectories improve by less than a third of the remaining gap after step 600. Futility can only kill, never promote.
* **Power.** At 1200 steps the round-5 seed CV of the network arms is 4–12% (up to 19% for `sk1` on instance 3). At CV 10% per arm, the log ratio of two seed medians has an sd of about 0.098 per instance and 0.057 pooled. The PASS and KILL bounds on $\bar\rho_D$ (1.10 and 1.30) are 0.167 apart in log, 2.9 sd. At CV 15% this falls to 2.0 sd, still at the Stage 3.5.5 minimum. The AMBIGUOUS band costs nothing extra: its action is the VMC test below, not more seeds.
* **Budget (written exception to the 24 CPU-h cap).** Instance-4 tasks need about 11 CPU-h each. Instance-0 and instance-3 HFPS tasks need about 25–45 CPU-h each (20–29 s per step at 4 threads × 1200 steps, plus about 0.5 h of ED and budget). About 820 CPU-h in total, 24 h wall time at most per task. **Reason no smaller instance suffices:** the 75-step design is invalid (Major 1); instance 4 alone has no reused three-seed references; and instances 0 and 3 carry the three-seed 1200-step `nnbf`, `agpj` and `skagp` results, which saves 18 tasks. The futility checks at steps 300 and 600 cap the loss on a failing route at about half of that.
* **Decision tree (if submitted):**
    * PASS: the size-trend VMC test of Major 4 (route against dense at $6\times6$ and $8\times8$, matched $P$), together with a pre-registered cost re-measurement at $L=24$ and 32. Accuracy parity at 16 sites alone does not close the leaf.
    * KILL by $\rho_D$: local hidden rows lose accuracy against dense rows in the same frame even at 16 sites. 7.1 is KILLED and the class is closed in the pairing frame.
    * KILL by $\rho_F$: the T2 patch gain was early-training speed or seed noise; 7.1 is KILLED.
    * KILL by $\rho_N$: the pairing frame does not survive convergence (the `agpj` pattern); 7.1 is KILLED.
    * AMBIGUOUS: no more seeds; go to the VMC test, with $\rho_D$ as the open item.
    * UNINFORMATIVE: the premise fails at 1200 steps; record it, no rerun.
    * INVALID or INCOMPLETE: fix the named guard, or resubmit the task (it resumes from its checkpoint).
* **Command, HELD by the Round 8c gate (do not submit unless the orchestrator re-scopes 7.1 as an accuracy question):** `cd /home/jek354/research/.claude/worktrees/nqs-brainstorm/brainstorms/nqs-lattice-fermions/tests/P2.1_subcubic_amplitudes_r10 && sbatch submit_t3_hfps.sbatch`. Verdict: `python3 t3_hfps.py --mode aggregate`. Futility: `python3 t3_hfps.py --mode checkpoint --at 300`, then `--at 600`.

### Local Autopsy Log (Round 10 additions)
* **Early-training ratios across frames**: a 75-step $\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}$ ratio between arms that start at different energies (pairing frame $-8.43$ against Slater frame $-8.06$ on instance 4) measures learning speed. `agpj` goes from 0.61 times NNBF at 75 steps to 1.3–1.8 times at 1200. Rule: cross-frame accuracy claims need the converged horizon, or a same-frame foil.
* **Cubic baseline at its best implementation**: a dense pairing state with $K\Omega_f$ precomputed costs one $N\times N$ determinant per ratio and matches the sub-cubic route's sweep time at $L=16$. Rule: a sub-cubic cost claim is scored against the cheapest correct implementation of the cubic baseline in the same frame, not against a Slater-frame NNBF.

* **Orchestrator decision on the 7.1 T3 (2026-11-16): HELD, not submitted.** The Round 8 critic showed that the 75-step T2 measured learning speed. At 1200 steps NNBF reaches $\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}=0.0159$, so 7.1's 0.0126 is about $\rho_N\approx0.80$, not 0.14. Round 8c gave KILL-COST (uncertain) against the dense pairing-frame baseline (ratio $0.99\pm0.56$ at $L=16$), so 7.1 is DOWNGRADED to an accuracy tool with no cost case at $L\le16$. The redesigned T3 needs about 820 CPU-hours (25–45 CPU-hours per task on instances 0 and 3), far outside this brainstorm's low-compute constraint, for a route that no longer carries the leaf's cost claim. It stays prepared in `tests/P2.1_subcubic_amplitudes_r10/` with its rule, for use only if a later result restores a cost case (for example a size where dense HFPS is measurably more expensive per sample).

### Round 11 per-leaf critic: closure (2026-11-16)
* **Inputs read:** the leaf from Round 6 on; `t1_r7.py` (`LHHFPS.ratio`), `accept_update.py` (slaved pairing-frame update), `r8b/cost_per_sample.py`, `r10/cost_8c.py`, `aggregate_8c_output.txt`, `results_8c/*.json`, `r9/aggregate_699528_output.txt`. No job was submitted.
* **Critic check** (`tests/P2.1_subcubic_amplitudes_r11/crossover_model.py`, output `crossover_model_output.txt`; single thread, under one minute). It counts the operations of one VMC sweep with the formulas of the implemented engines, for Route 7.1 (plus patch, $F=4$), dense HFPS ($F=4$) and NNBF of width $H$. Proposal and connection statistics are sampled with the 8b proposal at doping $1/8$, with acceptance $a=0.31$ from the 8c logs. It then compares the operation ratio $R_{\mathrm{ops}}$ with the measured 8c sweep-time ratio. Excerpt:

```
L=16 Ne= 224 props/sweep=   295 conns/Eloc=   419 route=6.75e+08 [accept 0.97, proposals 0.01, Eloc 0.02] dense=7.05e+08 R_ops(dense)=1.04 R_ops(nnbf6)=2.14 R_ops(nnbf32)=2.83 R_ops(nnbf64)=3.68 R_ops(nnbf128)=5.38
L=28 Ne= 686 ... R_ops(dense)=3.09
 dense: L=8: t_sw ratio=0.33 R_ops=0.27 eta=1.22  L=12: t_sw ratio=0.68 R_ops=0.62 eta=1.10  L=16: t_sw ratio=0.90 R_ops=1.04 eta=0.86
 dense: ops -> L=28; measured constants (eta16=0.86) -> L=32
v_h=0.50: L8:0.77 L12:1.73 L16:3.18 L20:4.87 L24:7.03 ...  -> ops crossover L=16
3x3 patch: L16:0.68 L24:1.57 L32:2.85 L40:4.41
```

* **Persistence (mandate item 1):** satisfied. There were eleven rounds and five generation batches, every kill was mutated or replaced, and the portfolio stayed at 3 live routes through Round 10.
#### Major objections
1. **The candidate diagnostic names the wrong bottleneck.** "The determinant work it removes is not the bottleneck" is backwards for the VMC sweep. The local energy that P2.1 made $\mathcal{O}(N_e^2)$ is 2% of the route's sweep operations at $L=16$, and its proposals are another 1%. **97% is the accepted-move Woodbury update.** It acts on the augmented inverse of dimension $n=N+V_h$ ($N=N_e/2$ in the pairing frame, $V_h=V$ hidden rows) and carries $V\times n$ Green's tables, so it costs about $2n^2k+4Vnk+VV_hk$ per accepted move, with $k=b+1\approx9$. At doping $1/8$, $V/N\approx2.3$ and $n/N\approx3.3$, so this constant is about $60kN^2\approx500N^2$ per accepted move, against $\tfrac23N^3$ per ratio for the dense baseline. P2.1 did not fail because determinant work is cheap. It moved the bottleneck from the local energy to the update of an inverse 3.3 times larger than the physical one.
2. **The crossover is set by operation counts, not by implementation, so compiled kernels do not move it much.** The operation model reproduces the measured 8c sweep-time ratio against dense HFPS: the implementation factor $\eta$ ($t_{\mathrm{sw}}$ ratio over $R_{\mathrm{ops}}$) is 1.22, 1.10 and 0.86 at $L=8$, 12 and 16. $R_D$ grows linearly in $N_e$ ($\mathcal{O}(N_e^3)$ against $\mathcal{O}(N_e^4)$ per sweep). In operations it is 1.04 at $L=16$, 2.25 at $L=24$ and 4.05 at $L=32$. **The KEEP threshold $R_D=3$ is first reached at $L\approx28$ ($N_e\approx690$) in operations, and at $L\approx32$ with the measured constants.** The $3\times3$ patch ($b\le14$) pushes it beyond $L=32$ ($R_{\mathrm{ops}}=2.85$ there). An independent count confirms the Round 10 extrapolation ($L\approx29$).
3. **"NNBF's per-step cost is nearly flat in width up to $H\approx48$" holds only for the exact-sum step on instance 4.** That step is dominated by width-independent work (420420 determinants, the $P_\chi$ projection, the SR solve; amendment A1). In VMC at $L=16$, NNBF's sweep time is roughly linear in $H$: 0.54 s, 2.17 s, 4.58 s and 9.74 s at $H=6$, 32, 64 and 128 (8c). There the route is 2.8–5.4 times cheaper than NNBF at $H=32$ to 128 in operations, and 5–23 times cheaper in measured sweep time. The candidate statement merges the two regimes and must not be used. The correct split is:
    * against same-frame cubic baselines (dense HFPS, the scored foil under the Round 10 autopsy rule), there is no cost case below $L\approx28$;
    * against wide Slater-frame NNBF, a cost case exists at $L=16$ already. It is worth something only if NNBF needs $H\gtrsim64$ to match the route's accuracy at $L=16$, which nothing at exact-sum size can measure (Major 4).
4. **No accuracy comparison at exact-sum size decides anything, in either direction.** The route PROMOTEs (7.3, 6.1″) are 75-step learning-speed ratios at matched $P$ on a 15-site cluster, where the $3\times3$ patch sees 40% of the sites. The reported-only `nnbf_w` win reverses them. But `nnbf_w` has $P=10488$ against $d_{S\chi}=15080$ ($P/d_{S\chi}=0.70$), the regime where rounds 3 and 5 found projected NNBF essentially exact. It is a sector-fitting artefact just as the route wins are early-training artefacts. The leaf's accuracy question can only be settled where $P\ll d$ and the patch is small compared with the cluster: VMC at $L\ge16$, with no exact reference.
5. **The regime where the cost class pays is beyond cheap verification.** At $L=28$ the route's sweep is about $2\times10^{10}$ operations, about 12 s at the measured rate (0.42 s at $L=16$ times an operation ratio of 29). A 1200-step SR run with 2000 samples per step then costs about $3\times10^7$ CPU-s, about $8\times10^3$ CPU-h per arm, with no exact reference and with variance extrapolation as the only accuracy measure. That is two orders of magnitude above the brainstorm's low-compute constraint, and above the held 820 CPU-h T3.
#### Minor objections
1. `cost_8c.py --mode aggregate` prints "-> KILL-COST" for NNBF at $H=64$ and 128, where $\tau_{\mathrm{int}}$ is NaN. The leaf correctly records "$\tau$ incomplete", but the printed verdict contradicts it. This repeats the round-6 Minor 3 defect (`backing-claims`): a NaN must print INCOMPLETE.
2. Round 8 Gate 0 is still PARTIAL. SCALE (arXiv:2604.25775) reports $\mathcal{O}(N^3)$ per sample for local backflow with low-rank updates, the same class as our $\mathcal{O}(N_e^3)$ per sweep. Its full text was never checked. The closure statement therefore claims only the measured crossover and the bottleneck lemma, not a new cost class.
3. The 8b and 8c timings and this operation model use untrained parameters, with $\tau_{\mathrm{int}}\approx1$ sweep for every arm. If a trained route has a longer $\tau$, the crossover moves further out, never closer.
4. Amendment A1 for 6.1″ matched compute by operations per amplitude ($H_c=16$). The route's measured step was 4 times slower than that foil's, so A1 flattered the route. This does not change the verdict. Any later rule should match the scored foil in measured time per step *and* in steps to convergence, not in operations per amplitude.
#### Rulings on the 75-step PROMOTEs (question 3)
* **The verdicts stand as T2 verdicts.** Both rules were pre-registered and applied unchanged: 7.3 has $\rho_N=0.594$ and $\rho_F=0.798$; 6.1″ has $\rho_N=0.282$, $\rho_C=0.589$ and $\rho_F=0.544$. Both are recorded as "T2 PROMOTE (learning speed at matched $P$)". Neither is capacity evidence, and neither carries forward to T3; the 6.1′ rule itself said so ("A PROMOTE is not capacity evidence").
* **The T2 rule is defective as a promotion gate for this leaf, but not as a kill gate.** Its kills are sound: a route that is worse at 75 steps, or no better than its own foil, has no claim. Its PROMOTE branch tests a quantity the leaf does not claim (accuracy at matched parameters after 25% of training) instead of the leaf's claim (equal accuracy at lower cost per sample). Three defects:
    * the horizon measures learning speed (Round 8 critic: NNBF goes from 0.088 at 75 steps to 0.0159 at 1200);
    * matched $P$ is the wrong axis when the baseline's cost does not grow with width;
    * the instance cannot separate local from global features (Major 4).
* **Process anti-pattern for the master: "parameter-matched comparisons at fixed short step counts".** A ratio of errors at matched $P$ after a fixed early step count measures learning speed and parameter efficiency, not the claim of a cost-reduction leaf. **Rule:** a cost-reduction leaf scores its foil at matched cost per decorrelated sample, in the deployment regime (VMC, not the exact-sum step), against the cheapest correct implementation of the cubic baseline in the same frame. Accuracy ratios are taken at the converged horizon, or against the foil's converged value. Short-horizon ratios may kill, never promote. This extends the Round 10 autopsy rule ("early-training ratios across frames") to same-frame comparisons.
#### Per-route verdicts
* **6.1″ LH-HFDS, $3\times3$ patch: CLOSED (no cost case).** The T2 PROMOTE stands as learning speed only. On instance 4 the route is dominated at equal wall time by `nnbf_w`, which is itself a sector-fitting result (Major 4). Its operation crossover against a same-frame dense baseline lies beyond $L=32$ ($b\le14$).
* **7.3 SH-HFDS: CLOSED (DOWNGRADE stands).** The T2 PROMOTE stands as learning speed only, and the 8b DOWNGRADE (uncertain) stands. It uses the same augmented-matrix update as 7.1, with $n=N_\sigma+V$ per spin, so the bottleneck lemma (Major 1) applies with a larger constant. No dense-baseline cost gate was run, and none is needed.
* **7.1 local-feature HFPS: CLOSED (DOWNGRADED; T3 stays HELD).** The T3 package remains in `tests/P2.1_subcubic_amplitudes_r10/` for a pairing-frame accuracy question. That question belongs to P1 and P4, not to P2.1.
* **No refill.** The one mutation the bottleneck lemma suggests is recorded below as the reopening condition, not as a live route. Even a cost PASS would leave its accuracy question in the regime of Major 5.
#### Answers to the closure questions
* **Question 1 (close?): YES, P2.1 closes with a refined Saturation Diagnostic.** This is not surrender after a failed batch. The diagnostic is a mechanism-level cost lemma confirmed against measurement ($\eta=0.86$–1.22), it states the regime where the opposite holds, and it names a falsifier.
* **Question 2 (if not closing): moot.** For the record, the two candidate tests were weighed.
    * **1200-step equal-wall-time run on instance 4: rejected.** It costs about 27 CPU-h, above the 2 CPU-h T2 cap. Both outcomes are uninformative (Major 4), and exact-sum wall time is not deployment cost (Major 3).
    * **Compiled-kernel cost crossover at larger $L$: cheap and well posed.** It is the falsifier below. It can move the crossover number, but it cannot decide accuracy, so it would reopen the leaf rather than continue it.
#### Saturation Diagnostic (P2.1 closes, 2026-11-16)
* **Statement.** An exact $\mathcal{O}(N_e^2)$ local energy is achievable and verified for determinant and pairing NQS with patch-local hidden rows and a learned linear long-range coupling. The ratios are exact to $10^{-14}$, the accepted-move update is $\mathcal{O}(N_e^2)$, and the drift is at most $6\times10^{-11}$ over $10^4$ moves with no refresh, so a sweep is $\mathcal{O}(N_e^3)$, one power below dense backflow. The saving does not pay at accessible sizes, for a structural reason. Once the local energy is sub-cubic, the Woodbury update of the augmented inverse dominates the sweep (97% at $L=16$). That inverse has dimension $N+V_h$, with one hidden row per site. With $V_h=V$ at doping $1/8$ the update constant is about $60kN^2$, against $\tfrac23N^3$ per ratio for the dense same-frame baseline. The two cross ($R_D=3$) only at $L\approx28$ ($N_e\approx690$) in operations, and at $L\approx32$ with measured constants. That is far beyond exact references, and costs about $10^4$ CPU-h per trained arm. Accuracy comparisons at exact-sum size cannot substitute: a patch covers 40% of the cluster, and any baseline wide enough to compete in wall time has $P\approx d_{S\chi}$.
* **Minimal extra structure that would bring the crossover into reach: fewer hidden rows than sites.** With hidden rows on a sublattice of density $v_h=V_h/V=1/2$ ($b$ scaled with it), the operation crossover against dense HFPS moves to $L=16$ ($R_{\mathrm{ops}}=3.18$). With $v_h=1/4$ it moves to $L=12$ (4.46). Gate 4 for this mutation is open. Size consistency survives, because the sublattice rows are block-local. Two things are unknown: whether $V_h<N_e$ hidden rows keep the sign capacity of B7.1, and whether the accuracy holds where the patch is small relative to the cluster.
* **Regime where the opposite would hold.** At $L=16$ the route is already 3.7 times cheaper in operations than Slater-frame NNBF of width $H\gtrsim64$, and 10.8 times cheaper in measured sweep time. It pays there if, and only if, NNBF needs that width to match the route's accuracy at $L=16$. That is a VMC accuracy question with no exact reference.
* **Falsifying experiments.**
    * **Cost (cheap, T1).** Implement BLAS-3 delayed updates for both the route and dense HFPS, and measure $R_D(L)$ at $L=16$, 24 and 32 with the 8c metric. The diagnostic predicts $R_D(16)<3$ and $R_D(24)\approx2$. It is falsified if $R_D(16)\ge3$ or $R_D(24)\ge4.5$; the update constant would then be an implementation artefact.
    * **Structure (T0, then T1).** Take the $v_h=1/2$ sublattice route through Gate 4. If its 8c cost gives $R_D(16)\ge3$, P2.1 reopens with that route only. Its accuracy test must be VMC at $L\ge16$, against dense HFPS at matched cost per decorrelated sample.
* **Recommended practice.** For local-backflow determinant and Pfaffian states at $L\le24$, use the dense same-frame form with $K\Omega_f$ precomputed: it costs the same per sample and is simpler. Keep the carried-table route for $L\gtrsim30$, or for fewer than one hidden row per site.

### Local Autopsy Log (Round 11 additions)
* **Sub-cubic local energy, cubic-constant update:** making $E_{\mathrm{loc}}$ $\mathcal{O}(N_e^2)$ through an augmented matrix moves the cost into the accepted-move update of an inverse of size $N+V_h$. With $V_h=V$ that update is 97% of the sweep, and it cancels the class gain until $L\approx28$. Rule: before any accuracy tier, cost the full sweep (update included) against the same-frame dense baseline, and count the hidden rows as part of the inverse.
* **Parameter-matched comparisons at fixed short step counts** (process anti-pattern; see the rulings above): in a cost-reduction leaf they may kill, never promote.
### Round 11b: sublattice-hidden-row falsifier (2026-11-16)
The second falsifier of the Saturation Diagnostic ("Structure"), run as a cost check only. No accuracy is tested here.
#### Pre-registration (written 2026-11-16, before any 11b code was run)
* **Route variants.** Same pairing frame and engine as 7.1 (`accept_update.HFPSAcc`, slaved stage-2 form, plus patch, $F=4$), but hidden rows sit only on a sublattice $S_h$: `half` = checkerboard sites ($x+y$ even, $v_h=1/2$), `quarter` = sites with $x$ and $y$ both even ($v_h=1/4$). $K$ is $V\times V_h$ and $\Omega$ is $V_h\times F\times V$; the augmented inverse has dimension $n=N+V_h$, and a move changes only the hidden rows in $S_h$ whose patch contains a touched site.
* **Correctness gates (T0), both variants.** (i) The augmented-determinant amplitude equals an explicit $\det\big(F_0+K\,C(\mathbf n)\big)[\mathrm{up},\mathrm{dn}]$ built site by site, to relative error at most $10^{-12}$; the variant with $S_h$ = all sites reproduces `t1_r7.LHHFPS` at the same seed. (ii) Fast $E_{\mathrm{loc}}$ equals the brute-force $E_{\mathrm{loc}}$ (sorted rows, explicit Jordan–Wigner signs, explicit amplitude) to at most $10^{-12}$ relative, on $5\times3$ OBC and on $6\times6$ and $8\times8$ PBC. (iii) Accepted-move drift at most $10^{-8}$ after $10^4$ accepted moves with no refresh on the same three geometries. A failure is a BUG, not a verdict, and no cost is measured until it is fixed.
* **Cost metric:** the 8b and 8c metric unchanged, $c=t_{\mathrm{sw}}\,\tau_{\mathrm{int}}$, with the 8b proposal, sweep and Sokal $\tau_{\mathrm{int}}$ imported read-only. $L=8$, 12 and 16 PBC at doping $1/8$, untrained parameters. Arms: `half`, `quarter`, `dense` (dense HFPS $F=4$, `cost_8c.DenseHFPS`, re-measured under the current load), NNBF at $H=6$ and $H=32$. CPU budgets per size: 15, 25 and 120 s for the two variants and `dense`; 10, 20 and 50 s for each NNBF. One single-threaded process per arm, at most 4 at a time, `ulimit -t 200`. Total about 11 CPU-minutes, plus about 3 for the gates.
* **Scored:** $R_D(L)=c_{\mathrm{dense}}/c_{\mathrm{variant}}$ with its $1\sigma$ error from the two $\tau$ errors.
* **Rule.** REOPEN P2.1 with this route only if, for `half` or `quarter`, $R_D(16)-\sigma\ge3$ (at least 3, resolved at $1\sigma$). Otherwise the Saturation Diagnostic stands and is strengthened: removing hidden rows does not bring the crossover to $L=16$. If $R_D(16)\ge3$ only at its central value, the verdict is "not resolved", and the Diagnostic stands. A missing or NaN $\tau$ at $L=16$ prints INCOMPLETE, never a verdict. The NNBF ratios $R_N$ are reported, not scored.
* **If REOPEN:** the next test is accuracy, not more cost. It asks whether $V_h<N_e$ hidden rows keep the block-parity sign capacity (B7.1). The test is VMC at $L\ge16$ of the reopening variant against dense HFPS at matched cost per decorrelated sample, with converged horizons. An exact-sum toy check of sign capacity at fixed $F$ comes first, as a kill-only gate.
#### Round 11b result
Code: `tests/P2.1_subcubic_amplitudes_r11b/sub_hfps.py` (subclass of `accept_update.HFPSAcc`, imported read-only; the 8b sweep, proposal and $\tau_{\mathrm{int}}$ and `cost_8c.DenseHFPS` are imported unchanged). Outputs: `check_output.txt`, `run_*.log`, `aggregate_output.txt`, `ops_recount_output.txt`. Single-threaded, at most 4 processes, about 12 CPU-minutes in total (gates 1.0, cost arms about 10.7, recount 0.1). Node load about 33 on 20 cores. Untrained parameters, as in 8b and 8c.
* **Gates (T0): PASS.** The amplitude equals the explicit $\det(F_0+KC)$ to at most $8\times10^{-14}$, and the all-sites variant reproduces `LHHFPS` exactly (0.0). Fast against brute $E_{\mathrm{loc}}$ agrees to at most $1.4\times10^{-14}$. Drift after $10^4$ accepted moves with no refresh is at most $3.0\times10^{-9}$ (quarter on $6\times6$), with every other chain at or below $2\times10^{-11}$. The plus patch covers all sites for `half`, and 75% of them for `quarter` (the odd-odd sites lie in no patch).

| 7.1, $v_h=1$ (8c, other load) | 256 | 0.036; 0.116; 0.424 | $1.36\pm0.55$ | 0.39; 1.22; 0.99 | 0.90 | reported only |

* **Scored verdict: the Saturation Diagnostic stands.** Neither variant comes near the REOPEN bar at $L=16$ ($R_D-\sigma\ge3$). Even the central values (1.00 and 1.40) are below 3, and the low-noise sweep-time ratios are only 1.48 and 1.68.
* **NNBF (reported only):** at $L=16$, $R_N$ at $H=6$ is $0.93\pm0.60$ (`half`) and $1.31\pm0.81$ (`quarter`). At $H=32$ the $L=16$ $\tau$ is NaN (30 sweeps), so INCOMPLETE; the sweep-time ratios are 6.7 and 7.6.
* **Why the predicted gain did not appear.** Two separate causes:
    * **Op model.** The Round 11 model counts the $X$-table update as $VV_hk$. In the engine $X$ is $V\times V$ for any $V_h$, so the term is $2V^2k$. Re-counted (`ops_recount.py`), $R_{\mathrm{ops}}(16)$ falls from 3.20 to 2.62 for $v_h=1/2$, and from 7.42 to 5.34 for $v_h=1/4$. For `half` the op count alone is already below 3.
    * **Fixed per-move overhead.** The measured sweep-time ratio is 0.57 of the op ratio for `half` and 0.31 for `quarter` (against $\eta=0.86$ for $v_h=1$ in Round 11). Shrinking $n$ removes BLAS work but not the per-move Python and small-matrix overhead, which then dominates. At $L=12$ both variants have the same sweep time (0.09 s).
* **Strengthened, with one stated limit.** For `half` the Diagnostic is strengthened outright: the measured cost and the corrected op count both give $R_D(16)<3$. For `quarter` the measured $R_D(16)=1.4$ rests partly on interpreter overhead, because the corrected op count is 5.3. A compiled engine with a lower per-move constant could therefore cross $R_D=3$ at $L=16$ for $v_h=1/4$. That would also need the $V\times V$ $X$ table replaced by on-demand $\mathcal{O}(n)$ entries. This is the same "implementation, not class" question as the first falsifier (BLAS-3 delayed updates), and it is recorded there rather than as a reopening. It also only matters if $v_h=1/4$ with 25% of sites unseen keeps the sign capacity, which is untested.
* **No accuracy test follows** (no REOPEN). If a compiled `quarter` engine ever gives $R_D(16)-\sigma\ge3$, the pre-registered next step above applies unchanged: an exact-sum sign-capacity check as a kill-only gate, then VMC at $L\ge16$ against dense HFPS at matched cost per decorrelated sample.
* **Autopsy addition:** an op-count lever that shrinks the matrix dimension also shrinks the regime where BLAS dominates. Rule: when a mutation is justified by an op-count ratio, re-derive every table in the update from the code (not the model), and measure $\eta$ for the mutated engine, not the parent.
### Round 11c: compiled delayed-update falsifier (2026-11-16)
The first falsifier of the Saturation Diagnostic ("Cost", Round 11), run as a cost check only. No accuracy is tested here.
#### Pre-registration (written 2026-11-16, before any 11c code was run)
* **Engines.** New module `tests/P2.1_subcubic_amplitudes_r11c/delayed.py` (numba); every earlier module is imported read-only. Parameters are taken from the existing Python objects at the same seeds (`sub_hfps.SubHFPS` for `full`, `half` and `quarter`; `cost_8c.DenseHFPS` for `dense`), so each compiled arm samples the same wave function as in 8c and 11b. The whole sweep (proposal, ratio, accepted-move update, local energy) is compiled.
    * **Route arms** (7.1 on all sites = `full`, and the `half` and `quarter` sublattice variants): carried inverse only, with delayed Sherman–Morrison–Woodbury updates. A move replaces the rows $R$ (up row and $b$ hidden rows) and, for a down move, column $j$: an update of rank $k'=|R|+1$. The inverse is held as $M^{-1}=M_0^{-1}-P^{\top}Z$ with $P$ and $Z$ of rank $r$. A ratio reads the needed columns of $M^{-1}$ in $\mathcal{O}(nrk)$ and, for a down move, one product $M_0^{-1}\tilde c$ in $\mathcal{O}(n^2)$. An accepted move appends rank $k'$; when $r$ would exceed $r_{\max}$, the pending rank is flushed with one GEMM of shape $n\times r\times n$. The Green's tables $G_r$, $G_c$ and $X$ of the Python engine are dropped: they make a ratio $\mathcal{O}(nk)$ but cost $(4Vn+2V^2)k$ per accepted move, more than they save. This is the cheapest correct form of the route we know, so the falsifier gives the route its best case.
    * **Dense HFPS** ($F=4$): every entry depends on the global features, so there is no low-rank update; a ratio is the $FN^2$ gather plus one LAPACK LU, compiled.
    * $r_{\max}$ is chosen per route arm from 0, 16, 32 and 64 (0 = flush at every accepted move) as the smallest $t_{\mathrm{sw}}$ in a 10-sweep scan at $L=16$, before the scored runs.
* **Gates (T0)**, every route arm, on $5\times3$ OBC and on $6\times6$ and $8\times8$ PBC, at $r_{\max}=32$: (i) after random accepted moves, every connection ratio of the compiled engine equals the brute-force ratio (explicit $\det(F_0+KC)$ on sorted rows with Jordan–Wigner signs, `SubHFPS.amp_explicit`) to relative error at most $10^{-12}$, and the compiled $E_{\mathrm{loc}}$ equals `t1_r7.eloc_brute` to at most $10^{-12}$ relative; (ii) drift at most $10^{-8}$ after $10^4$ accepted moves with no refresh (normalised max error of $M^{-1}$ and $C$ against a rebuild, and of $E_{\mathrm{loc}}$). Dense: the compiled $E_{\mathrm{loc}}$ equals `eloc_brute` on `DenseHFPS.amp_sorted` to at most $10^{-12}$, and its carried log-determinant drifts by at most $10^{-8}$ over $10^4$ accepted moves. A failure is a BUG, and no cost is measured until it is fixed.
* **Metric:** unchanged from 8c and 11b: $c=t_{\mathrm{sw}}\,\tau_{\mathrm{int}}$, the 8b proposal and sweep of $4V$ attempts, the Sokal $\tau_{\mathrm{int}}$ of `cost_per_sample.tau_int`, untrained parameters, doping $1/8$. CPU budgets per arm: 10, 20 and 90 s at $L=8$, 12 and 16. $L=24$ is timed for $t_{\mathrm{sw}}$ only (at least 3 sweeps), because $\tau_{\mathrm{int}}$ needs more sweeps than the budget allows there.
* **Scored:** $R_D(L)=c_{\mathrm{dense}}/c_{\mathrm{arm}}$, with $\sigma$ from the two $\tau$ errors in quadrature.
* **Rule.** FALSIFIED (P2.1 reopens with that variant only, and the next test is accuracy) if, for any route arm, $R_D(16)-\sigma\ge3$ or $R_D(24)-\sigma\ge4.5$. Otherwise the Diagnostic stands, now with compiled updates. A NaN $\tau$ at $L=16$ prints INCOMPLETE. Since $\tau$ is not measured at $L=24$, the $L=24$ clause is read on the sweep-time ratio: if that ratio is at least 4.5 for some arm, the verdict is "$L=24$ unresolved" (a $\tau$ run at $L=24$ is then required), never FALSIFIED by itself.
* **Budget:** at most 4 single-threaded processes at a time (`OMP`, `OPENBLAS` and `NUMBA` threads set to 1), `ulimit -t` per process, and at most 30 CPU-minutes for the whole round, gates and compilation included.
#### Amendment A1 to gate (i) (written 2026-11-16, after the first gate run and before any cost measurement)
* **What failed.** The first gate run (`check_output_v1.txt`) met the per-ratio relative bound of $10^{-12}$ on $5\times3$ OBC for every arm and on all three geometries for `dense`, but not on $6\times6$ (`full` $1.7\times10^{-11}$, `half` $3.5\times10^{-11}$, `quarter` $1.8\times10^{-12}$) or on $8\times8$ (up to $2.8\times10^{-10}$). Everything else passed: the compiled $E_{\mathrm{loc}}$ matches `eloc_brute` to at most $9.6\times10^{-14}$, and the drift after $10^4$ accepted moves is at most $9.6\times10^{-13}$.
* **Diagnosis** (`diag_ratio.py`, `diag_ratio_output.txt`). The worst relative errors sit on small ratios ($\lvert\rho\rvert\sim10^{-4}$ to $10^{-2}$). The absolute error, normalised by the largest $\lvert\rho\rvert$ of the configuration, is at most $2.8\times10^{-13}$. A 50-digit determinant shows that the float64 brute force is the more accurate of the two, so the error belongs to the carried inverse. The same error measure for an exact fresh inverse and for the round-7 Python engine (`SubHFPS.ratio`) is about $10^{-14}$ normalised, but up to $3.8\times10^{-12}$ and $8.4\times10^{-12}$ per-ratio relative. **So no updated-inverse ratio engine in this leaf meets a per-ratio relative bound of $10^{-12}$ on small ratios, the exact one included.** The bound was mis-posed: Round 11b gated amplitudes, not ratios.
* **Amended gate (i):** per configuration, $\max_c\lvert\rho_c^{\mathrm{fast}}-\rho_c^{\mathrm{ref}}\rvert/\max_c\lvert\rho_c^{\mathrm{ref}}\rvert\le10^{-12}$. The $E_{\mathrm{loc}}$ bound ($10^{-12}$ relative) and gate (ii) are unchanged. The per-ratio relative error is still printed and reported. The cost rule is unchanged. No cost number had been measured when this was written.
#### Round 11c result
Code: `tests/P2.1_subcubic_amplitudes_r11c/delayed.py` (modes `check`, `scan`, `run` and `aggregate`), `run_all.sh`, and the diagnostics `diag_ratio.py`, `bench_dense.py` and `bench_blas.py`. Outputs: `check_output_v1.txt`, `check_output.txt`, `diag_ratio_output.txt`, `scan_output.txt`, `run_*.log`, `aggregate_output.txt`, `bench_dense_output.txt` and `bench_blas_output.txt`. Single-threaded, at most 4 processes at a time, about 16 CPU-minutes in total (gates and diagnostics 2.2, scan 0.1, the four scored arms 10.8, `dense2` 2.7). Node load 37 to 44 on 20 cores. Untrained parameters, as in 8b, 8c and 11b.
* **Gates (T0): PASS under A1.** Normalised ratio error at most $2.8\times10^{-13}$; per-ratio relative error up to $2.8\times10^{-10}$ on small ratios (reported, see A1). $E_{\mathrm{loc}}$ against `eloc_brute` at most $9.6\times10^{-14}$ relative. Drift after $10^4$ accepted moves with no refresh at most $9.6\times10^{-13}$ ($8\times8$ `full`). Both dense arms pass every gate at $3.8\times10^{-13}$ or better.
* **$r_{\max}$ scan** (`scan_output.txt`, $L=16$): $t_{\mathrm{sw}}$ varies by at most 20% across $r_{\max}=0$, 16, 32 and 64 for every arm. Chosen by the rule: 32 for `full`, 16 for `half` and `quarter`. **The delay depth is not the lever.** A move already has rank $k'\approx b+2$ (about 10 for `full`), so the immediate update is already a GEMM.
* **Post-hoc strengthening of the baseline (`dense2`).** The first dense ratio cost 1.9 bare LUs (`bench_dense_output.txt`). `dense2` carries the gathered blocks $F_0[\mathrm{up},\mathrm{dn}]$ and $(K\Omega_f)[\mathrm{up},\mathrm{dn}]$ in slot order, patching them in $\mathcal{O}(NF)$ per accepted move. It is the same wave function, and it passes the same gates. It is 4% to 15% cheaper per sweep (up to 35% at $L=24$, where it ran alone on the node), and it still costs 1.7 to 1.9 bare LUs per ratio: the $FN^2$ build is part of the dense ratio. The verdict is scored against `dense2`, the cheaper baseline, which can only make falsification harder. Numba's LU is 1.1 times numpy's (`bench_blas_output.txt`), so the dense arm has no library handicap.

| arm | $V_h$ at $L=16$ | $r_{\max}$ | $t_{\mathrm{sw}}$ (s) at $L=8$, 12, 16, 24 | $\tau_{\mathrm{int}}$ at $L=16$ | $R_D$ vs `dense2` at $L=8$, 12, 16 | $R_D(16)$ vs `dense` | $t_{\mathrm{sw}}$ ratio vs `dense2` at $L=16$, 24 | verdict |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| `dense` (pre-registered baseline) | — | — | 0.0027; 0.026; 0.147; 2.73 | $1.66\pm0.46$ | — | — | — | reference |
| `dense2` (scored baseline) | — | — | 0.0025; 0.024; 0.124; 1.77 | $1.30\pm0.30$ | — | — | — | reference |
| `full` (7.1, $v_h=1$) | 256 | 32 | 0.0035; 0.017; 0.080; 0.78 | $1.14\pm0.21$ | $0.57\pm0.08$; $2.04\pm0.62$; $1.78\pm0.52$ | $2.70\pm0.89$ | 1.56; 2.26 | below 3 |
| `half` ($v_h=1/2$) | 128 | 16 | 0.0018; 0.0072; 0.027; 0.32 | $1.19\pm0.13$ | $1.42\pm0.17$; $5.33\pm1.43$; $5.05\pm1.27$ | $7.68\pm2.27$ | 4.65; 5.54 | **FALSIFIES** ($R_D-\sigma=3.78$) |
| `quarter` ($v_h=1/4$) | 64 | 16 | 0.0013; 0.0045; 0.012; 0.12 | $1.27\pm0.09$ | $1.77\pm0.20$; $8.02\pm2.09$; $10.26\pm2.45$ | $15.6\pm4.4$ | 10.0; 15.0 | **FALSIFIES** ($R_D-\sigma=7.80$) |

* **Scored verdict: the Saturation Diagnostic is FALSIFIED** by `half` and `quarter`, against both dense baselines. $R_D(16)-\sigma$ is 3.78 and 7.80 against `dense2` (5.41 and 11.1 against `dense`). The $L=24$ clause is moot; $\tau$ was not measured there, and the sweep-time ratios are 5.5 and 15. For `full` ($v_h=1$) the Diagnostic's numbers survive: $R_D(16)=1.78\pm0.52$, and the $L=24$ sweep-time ratio is 2.3, close to the predicted $R_D(24)\approx2$.
* **What was wrong in the Diagnostic.** Its update constant ($2n^2k+4Vnk+2V^2k$ per accepted move, 97% of the sweep) counted the Green's tables $G_r$ ($V\times n$), $G_c$ ($n\times V$) and $X$ ($V\times V$). The sweep does not need them. Carrying only $M^{-1}$ costs about $2n^2k$ multiply-adds per accepted move, plus one $n^2$ product for each down-move ratio. That keeps the $\mathcal{O}(N_e^3)$ class per sweep and drops the $V$-sized terms. At $L=16$ the table terms are 1.9, 3.3 and 5.0 times the $2n^2k$ term for `full`, `half` and `quarter` (arithmetic from the formulas). This is why shrinking $V_h$ pays only once the tables are gone: the $V\times V$ $X$ table that the 11b recount found does not shrink with $V_h$. Compiling removes the per-move interpreter overhead that 11b identified for `quarter`. The route's GEMMs run at 20 to 50 GFLOP/s, against 8 to 15 for the dense LU at $N=112$ and 252 (`bench_blas_output.txt`). That efficiency gap is real hardware behaviour, but it is about a factor 2 of the measured $R_D$, so the operation ratio alone is roughly half of it.
* **Measured against the Python engines:** compiling sped up the sweep at $L=16$ by 9.5 times for `half`, 18 times for `quarter` and 5.3 times for `full`, but only by 2.6 to 3.0 times for dense, which was already LAPACK-bound.
* **Limits:**
    * The parameters are untrained, and every $\tau_{\mathrm{int}}$ is between 1 and 1.8 sweeps.
    * $\tau$ was not measured at $L=24$.
    * The node was heavily loaded. `dense2` ran alone, which favours the baseline.
    * $R_D$ compares cost per decorrelated sample of different wave functions. **Nothing here says whether $V_h<N_e$ hidden rows keep the sign capacity.** `quarter` leaves 25% of the sites in no patch.
* **Consequence (pre-registered): P2.1 reopens with the sublattice-hidden-row route only (`half` and `quarter`), and the next test is accuracy, not cost.** The Round 11b next step applies unchanged: first an exact-sum sign-capacity check at fixed $F$ (kill-only), then VMC at $L\ge16$ against dense HFPS at matched cost per decorrelated sample, with converged horizons. Any later cost comparison uses the carried-inverse engine of `delayed.py`, not the Green's-table engine. Route 7.1 itself (`full`) stays CLOSED. The status change in the portfolio table is left to the orchestrator.
* **Autopsy addition:** a cost lemma derived from one implementation's tables is a statement about that implementation. Rule: before closing on a cost bottleneck, list which carried tables the sweep strictly needs, and cost the minimal set.
### Round 12: sublattice accuracy test (2026-11-16)
The accuracy (sign-capacity) test of the reopened route, the sublattice-hidden-row variants `half` ($v_h\approx1/2$) and `quarter` ($v_h\approx1/4$) of Route 7.1. T1 is kill-only. A T2 is designed only for a variant that passes T1.
#### T1 rule (pre-registered 2026-11-16, before any scored run)
* **Code** `tests/P2.1_subcubic_amplitudes_r12/t1_sub.py` (imports `t2_routes`, `lh_train`, `t3_hfps` and the round-5 core read-only). The variants are `HFPSAnsatz` (plus patch) with $K[:,m]=0$ and $\Omega_m=0$ for the sites $m$ outside the hidden sublattice. This is exactly the 11b and 11c sublattice ansatz $\psi=e^J\det\big(F_0+\sum_{m\in S_h}K[:,m]\,h_m\Omega_m\big)[\mathrm{up},\mathrm{dn}]$. The masked entries receive an identically zero force and log-derivative, so SR never moves them.
* **Checks already run** (`check_output.txt`, `synthetic_output.txt`; about 2 CPU-seconds). The masked all-sites arm equals `HFPSAnsatz` bit for bit. `half` and `quarter` amplitudes equal an explicit site-by-site build to $1.4\times10^{-15}$. The projected analytic gradient matches finite differences to $7\times10^{-11}$. Force and log-derivatives on masked entries are exactly 0. All 9 synthetic cases return their intended verdict.
* **Arms** at fixed $F=4$ with identical non-backflow parts (pairing reference $F_0$ = round-5 projected d-wave BCS, two-body density Jastrow, no tanh-Jastrow unit): `full` (7.1, one hidden row per site), `half` (checkerboard), `quarter` ($x$ and $y$ even), and `dense` (dense HFPS, `t3_hfps.DenseHFPSAnsatz`, reported only). Fixed $F$, not matched $P$: the question is whether removing hidden rows removes sign capacity.
* **Toy sizes** ($t$–$t'$–$J$, $t'=-0.25$, $J=0.4$, OBC, singlet ground state, distinct $V$; ED gaps from `check_output.txt`):

| key | cluster | $V$ | $D$ | $E_1-E_0$ | $V_h$ (`half`, `quarter`) | $d_{S\chi}$ |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| 20 | $4\times2$, 3+3 | 8 | 560 | 0.066 | 4, 2 | 44 |
| 21 | $3\times3$, 3+3 | 9 | 1680 | 0.145 | 5, 4 | 60 |
| 22 | $5\times2$, 2+2 | 10 | 1260 | 0.387 | 5, 3 | 118 |
| 23 | $4\times3$, 5+5 | 12 | 16632 | 0.085 | 6, 4 | 724 |

* Excluded: $5\times2$ 4+4 ($E_1-E_0=0.0041t$, the P1.2 near-degenerate case) and $5\times2$ 3+3 (triplet ground state). The doping differs between sizes; this confounds the trend clause and is stated as a limit.
* **Protocol.** Exact-force SR (lr 0.05, shift $10^{-3}$, 1000 samples for the metric), **without symmetry projection**. The projected sectors have $d_{S\chi}=44$ to 724, below the parameter counts (464 to 986 for `full`), which is the sector-fitting regime of the cross-node anti-pattern. Capacity is a property of the unprojected family, and projection would act on every arm alike. Training runs to a plateau: from step 300 on, checked every 50 steps, it stops when the best energy improved by less than $2\times10^{-4}t$ over the last 200 steps. One seed (11). Then one budget (`p11_core.budget`) of the trained state, scored on $\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}$.
* **Counted size**: $E_1-E_0\ge0.02t$; calibration and multi-start spread guards of the earlier rules; `full` and the variant both converged (plateau reached); premise $\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}(\texttt{full})\ge10^{-3}t$ (otherwise the size cannot see sign capacity).
* **Scored**: $\rho=\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}(\text{variant})/\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}(\texttt{full})$ per counted size.
    * **KILL** the variant if $\rho>1.25$ at 2 or more counted sizes, or if $\rho$ increases strictly with $V$ over at least 3 counted sizes and $\rho$ at the largest counted $V$ exceeds 1.15.
    * **PASS-TO-T2** if no kill clause fires on at least 3 counted sizes. This is not a promotion. It only licenses the T2 below.
    * **INCONCLUSIVE** if fewer than 3 sizes count. Next action then: no T2 for that variant from this T1; the accuracy question moves to the VMC test at $L\ge16$ that 11b named, and the leaf records why the toys cannot decide it.
    * Single seed: a KILL whose triggering ratios are all below 1.5 is recorded as "KILL (marginal, single seed)" and is put to the per-leaf critic before it is treated as final.
* **Caps** (enforced): single-threaded processes (`OMP`, `OPENBLAS` and `MKL` threads 1), at most 4 at a time, `ulimit -t 600` each. SR loop capped at 90 CPU-s on sizes 20 to 22 and 200 CPU-s on size 23; a run that hits the cap is unconverged. The scored arms run first. `dense` runs last and only within the remaining part of the 30 CPU-minute total (size 23 first). The probe before this rule (timing only, 50 steps of `full` and `dense`, about 0.6 CPU-minutes) is counted in the total.
#### Amendment A1 to the T1 caps (written 2026-11-16, after the scored runs and before any rerun)
* **What happened.** Every variant run except `quarter` on size 23 reached the plateau. The `full` arm hit the SR cap on size 21 (1579 steps at 90 CPU-s) and on size 23 (757 steps at 200 CPU-s), so by rule neither size counts. `aggregate_scored_output.txt` therefore prints INCONCLUSIVE for both variants, with one counted size (20).
* **Amendment.** Rerun `full` only, on sizes 21 and 23, from the same seed (the SR trajectory is deterministic, so the rerun extends the capped run), with SR caps of 200 and 400 CPU-s. Thresholds, guards and the counted-size definition are unchanged. The previous `full` results are kept as `results/capped/`. The amendment only completes the reference arm. It cannot move a variant's own number, and a `full` value that falls further can only raise $\rho$. Spent so far: about 18.4 CPU-minutes (checks, probe and 12 scored runs). The amendment adds at most about 10.5, so the round stays near the 30 CPU-minute total, and `dense` (reported only) is dropped to stay within it.
* **Amendment A2 (written after the size-21 rerun and before any size-23 rerun result).** The size-23 `full` rerun of A1 did not start: it was launched from the wrong directory and exited at once, with no number produced. The size-21 rerun reached the plateau at step 1700 with the same best state as the capped run ($\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}=6.8\times10^{-4}t$). Size 21 therefore now fails the premise instead of convergence, and size 23 is the only size left that can count. A2 reruns `full` **and** `quarter` on size 23 from seed 11, each with an SR cap of 400 CPU-s. The `quarter` run was capped at 757 steps. Rerunning it completes an unconverged run of the same seed and changes no threshold. Both capped results are kept in `results/capped/`. With A2 the round totals about 29.5 CPU-minutes.
#### Round 12 T1 result: INCONCLUSIVE for both variants (one counted size); no T2 is licensed
Outputs: `check_output.txt`, `synthetic_output.txt`, `aggregate_scored_output.txt` (before A1), `aggregate_output.txt` (final), `logs/`, `results/` and `results/capped/`. Single-threaded, at most 4 processes, node load about 37 on 20 cores. **CPU spent: about 33 CPU-minutes**, 3 over the 30-minute cap. The A2 estimate left out the CPU time already spent on the capped runs, which is about 8.4 minutes. `dense` was not run.

| size ($V$) | `full` $\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}$ (steps, converged) | `half` $\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}$; $\rho$ | `quarter` $\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}$; $\rho$ | $\Delta E_{\mathrm{tot}}$: `full`; `half`; `quarter` | counted? |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| 20 (8) | $1.47\times10^{-3}$ (1050, yes) | $3.59\times10^{-3}$; 2.44 | $1.46\times10^{-2}$; 9.92 | 0.011; 0.033; 0.093 | yes |
| 21 (9) | $6.8\times10^{-4}$ (1700, yes) | $2.93\times10^{-3}$; 4.3 | $6.15\times10^{-3}$; 9.0 | 0.014; 0.050; 0.072 | no: premise ($<10^{-3}t$) |
| 22 (10) | $2.1\times10^{-6}$ (800, yes) | $4.3\times10^{-6}$; 2.0 | $5.2\times10^{-5}$; 24 | 0.0017; 0.0047; 0.0098 | no: premise |
| 23 (12) | $7.95\times10^{-3}$ (1539, **no**) | $1.26\times10^{-2}$ (700, yes); 1.59 | $1.48\times10^{-2}$ (1518, no); 1.86 | 0.047; 0.078; 0.095 | no: `full` unconverged at 400 CPU-s |

* **Scored verdict (rule unchanged): INCONCLUSIVE for `half` and for `quarter`.** Only size 20 counts. There $\rho=2.44$ and 9.92, both above the 1.25 kill bar, but the rule needs two counted sizes to kill.
* **Every comparison points the same way.** All 8 uncounted ratios are also above 1.25, and both variants have a larger $\Delta E_{\mathrm{tot}}$ at every size. This is reported, not scored. The direction is consistent, but the rule cannot score it. On size 23, `full` was still improving when it hit the cap (0.0491 to 0.0469 in $\Delta E_{\mathrm{tot}}$ between steps 757 and 1539). Its converged value can only be lower, so the reported ratios there are lower bounds if $\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}$ follows $\Delta E_{\mathrm{tot}}$ (not guaranteed).
* **Why the toys cannot decide it.** At $V\le10$ the all-sites route is nearly exact in sign ($\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}\le1.5\times10^{-3}t$), so only one size passes the premise. At $V=12$ it keeps a real sign error (0.008), but it does not converge within 400 single-threaded CPU-s (about 1500 steps at 0.26 s per step). A counted ladder needs at least three sizes with $V\ge12$ and converged training. That costs 10 to 30 CPU-minutes per run, which is above the T1 cap. Also, at fixed $F$ the variants have 30% to 50% fewer parameters (for example 304 and 224 against 464 at $V=8$). The test therefore also measures the parameter cost of removing hidden rows, which is the intended question at fixed $F$.
* **No T2.** The task's T2 (instance 4, equal wall time against dense HFPS and NNBF) was conditional on a T1 PASS. It is not designed, and no `sbatch` file is prepared. For a later design, the 1200-step NNBF pilot (457482) sets the horizon. $E-E_0$ is 0.340 at step 50, 0.139 at 200, 0.123 at 300, 0.113 at 500 and 0.101 at 1200. By step 300, 98.6% of the total descent is done, and after that the error falls by only about 2% per 100 steps. An equal-wall-time T2 must therefore give every arm at least the wall time of 300 NNBF steps, not 75.
* **Decision tree from here** (pre-registered INCONCLUSIVE branch, with one cheaper option for the orchestrator):
    1. Pre-registered branch: the accuracy question moves to VMC at $L\ge16$ against dense HFPS at matched cost per decorrelated sample (Round 11b). That needs SR log-derivatives in the compiled carried-inverse engine (`delayed.py` has ratios and $E_{\mathrm{loc}}$ only), which is a build at T2 scale.
    2. Cheaper and decisive at exact-sum size (proposed, not run): a T2-sized exact-sum ladder at $V=12$ to 15 ($4\times3$ 5+5, $6\times2$ 4+4, $4\times3$ 4+4, gaps 0.085, 0.095 and 0.096; $d_{S\chi}$ 724 to 1800), with the same arms and rule, 4 threads, and convergence by the same plateau test with a cap of 3000 steps. The cost is about 1 to 3 CPU-h per size for the three arms. The kill rule above would apply unchanged.
    3. Mutation (Operation A, not yet gated): the variants at larger $F$. The accepted-move cost depends on $n=N+V_h$, while $F$ enters only the $bFN$ ratio term. A `half` variant at $F=8$ might recover the capacity and keep most of the 5x cost advantage. It should be gated against the 11c cost first (one `delayed.py` run at $L=16$).
* **Autopsy addition:** a capacity toy whose reference arm is exact in sign on small clusters and unconverged on the first cluster where it is not cannot count three sizes inside a T1 cap. Rule: size the convergence cap from a timing probe of the largest size *at its plateau*, not from a 50-step probe, before writing the per-run cap.

| sublattice hidden rows, `half` ($v_h\approx1/2$) | T1 | T0 cost PASS (11c: $R_D(16)=5.05\pm1.27$); T1 sign capacity INCONCLUSIVE (one counted size, $\rho=2.44$; every uncounted ratio is above 1.25) | orchestrator choice between the exact-sum $V\ge12$ ladder and the VMC build | Round 12 T1 rule, unchanged | KILL: route dead, refill with the larger-$F$ mutation; PASS-TO-T2: the equal-wall-time T2 on instance 4 (horizon of at least 300 NNBF steps' wall time) |

| 7.1 (`full`), 7.3, 6.1″ and all earlier routes | — | CLOSED or KILLED (Round 11) | — | — | none |

### Round 12b: exact-sum ladder (2026-11-16)
The pre-registered fallback of Round 12 (decision tree, option 2), prepared as a Slurm array and **not yet submitted**. Same question, arms, protocol and rule. Only the toy sizes and the convergence budget change, so that `full` can reach its plateau on clusters where it keeps a real sign error.
#### Design (written 2026-11-16, before any ladder run)
* **Code** `tests/P2.1_subcubic_amplitudes_r12b/ladder.py`. It imports `t1_sub.py` read-only and uses its `setup`, `make`, `SubHFPSAnsatz` and `decide` unchanged. Its training loop is `t1_sub.train` step for step (same RNG streams, same plateau test), with checkpoints every 250 steps and resume added. Modes: `check`, `tasks`, `train`, `probe`, `progress`, `aggregate` and `synthetic`. Job file `submit_ladder.sbatch`.
* **Sizes** ($t$–$t'$–$J$, $t'=-0.25$, $J=0.4$, OBC, singlet non-degenerate ground state; `check_output.txt` asserts the gap and the singlet):

| key | cluster | $V$ | $D$ | $E_1-E_0$ | $d_{S\chi}$ | $V_h$ (`half`, `quarter`) | $n_{\mathrm{eff}}$ (`full`, `half`, `quarter`) |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| 20 (Round 12, reused) | $4\times2$, 3+3 | 8 | 560 | 0.066 | 44 | 4, 2 | 464, 304, 224 |
| 23 (rerun) | $4\times3$, 5+5 | 12 | 16632 | 0.085 | 724 | 6, 4 | 986, 626, 506 |
| 24 | $7\times2$, 6+6 | 14 | 84084 | 0.037 | 3078 | 7, 4 | 1325, 835, 625 |
| 25 | $5\times3$, 7+7 | 15 | 51480 | 0.196 | 1615 | 8, 6 | 1514, 989, 839 |

* **Why these.** $V=13$ has no rectangular OBC cluster with a 2D $t'$ ($13\times1$ is a chain), so the ladder skips it. The premise favours high density: in Round 12, $\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}(\texttt{full})$ was $2\times10^{-6}t$ at density 0.4, $7\times10^{-4}t$ at 0.67, $1.5\times10^{-3}t$ at 0.75 and $8\times10^{-3}t$ at 0.83. The chosen fillings have densities 0.83, 0.86 and 0.93. Rejected in the ED scan: $7\times2$ 4+4 (triplet, gap 0.007), $5\times3$ 3+3 (triplet, gap 0.0199), $7\times2$ 3+3 (density 0.43, premise risk), $5\times3$ 4+4 ($D=450450$, over budget). The Round 12 proposal ($6\times2$ 4+4 and $4\times3$ 4+4) had $V=12$ only, so it gave no ladder in $V$.
* **Memory.** Peak RSS is at most 0.55 GB at $V=15$ (`probe_output.txt`); the job asks for 4 GB.
* **Rule: the Round 12 T1 rule, unchanged** (`t1_sub.decide`), applied over sizes 20, 23, 24 and 25. A size counts if the gap is at least $0.02t$, the calibration and spread guards hold, `full` and the variant both converged, and $\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}(\texttt{full})\ge10^{-3}t$. KILL if $\rho>1.25$ at 2 or more counted sizes, or if $\rho$ rises strictly with $V$ over at least 3 counted sizes with $\rho(V_{\max})>1.15$. PASS-TO-T2 if no kill clause fires on at least 3 counted sizes. INCONCLUSIVE if fewer than 3 count. The single-seed marginal-kill clause (all triggering ratios below 1.5) applies as before. Sizes 21 and 22 of Round 12 are left out because both fail the premise. Size 20 is already counted, with $\rho=2.44$ (`half`) and 9.92 (`quarter`), so **one more counted size with $\rho>1.25$ kills a variant**.
* **Convergence criterion (unchanged test, larger cap).** Round 12's plateau test: from step 300 on, every 50 steps, stop when the best energy improved by less than $2\times10^{-4}t$ over the last 200 steps. A run that reaches 6000 steps or 10800 SR CPU-seconds first is unconverged, and its size does not count. The step cap is twice the 3000 of the Round 12 proposal, because `full` on size 23 was still improving at step 1539.
* **Threads.** One per task. The probe (`probe_output.txt`) gave 1.07 CPU-s per `full` step on size 23 with 4 threads, against 0.26 with one thread in Round 12, and 6.13 against 1.68 on size 24. Four threads cost about 4 times the CPU for no measurable wall-time gain on a loaded node.
* **Budget** (`full` per-step cost from the one-thread probe; the variants are cheaper). A task that reaches the step cap costs about 0.45 CPU-h on size 23, 2.0 on size 25, and 3.0 on size 24, where the CPU cap binds near 6000 steps. The budget step after training adds under a minute. The worst case for the 9 tasks is about 16.5 CPU-h. If the plateau arrives near the Round 12 step counts (700 to 1600), the total is about 3 to 5 CPU-h.
* **Early stops.** No metric-based futility clause: $\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}$ exists only after the budget step, so a checkpoint cannot score. `ladder.py --mode progress` reports $E-E_0$ at each checkpoint, for information only. Queue cleanup: once `aggregate` returns KILL for both variants from finished tasks, the remaining tasks cannot change the verdict and are cancelled.
* **Decision tree.** KILL: that variant is dead; refill with the larger-$F$ mutation (Round 12, option 3), gated first on the 11c cost check. PASS-TO-T2: design the equal-wall-time T2 on instance 4 (horizon at least the wall time of 300 NNBF steps). INCONCLUSIVE again: the exact-sum route is spent, and the accuracy question moves to the VMC test at $L\ge16$ (Round 12, option 1).

### Round 12c: larger-F cost gate (2026-11-16)
The cost gate of the Round 12 mutation (decision tree, option 3): `half` ($v_h=1/2$) at a larger hidden width $F$, to buy back sign capacity with more features per hidden row while keeping the 11c cost advantage. Cost only. No accuracy test is run in this round.
#### Pre-registration (written 2026-11-16, before any op count or timed run)
* **Code** `tests/P2.1_subcubic_amplitudes_r12c/larger_f.py` (new module). It imports the 11c engine `delayed.py` read-only and changes only the hidden width $F$ of the `half` route arm (`delayed.Route` already takes $F$). Nothing in r11c, r12 or r12b is edited, so the running ladder (704752) is untouched. Modes `params`, `gate`, `ops`, `run` and `aggregate`.
* **Arms.** `half_F` for $F\in\{4,8,12,16\}$, at $r_{\max}=16$ (the 11c choice for `half`, not rescanned; 11c found $t_{\mathrm{sw}}$ flat to 20% in $r_{\max}$), parameters from `sub_hfps.SubHFPS` at seed $11+L$. `half_F4` is the 11c `half` arm with the same parameters and serves as the reproduction control. Baseline: `dense2` at $F=4$, the 11c scored baseline, re-measured here. The baseline stays at $F=4$ for every $F$, because the accuracy question compares against the $F=4$ references; a matched-$F$ dense baseline would only be costlier and easier to beat.
* **Parameter counts** (`params_output.txt`; $F_0$, $K$, $\Omega$, and $W$ with $b$; $\Omega$ grows as $V_hFV$):

| arm | $L=12$ engine | $L=16$ engine | toy $n_{\mathrm{eff}}$ at $V=8$, 12, 14, 15 (`t1_sub`) |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| `half_F4` | 72620 | 229420 | 304; 626; 835; 989 |
| `half_F8` | 114136 | 360536 | 476; 958; 1271; 1513 |
| `half_F12` | 155652 | 491652 | 648; 1290; 1707; 2037 |
| `half_F16` | 197168 | 622768 | 820; 1622; 2143; 2561 |
| `full` ($F=4$, reference) | 124460 | 393260 | 464; 986; 1325; 1514 |
| `dense2` ($F=4$, baseline) | 104836 | 329732 | — |

* `half_F8` matches the parameter count of `full` at $F=4$ on every toy cluster (within 3%); `half_F12` and `half_F16` exceed it by 1.3 to 1.8 times.
* **Correctness gates (T0)** for `half_F8` and `half_F16` on $5\times3$ OBC and $6\times6$ PBC: per-configuration normalised ratio error at most $10^{-12}$ (11c amendment A1), compiled $E_{\mathrm{loc}}$ against `t1_r7.eloc_brute` at most $10^{-12}$ relative, and drift at most $10^{-8}$ after 2000 accepted moves with no refresh (fewer than the 11c $10^4$, to fit the cap; the 11c drift was $10^{-12}$ at $10^4$). A failure is a BUG and no cost is scored.
* **Op-count prediction** (mode `ops`, run before the timed runs and recorded below before they start). Move statistics ($b$, $k$, down moves, pending rank $r$, connections per sweep) are sampled by stepping the compiled `half` engine move by move. They do not depend on $F$. Multiply-adds per sweep come from the engine's terms: the $F$-dependent ones are the features ($10bF$), the $D$ rows ($bFN$) and the $C$ update ($bFV$ per accepted move), the rest is the $F$-independent $\mathcal{O}(n^2k)$ update. The predicted $R_D(16,F)$ is $R_D^{11c}(16)\cdot\mathrm{ops}(4)/\mathrm{ops}(F)$, with $R_D^{11c}(16)=5.05$. It assumes the $F$ loops run at the same speed per operation as the GEMMs, which favours larger $F$: the $F$ loops are scalar numba loops, and the 11c GEMMs ran at 20 to 50 GFLOP/s.
* **Metric:** the 11c metric unchanged (`delayed.run`: $c=t_{\mathrm{sw}}\,\tau_{\mathrm{int}}$, 4V attempts per sweep, Sokal $\tau_{\mathrm{int}}$, untrained parameters, doping $1/8$), at $L=12$ and 16 PBC with CPU budgets of 20 and 90 s per arm. Two waves of three single-threaded processes: wave A = `dense2`, `half_F4`, `half_F16`; wave B = `dense2`, `half_F8`, `half_F12`. Each `half_F` is scored against the `dense2` of its own wave, so both sides of a ratio see the same node load.
* **Scored:** $R_D(L)=c_{\mathrm{dense2}}/c_{\mathrm{half\_F}}$, $\sigma$ from the two $\tau$ errors in quadrature.
* **Rule.** GATE-PASS for the largest $F>4$ with $R_D(16)-\sigma\ge3$; that $F$ is the candidate for a later accuracy test. GATE-FAIL if no $F>4$ keeps $R_D(16)-\sigma\ge3$. A NaN $\tau$ at $L=16$ for an $F>4$ arm prints INCOMPLETE for that arm. `half_F4` is the control, not scored: if it falls below 3 at $1\sigma$ here, the round records that the 11c margin is load-sensitive, and the rule is still applied as written.
* **Caps** (enforced): `OMP`, `OPENBLAS`, `MKL` and `NUMBA` threads 1, at most 3 processes at a time, `ulimit -t 150` per timed process, at most 20 CPU-minutes for the round (params, gates, op count and six timed runs of about 115 CPU-s each).
* **When the larger-F accuracy test runs (margin argument).** The ladder (704752) decides the base variant at $F=4$ first. Its kill bar is $\rho>1.25$. The largest change in $\rho$ that any capacity step in this family has produced is a factor of about 4: `quarter` to `half` at $V=8$ ($\rho$ 9.92 to 2.44, Round 12), a step that doubles $V_h$ and adds about 80 parameters. Going from $F=4$ to the GATE-PASS $F$ adds per-row width but no new rows, and on the toys it adds 170 to 1570 parameters. A factor of 4 is therefore taken as the most that larger $F$ could plausibly buy. So: if the ladder KILLs `half` with $\rho>5$ ($=4\times1.25$) at 2 or more counted sizes, larger $F$ could not plausibly bring it under the kill bar, `half` is dead outright, and no larger-$F$ accuracy test runs. If the ladder KILLs `half` with $\rho\le5$ at a triggering size, or returns INCONCLUSIVE, the larger-$F$ accuracy test runs on the GATE-PASS $F$ (same ladder protocol, `full` at $F=4$ as reference). If the ladder returns PASS-TO-T2 for `half` at $F=4$, larger $F$ is not needed and is not tested. Size 20 already counts with $\rho=2.44$, inside the factor-4 reach.
#### Gates and op-count prediction (recorded before the timed runs)
* **Gates (T0): PASS** (`gate_output.txt`). Normalised ratio error at most $4.9\times10^{-14}$, $E_{\mathrm{loc}}$ against `eloc_brute` at most $7.0\times10^{-14}$ relative, drift after about 2000 accepted moves at most $3.1\times10^{-13}$, for `half_F8` and `half_F16` on both geometries.
* **Op-count prediction** (`ops_output.txt`; 3 sampled sweeps after 5 burn-in sweeps). Move statistics at $L=16$: 843 ratios and 127 accepted moves per sweep, $\langle b\rangle=4.0$, $\langle k\rangle=4.8$, pending rank $\langle r\rangle=10$. The $F$-dependent terms are 2% of the route's multiply-adds at $F=4$ and 6% at $F=16$, because $bFN$ and $bFV$ are small next to the $n^2k$ update ($n=240$).

| $F$ | ops$(F)$/ops$(4)$ at $L=12$, 16 | $R_{\mathrm{ops}}$ (dense2 / half) at $L=12$, 16 | predicted $R_D(16)$ |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| 4 | 1.00; 1.00 | 1.81; 3.19 | 5.05 (11c measured) |
| 8 | 1.03; 1.02 | 1.77; 3.14 | 4.97 |
| 12 | 1.05; 1.03 | 1.72; 3.09 | 4.90 |
| 16 | 1.08; 1.05 | 1.68; 3.05 | 4.82 |

* **Prediction:** GATE-PASS at $F=16$. With the 11c $\sigma\approx1.3$ the predicted $R_D(16)-\sigma$ is about 3.5 for every $F$, so the margin is set by the $\tau$ errors, not by $F$. Op-count alone, $F$ is nearly free.
#### Round 12c result: GATE-PASS at $F=16$
Outputs in `tests/P2.1_subcubic_amplitudes_r12c/`: `params_output.txt`, `gate_output.txt`, `ops_output.txt`, `logs/run_*.log`, `results/*.json` and `aggregate_output.txt`; driver `run_all.sh`. Single-threaded, at most 3 processes at a time, node load about 37 on 20 cores. **CPU spent: about 12.2 CPU-minutes** (params 0.03, gates 0.4, op count 0.4, six timed runs 11.4), inside the 20-minute cap.

| arm (wave) | $t_{\mathrm{sw}}$ (s) at $L=12$, 16 | $\tau_{\mathrm{int}}$ at $L=16$ | $R_D$ at $L=12$, 16 | $t_{\mathrm{sw}}$ ratio at $L=16$ | $R_D(16)-\sigma$ | predicted $R_D(16)$ |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| `dense2` (A) | 0.0258; 0.137 | $1.38\pm0.35$ | — | — | — | — |
| `dense2` (B) | 0.0235; 0.124 | $1.30\pm0.30$ | — | — | — | — |
| `half_F4` (A, control) | 0.0072; 0.0224 | $1.21\pm0.12$ | $5.26\pm1.51$; $6.94\pm1.88$ | 6.10 | 5.06 | 5.05 |
| `half_F8` (B) | 0.0077; 0.0241 | $0.94\pm0.08$ | $4.51\pm1.17$; $7.08\pm1.73$ | 5.15 | 5.35 | 4.97 |
| `half_F12` (B) | 0.0086; 0.0299 | $1.31\pm0.15$ | $4.42\pm1.16$; $4.13\pm1.05$ | 4.16 | 3.08 | 4.90 |
| `half_F16` (A) | 0.0095; 0.0289 | $1.23\pm0.14$ | $3.67\pm1.09$; $5.34\pm1.47$ | 4.74 | 3.87 | 4.82 |

* **Scored verdict (rule unchanged): GATE-PASS, and $F=16$ is the candidate** for the later accuracy test. Every $F$ clears $R_D(16)-\sigma\ge3$. The control `half_F4` reproduces 11c ($R_D(16)=6.94\pm1.88$ against 11c's $5.05\pm1.27$, both at or above 3 at $1\sigma$).
* **The margin is thin at large $F$, and it is set by $\tau$ noise.** `half_F12` passes by 0.08. The ordering $F=12$ below $F=16$ comes from its larger $\tau$ (1.31 against 1.23), not from its sweep time. The sweep-time ratios fall steadily with $F$ within each wave: 6.10 to 4.74 in wave A ($F=4$ to 16), 5.15 to 4.16 in wave B ($F=8$ to 12).
* **The op count underpredicts the cost of $F$.** It predicted $t_{\mathrm{sw}}(16)/t_{\mathrm{sw}}(4)=1.05$ at $L=16$; the measured ratio is 1.29 (wave A), and $t_{\mathrm{sw}}(12)/t_{\mathrm{sw}}(8)$ is 1.24 against a predicted 1.01 (wave B). The $F$ terms ($bFN$ features and $D$ rows, $bFV$ in the $C$ update) are scalar numba loops, slower per operation than the GEMMs of the $n^2k$ update, as the pre-registration warned. Measured, the $F$-dependent part is about 20 to 25% of the sweep at $F=16$, not 6%. Extrapolating that slope, $t_{\mathrm{sw}}$ at $F=32$ would be about 1.7 times the $F=4$ value, which would put the sweep-time ratio near 3.6 and $R_D(16)-\sigma$ near 3 at the current $\tau$ errors; $F=16$ is the largest width this gate supports.
* **Limits:** untrained parameters (every $\tau$ between 0.9 and 1.7 sweeps), single seed per arm, heavily loaded node, and $L=24$ not measured. The cost gate says nothing about whether larger $F$ restores sign capacity.
* **Next action (pre-registered branch above).** Nothing runs now. When 704752 finishes: if the ladder KILLs `half` at $F=4$ with $\rho>5$ at 2 or more counted sizes, `half` is dead and `half_F16` is not tested. If it KILLs with a triggering $\rho\le5$, or returns INCONCLUSIVE, the accuracy test runs `half` at $F=16$ on the ladder protocol against `full` at $F=4$ (toy $n_{\mathrm{eff}}$ 820 to 2561, 1.7 to 1.8 times `full`; a pass there must therefore be read against the parameter count, and `half_F8`, matched in parameters to `full`, is the natural second arm). If it returns PASS-TO-T2, larger $F$ is not tested.
* **Autopsy addition:** an op count that is dominated by BLAS terms will under-weight any change confined to the scalar loops. Rule: when a mutation only grows the non-BLAS terms, predict its cost from a measured per-operation speed of those loops, not from multiply-adds.

| `full_F8` | 764; 1606; 2153; 2458 | 1.65; 1.63; 1.62; 1.62 |

* **Matched parameters can be formed cheaply.** `half_F8` matches `full_F4` within 4%, and `full_F8` matches `half_F16` within 7%, on every size. Both matched pairs are in the array, so no comparison has to be read at unmatched parameters only.
* **Comparisons**, each scored by `t1_sub.decide` with the rule unchanged (KILL if $\rho>1.25$ at 2 or more counted sizes, or if $\rho$ rises strictly with $V$ over at least 3 counted sizes with $\rho(V_{\max})>1.15$; PASS-T1, printed as PASS-TO-T2, if no kill clause fires on at least 3 counted sizes; INCONCLUSIVE if fewer than 3 count; counted-size guards as in Round 12; the single-seed marginal-kill clause applies):
    * **A (primary, decides the route):** `half_F16` against `full_F4`. Unmatched parameters: `half_F16` has 1.62 to 1.77 times the parameters of `full_F4`.
    * **B (matched parameters):** `half_F8` against `full_F4`.
    * **C (matched parameters):** `half_F16` against `full_F8`.
    * **D (reported only):** `full_F8` against `full_F4`, to show whether $F$ alone helps the all-sites route.
* **Decision tree.**
    * A KILL: `half` is dead. $F=16$ is the largest width the Round 12c cost gate supports, so no larger-$F$ test follows. Both sublattice variants are then closed, and the Round 11 closure of P2.1 stands.
    * A PASS-T1 and C not KILL: the checkerboard keeps the sign capacity of the all-sites route at matched parameters. The equal-wall-time T2 on instance 4 is licensed (horizon at least the wall time of 300 NNBF steps).
    * A PASS-T1 and C KILL: recorded as **PASS at unmatched parameters only**. `half` recovers the capacity of `full_F4` only by spending 1.6 to 1.8 times its parameters, and `full` at the same parameter count is better. The T2 is still licensed, because the route's claim is accuracy at a given wall time and the Round 12c gate priced $F=16$ into the sweep cost. The T2 must then charge the larger SR solve (parameter count) to `half` inside the equal wall time. The leaf states that the route saves hidden rows, not parameters.
    * A INCONCLUSIVE (for example `half_F16` unconverged at 2 sizes): the exact-sum route is spent, and the question moves to the VMC test at $L\ge16$ (Round 12, option 1).
    * B does not change the A verdict. It answers whether the sublattice loses capacity at matched parameters with half as many rows. D is reported only.

### Round 13: refill (2026-11-16)
Portfolio refill while 706240 (`half` at larger $F$) runs. Target question: keep the 11c cost advantage while keeping the all-sites sign capacity. New code only, in `tests/P2.1_subcubic_amplitudes_r13/`; r11c, r12, r12b and r12d are imported read-only. No `sbatch` is run here.
#### What limits `full` on the 11c engine (T0, from the `delayed.py` code)
* The 11c engine carries the augmented inverse of dimension $n=N+V_h$. At $L=16$, `full` has $n=368$ and `half` has $n=240$; $N=112$.
* Per accepted move it pays about $2n^2k$ (the product $D\,M_i$ and the amortised flush), with $k\approx b+2\approx10$ for `full` and about 6 for `half`.
* Per down-move ratio it pays one dense product $M_0^{-1}\tilde c$ of $n^2$, because the new column has a nonzero entry in every hidden row ($C[:,s']$ is dense in $m$).
* The hidden–hidden block $(V_h\times V_h)$ is $V_h^2/n^2=48\%$ of the carried inverse at $L=16$. No ratio needs it except through its $S\times S$ entries, and those equal $C_S(A^{-1}K)_{:,S}$, which can be formed on demand in $\mathcal{O}(Nb^2)$.
* So the limit of `full` is $n^2$ with $n=N+V$, not the hidden rows' capacity. Shrinking $n$ by dropping rows (sublattices) bought the cost and lost the capacity (704752).
#### Candidates and gates (written before any Round 13 code)
* **13.1 SCH-full (Operation A on the 11c engine).** Same wave function as `full` (7.1, all sites, $F=4$). New engine: carry the physical inverse $A^{-1}$ ($N\times N$, $A=\Phi[\mathrm{up},\mathrm{dn}]$, $\Phi=F_0+KC$), the table $Q=A^{-1}K[\mathrm{up},:]$ ($N\times V_h$), and the slot-ordered blocks $C[:,\mathrm{dn}]$ and $K[\mathrm{up},:]$. A move is $A'=A+UW^{\top}$ with $U=[\delta c,\,e_i,\,K[\mathrm{up}',S]]$ and $W=[e_j,\,\delta r,\,\Delta C_S[:,\mathrm{dn}']^{\top}]$. The ratio is $\det(I+W^{\top}A^{-1}U)$. The row $\delta r$ and the column $\delta c$ of $\Phi$ are built on demand in $\mathcal{O}(NV_h)$, so no $V\times V$ table is carried.
    * Gate 0: PASS (engineering). Woodbury updates of a Slater matrix are standard (SCALE, arXiv:2604.25775); HFDS codes evaluate the augmented determinant. The route claims only a cost constant for an unchanged wave function.
    * Gate 1: PASS. Exact amplitude (same $\psi$); the gate below checks the ratios against the 11c engine.
    * Gate 2 foil: the 11c engine on the same wave function (`full`, measured $R_D(16)=1.78$) and `dense2`. The route is gameable if it is not cheaper than the 11c engine.
    * Gate 3: PASS (same instance class as 7.1).
    * Gate 4 (operation count): per accepted move about $2kN(N+V_h)$ instead of $2kn^2$; per ratio about $NV_h$ plus $N^2$ for a down move, instead of $n^2$ plus $nrk$. At $L=16$ that is about 3.3 times fewer per accepted move. The class is still $\mathcal{O}(N_e^3)$ per sweep.
* **13.2 HG, hole-gated hidden rows (Operation A, configuration-dependent placement).** $\psi=e^J\det\big(F_0+\sum_{m:\,n_m=0}K[:,m]\,h_m\Omega_m\big)[\mathrm{up},\mathrm{dn}]$: a hidden term is active only on an empty site. The sum is order-free, so no sign convention is needed. At doping $\delta$ there are $\delta V$ active rows (32 at $L=16$), and a hop moves one term and changes the few holes whose patch it touches ($b_{\mathrm{eff}}\approx2$–3).
    * Gate 0: PASS (quick search: G-HFDS for $t$–$J$, arXiv:2411.10430, uses a fixed hidden-fermion number; no hole-placed hidden rows found).
    * Gate 1: PASS. Order-free sum; $V_h=\delta V$ is extensive; size consistent.
    * Gate 2 foil: `half` (cheaper rows, all-sites patch cover) and `full_F4`. Scored against `full_F4`; `half` is reported beside it.
    * Gate 3: PASS. Doped $t$–$t'$–$J$; the toys' densities 0.83 to 0.93 bracket deployment (0.875).
    * Gate 4: on engine 13.1 the $A^{-1}$ part has $k\approx4$; the $Q$ table stays $N\times V$, so the cost per accepted move is about $2kN(N+V)$ with the smaller $k$.
* **13.3 CBH, checkerboard plus holes (Operation A, both levers).** Hidden terms on the checkerboard always, and on the other sublattice when the site is empty. Contains `half` as a sub-family.
    * Gates 0, 1 and 3 as 13.2.
    * Gate 2 foil: `half`. Because CBH contains `half` and costs more, it is gameable unless it beats `half` in $\rho$ at every counted size.
    * Gate 4: $V_h\approx V/2+\delta V/2$, $k$ between `half` and `full`.
* **13.4 LR, shared low-rank hidden couplings (Operation A, option a).** $KC=\sum_\rho U_\rho(\mathbf n)\bar\Omega_\rho$ with $U=K\bar H(\mathbf n)$ and $R$ shared basis rows. **KILLED at Gate 4 and Gate 2 (T0).** A move changes $U$ by $K[:,S]\Delta\bar H_S$, which has rank $b$ as in `full`, so $k$ stays at $b+2$. With $R$ scaling as $V$ (needed for size consistency) the update is $2kN(N+R)$ on engine 13.1, at most $(N+V)/(N+R)$ cheaper than 13.1 itself (1.5 times at $R=V/2$). It is not a superfamily of anything that holds the capacity. 13.1 has the same $k$ and the full capacity, and 13.4 saves at most a factor 1.5 on one term. 13.1 is the foil that games it.
* **13.5 LSF, local sign factor on $F_0$ (Operation B).** $\psi=e^J\det F_0[\mathrm{up},\mathrm{dn}]\prod_m g_m(\mathbf n)$ with patch-local real factors that can change sign; $\mathcal{O}(N)$ per ratio. **KILLED at Gate 0.** It is the correlator-product-state × Slater ansatz (master post-critic kill of P1.2, Changlani et al. PRB 80, 245116).
* **13.6 DA, two-stage delayed-acceptance Metropolis with `half` as surrogate (Operation B).** **KILLED at Gate 4 (T0).** The exact `full` ratio is still needed for every proposal that passes stage 1, and every accepted move still pays the `full` update. On the 11c engine accepted-move work is about 70% of the sweep, so the saving is bounded by the 30% ratio share (at most a factor 1.4).
* **Live after gating: 13.1, 13.2 and 13.3** (with `half` at $F=16$ in 706240, four).
#### Pre-registered T1 rules (written 2026-11-16, before any Round 13 code was run)
* **Engine gates (T0), every arm of the new engine** (`schur.py --mode check`), on $5\times3$ OBC and on $6\times6$ and $8\times8$ PBC:
    * (i) after random accepted moves, every connection ratio equals the brute-force ratio of an explicit gated $\det(F_0+KC)$ build (JW signs, sorted rows) to a normalised error of at most $10^{-12}$ (11c amendment A1), and $E_{\mathrm{loc}}$ equals `t1_r7.eloc_brute` to $10^{-12}$ relative;
    * (ii) for `full_S`, every connection ratio equals the 11c `delayed.Route('full')` ratio at the same parameters to at most $10^{-12}$ normalised (this is 13.1's accuracy gate: $\rho\equiv1$ by construction);
    * (iii) drift at most $10^{-8}$ after 2000 accepted moves with no refresh ($A^{-1}$, $Q$, $C$ and $E_{\mathrm{loc}}$ against a rebuild).
    * A failure is a BUG, and no cost is measured until it is fixed.
* **T0 op count (Gate 4) before the timed runs** (`--mode ops`): move statistics sampled from the new engine at $L=12$ and 16; multiply-adds per sweep from the formulas above for the arm and for the 11c `full` engine. Predicted $R_D(16)=1.78\times\mathrm{ops}_{11c}/\mathrm{ops}_{\mathrm{arm}}$ for 13.1. **T0 KILL** for an arm whose predicted $R_D(16)$ is below 3.
* **T1 cost** (the 11c harness and metric unchanged: `delayed.run` logic, $c=t_{\mathrm{sw}}\tau_{\mathrm{int}}$, $4V$ attempts per sweep, Sokal $\tau$, untrained parameters from `sub_hfps.SubHFPS(..., 'full')` at seed $11+L$, doping $1/8$, $L=12$ and 16, CPU budgets 20 and 90 s). Wave A: `dense2`, `full_S`, `hg_S`, and the 11c `full` as control. Wave B: `dense2`, `cbh_S`. Each arm is scored against the `dense2` of its own wave.
    * **PASS-cost** if $R_D(16)-\sigma\ge3$; **KILL-cost** otherwise; NaN $\tau$ at $L=16$ prints INCOMPLETE.
    * 13.1 also needs $c_{\mathrm{full,11c}}/c_{\mathrm{full\_S}}-\sigma>1$ (it must beat its own foil, Gate 2); otherwise KILL.
* **T1 accuracy** (13.2 and 13.3 only; 13.1 is $\rho\equiv1$ by gate (ii)). Round 12 protocol unchanged (`t1_sub.train`: unprojected exact-force SR, lr 0.05, shift $10^{-3}$, 1000 samples, seed 11, $F=4$, $H_a=0$, plateau test from step 300), on sizes 20 ($4\times2$, 3+3) and 23 ($4\times3$, 5+5), whose gaps are asserted. References: `full_F4` from Round 12 (size 20) and 704752 (size 23), both converged. SR cap 480 CPU-s, `ulimit -t 600` per run; an arm that hits the cap is unconverged and its size does not count. Counted-size guards as Round 12 (gap, calibration and spread, convergence, premise $\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}(\texttt{full})\ge10^{-3}t$). $\rho=\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}(\text{route})/\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}(\texttt{full\_F4})$.
    * **KILL** if $\rho>1.25$ at both sizes (the Round 12 two-size clause).
    * For 13.3 only, also **KILL** if $\rho(\texttt{cbh})\ge\rho(\texttt{half})$ at both sizes (Gate 2: no better than its own sub-family). The `half` values are 2.44 and 1.59.
    * **T1-PASS** if both sizes count and no kill clause fires. This licenses a T2: the same ladder on sizes 24 and 25 (Round 12b protocol, caps of 12b), which completes the Round 12 three-size rule.
    * **INCONCLUSIVE** if fewer than 2 sizes count: no T2 from this T1.
* **T2 for 13.1 if it passes T1 cost.** Cost at $L=20$ and 24 with $\tau$ measured (11c's $L=24$ clause: PASS if $R_D(24)-\sigma\ge4.5$), one single-CPU task per arm (`dense2`, `full_S`, 11c `full`), at most 2 CPU-h per task.
* **Caps (enforced).** `OMP`, `OPENBLAS`, `MKL` and `NUMBA` threads 1; at most 4 processes at a time; `ulimit -t 600` per process; at most 40 CPU-minutes for the round. CPU is logged per step, and the accuracy runs start only if the spent total leaves room for them.
#### Amendment A1: mutation 13.1′ (written 2026-11-16, after the T1 cost result and before any 13.1′ or accuracy run)
* **Why.** By the rule above, `full_S` is KILL-cost at $L=16$: $R_D(16)=3.50\pm0.85$, so $R_D-\sigma=2.66$. It does beat its foil, the 11c engine, by $1.81\pm0.34$. The route's claim is a class gain ($\mathcal{O}(N_e^3)$ against $\mathcal{O}(N_e^4)$ per sweep), so $R_D$ should grow with $L$. The 11c rule for this leaf carried a second clause for that reason ($R_D(24)-\sigma\ge4.5$). The Round 13 rule left it out.
* **13.1′ (Operation A on the 13.1 kill): the same engine and wave function, scored at $L=24$.** A new T1 run, not a rescue of the 13.1 verdict, which stands. Arms `dense2` and `full_S` at $L=24$ only, 360 CPU-s each, run at the same time; seed $11+24$; metric unchanged. **PASS-cost** if $R_D(24)-\sigma\ge4.5$; **KILL** otherwise; INCOMPLETE if $\tau$ is NaN.
* **Order and budget.** About 14 CPU-minutes are spent (gates 0.4, op count 0.05, seven timed runs about 13.5). The `hg` accuracy runs come first (sizes 20 and 23, at most 10 CPU-minutes each), in parallel with the two 13.1′ runs (6.5 CPU-minutes each, `ulimit -t 600`). `cbh` is KILL-cost, so its accuracy runs are skipped. The round total stays under 40 CPU-minutes.
