The low-noise sweep-time ratio is 3.89 (3.54 at $L=16$), also below 4.5. The class gain shows up only slowly: `dense2`'s LU runs more efficiently as $N$ grows.
* **Why the measured cost falls short of the op count.** For `full_S` the measured sweep-time ratio is 0.66 of $R_{\mathrm{ops}}$ (3.54 against 5.40). Most of its work is BLAS-2 (one $N^2$ and one $NV$ product per down-move ratio, about 80% of ratios) and thin rank-10 GEMMs. The dense LU at $N=112$–252 runs faster per operation. For `cbh_S` and `half_S` the gathered active-row loops ($N\times n_a$ per ratio) are scalar numba code. Here `half_S` is slower than `full_S` (0.056 s against 0.035 s), which makes 13.3's kill partly an implementation artefact. That is the 12c autopsy pattern again (the scalar terms are under-weighted). It is recorded, not used to change the verdict. `hg_S` passes despite using the same scalar path, because $n_a=32$.

* **Live routes: 3** (`half_F16` in flight, `hg`, and 13.7 gated behind it). The leaf is not blocked: `hg` has a prepared job.
* **Sharpest gap, sharper now.** Rows placed on holes keep the $V=8$ capacity of `full` with a quarter of the rows. Whether that holds at $V\ge12$, where `full` keeps a real sign error, is the question the ladder answers. On the cost side, the all-sites route cannot reach $R_D\ge3$ at $1\sigma$ by engine work alone at $L\le24$. The 11c engine is limited by $n^2$ with $n=N+V$; the Schur engine by the BLAS-2 ratio products against the dense LU's efficiency. So capacity has to come from where the rows sit, not from how many there are.
### Local Autopsy Log (Round 13 additions)
* **An op-count win that converts GEMM work into BLAS-2 products loses about a third of it on the clock** (13.1: $R_{\mathrm{ops}}=5.4$, measured sweep-time ratio 3.54). The dense baseline's LU gets more efficient as $N$ grows, so the class gain appears slowly (3.54 at $L=16$, 3.89 at $L=24$). Rule: predict cost gates from the measured speed of each kernel class (GEMM, GEMV, LU, scalar loops), not from multiply-adds.
* **Fewer rows is not the lever; where the rows sit is.** At $V=8$, the sublattice cuts (fixed placement) lost capacity at every size ($\rho$ 1.6 to 9.9), while hole-placed rows with even fewer active rows matched `full` ($\rho=0.83$). Size 12 is still open.

* **Value-of-information note for job 707185 (`submit_hg_ladder.sbatch`, 2026-11-16).** Decision: it decides whether route 13.2 (`hg`, hidden rows only on empty sites; the only P2.1 route that passes the T1 cost gate at $R_D(16)=4.45\pm1.04$) keeps the all-sites sign capacity. A KILL closes this branch of the reopened leaf, and a pass licenses its first T2. Prediction: open — $V=8$ gives $\rho=0.83$ (better than all-sites), while the capped $V=12$ run gives an uncounted 1.40, so neither outcome has probability near 0.8. Cost: 3 single-CPU tasks of at most about 2 CPU-hours each (tier at most 10). Cheapest decisive version: it finishes the capped $V=12$ run and adds $V=14$ and 15, the minimum needed for two counted sizes under the Round 12 rule. Submitted by the orchestrator.

### Partial results of the cancelled jobs (read 2026-11-17, documentation pass, no new compute)
Both jobs were cancelled at the 2026-11-16 pause (17:22). Their finished tasks wrote results; the read-only aggregates (`python3 hg_ladder.py --mode aggregate` in `r13/`, `python3 ladder_d.py --mode aggregate` in `r12d/`, about 1 CPU-second each) give:
* **707185 (`hg` ladder, R13.2)**: sizes 23 ($V=12$) and 25 ($V=15$) finished and converged; size 24 ($V=14$) was cancelled at step 1200, unconverged. $\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}$ of `hg` against `full_F4`: $1.09\times10^{-2}$ against $7.95\times10^{-3}$ at $V=12$ ($\rho=1.37$), $3.02\times10^{-3}$ against $1.48\times10^{-3}$ at $V=15$ ($\rho=2.04$); with $V=8$ ($\rho=0.83$) that is three counted sizes. The aggregate prints **KILL (ρ above 1.25 at 2 counted sizes)**. The kill clause needs only two counted sizes, both are converged and pass every guard, and the missing $V=14$ cannot remove a fired kill clause, so this verdict is complete in its inputs. Neither triggering ratio is below 1.5, so the single-seed "marginal" clause does not apply. Upheld by the per-leaf critic on 2026-11-17 (aggregate re-run, $n_{\mathrm{eff}}$ matched to `full_F4`, both counted sizes plateau-converged).
* **706240 (`half` at $F=16$ and $F=8$, R12.3)**: only the comparison-A arm `half_F16` ran, on $V=8$, 12 and 14 (all converged); its $V=15$ task was cancelled at step 150, and none of the 8 `half_F8` and `full_F8` tasks started. Comparison A prints PASS-TO-T2 on three counted sizes with $\rho=0.038$, 0.886 and 1.110. **This is not scored.** The rule's trend clause (KILL if $\rho$ rises strictly over at least 3 counted sizes with $\rho(V_{\max})>1.15$) can still fire at $V=15$, because the three ratios already rise strictly; a $V=15$ ratio above 1.15 and above 1.110 would turn the verdict into KILL. Comparisons B and C (matched parameters) have no data.

### Round 14: comparison A at $V=15$, re-gated (2026-11-17)
* **Job in flight.** `half_F16` at $V=15$ (Round 12d task 3, the only missing point of comparison A) was re-gated through `compute_gate.py` and submitted as job 731409 (T1, cap 3.17 CPU-h; `tests/P2.1_subcubic_amplitudes_r14/voi.md`, `submit_v15.sbatch`). The sizes $V=8$, 12 and 14 are on disk and are not rerun. Prediction PASS-TO-T2, $p=0.65$.
* **What $V=15$ decides (rule of Round 12d, unchanged).** The counted ratios so far are $\rho=0.038$, 0.886 and 1.110 at $V=8$, 12 and 14, strictly rising. The reference `full_F4` has $\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}=1.48\times10^{-3}$ at $V=15$ (704752).
    * $\rho(15)>1.15$, that is $\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}(\texttt{half\_F16})>1.70\times10^{-3}$: **KILL** by the trend clause (strict rise over four counted sizes with $\rho(V_{\max})>1.15$). The 1.25 clause cannot fire on its own, because only one size could exceed 1.25. A KILL with $\rho(15)<1.5$ is "marginal, single seed" and goes to the per-leaf critic before it is final. A final KILL closes both sublattice variants and cancels comparison C.
    * $\rho(15)\le1.15$: **PASS-TO-T2** on four counted sizes.
    * $V=15$ not counted (unconverged at the 6000-step or 10800 CPU-s cap, or any counted-size guard fails): comparison A stays at **PASS-TO-T2** on its three counted sizes, because neither kill clause can fire without the fourth size.
    * Either PASS is a PASS **at unmatched parameters only**: `half_F16` has 1.6 to 1.8 times the parameters of `full_F4`, and the matched-parameter comparisons B and C have no data.
* **Which document governs after a PASS (conflict settled 2026-11-17).** The Round 12d decision tree, written before any scored run, licenses the equal-wall-time T2 on a comparison-A PASS whatever comparison C says; C only sets how the T2 is read (a C KILL makes it "PASS at unmatched parameters only", and the T2 must charge `half`'s larger SR solve inside the equal wall time). The 2026-11-17 Leaf Status Summary had instead let a C KILL close the leaf. Pre-registration precedes the summary, so **the Round 12d tree governs**, and the summary below now says so. The counted ratios rise strictly over four sizes, 0.038, 0.886, 1.110 and 1.915, and $\rho(V_{\max})$ exceeds both 1.15 and the 1.5 marginal line, so the trend clause fires without a critic referral: **comparison A KILL; R12.3 KILLED at T1.** The VOI predicted PASS-TO-T2 with $p=0.65$: MISS. Comparison C is not run (Round 12d tree). R13.7 stays SHELVED: its unshelve condition (R12.3 passing at $V=15$) is not met. No LIVE route remains; generation round 15 is in progress (another agent).

### Round 15 (2026-11-17): refill after the R12.3 kill
Route generation after job 731409 killed R12.3, the last LIVE route. Seven routes were generated and gated (cards R15.1–R15.7 in the Route Register); the only computation at generation was reading stored results (`tests/P2.1_subcubic_amplitudes_r12/`, `r12b/`, `r12d/`, `r14/`). Notation as in the Route Register; $\rho=\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}(\text{arm})/\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}(\texttt{full\_F4})$, and $R_D(L)$ is the cost per decorrelated sample of dense HFPS (`dense2`) divided by that of the route on an $L\times L$ torus at doping $1/8$; the leaf's cost bar is $R_D(16)-\sigma\ge3$.
* **What the R12.3 kill says.** From the result JSONs, $\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}$ of `full_F4`, `half_F4` and `half_F16` at $V=8$, 12, 14, 15: $1.47$, $3.59$, $0.056\times10^{-3}$; $7.95$, $12.6$, $7.04\times10^{-3}$; $3.76$, $7.77$, $4.18\times10^{-3}$; $1.48$, $2.66$, $2.83\times10^{-3}$. At $V=15$ width bought total energy (`half` 0.0284 → 0.0239 from $F=4$ to 16) but no sign capacity (2.66 → $2.83\times10^{-3}$), so the $F=4\to16$ recovery at $V=12$ and 14 is not a capacity trend. Three explanations remain: an optimiser gap (every arm was scored at the best point of a noisy SR trajectory, `half_F16` 0.009 below its late mean against 0.003 for `full_F4`; tested by R15.1), a receptive-field limit (`half` rows never see an odd-centred patch; costed by R15.2), or row count itself (L28). The absolute sign-error differences are at most $9\times10^{-5}t$ per site, recorded but not used: the pre-registered ratio rule governs.
* **Batch outcome.** R15.2 (radius-2 receptive field), R15.3 (Schur engine at $L=32$) and R15.7 (multiple-try Metropolis) KILLED at G4; R15.4 (domain-local couplings, from local correlation in quantum chemistry) and its translation repair R15.5 (near-field plus low-rank far field, from fast multipole methods) KILLED at G0. R15.6 (learned sparse gating of hidden rows) passed T0 and was KILLED at T1 (interactive, about 7 CPU-minutes, `tests/P2.1_subcubic_amplitudes_r15/`): with the active fraction held at 0.495, $\rho(12)=2.57$ against the 1.59 of `half_F4`. R15.1 (polished re-score of `half_F16` against `full_F4` at $V=15$) is LIVE as gated job 742029 (`tests/P2.1_subcubic_amplitudes_r15b/`, T1, cap 0.42 CPU-h; KILL if $\rho_{\mathrm{pol}}(15)>1.25$, PASS-TO-LADDER if $\le1.15$; predicted KILL, $p\approx0.7$; a 5-iteration probe moved $\rho$ from 1.915 to about 1.78).
* **Portfolio.** One LIVE route (R15.1), below the three the skill asks for. The batch contains cross-field translations with a translation repair (R15.4 to R15.5), so if R15.1 dies the leaf meets the two-batch idea-saturation condition with Round 13 and goes DORMANT with the Saturation Diagnostic "the sub-cubic sweep pays at reachable sizes only with a carried matrix smaller than one hidden row per site; fixed, hand-ruled and learned placements all lose sign capacity on 12- to 15-site clusters, and width does not repair it ($V=15$)", whose falsifier is the equal-wall-time VMC test at $L\ge16$.
* **Effect on existing cards.** R13.7 (hole-gated rows at $F=8$, SHELVED) keeps its state; its unshelve condition (R12.3 passing at $V=15$) is now definitively unmet, and the $V=15$ data show width did not recover sign capacity there. Comparison C of Round 12d is not run (pre-registered).

## Route Register
Cards for every route generated in this leaf, in the order of generation; the card id keeps the leaf's own route label. Recurring terms, also defined in each card where they first matter: NQS is a neural quantum state; NNBF is neural-network backflow (a network maps the configuration to the orbital matrix whose determinant is the amplitude, Luo and Clark); HFDS and HFPS are hidden-fermion determinant and Pfaffian states (extra "hidden" rows and columns whose entries a network produces); the Foil is projected BCS (or Slater) times a Jastrow factor; SR is stochastic reconfiguration; ED is exact diagonalisation; $N_e$ is the electron number, $N=N_e/2$ per spin, $V$ the number of sites, $C_E$ the cost of one local energy and $C_{\mathrm{sw}}$ the cost of one Monte Carlo sweep; $\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}$ is the part of the energy error attributable to wrong signs (P1.1 definition, upper estimate) and $\Delta E_{\mathrm{tot}}$ the total energy error.

### R1 — Finite-range convolutional backflow with Woodbury updates (Route 1) (KILLED)
* **The idea**: Restrict NNBF to a single convolution of range $r$, so that a hop changes only the $\mathcal{O}(r^2)$ orbital rows of particles near the two touched sites. The determinant ratio then follows from a carried inverse by the Woodbury identity as a small determinant, giving $C_E=\mathcal{O}(N_e^2)$ and $C_{\mathrm{sw}}=\mathcal{O}(N_e^3)$, the Foil's asymptotic cost, instead of NNBF's $\mathcal{O}(N_e^4)$.
* **Lineage**: initial batch (round 1, 2026-11-10); its locality mechanism reappears in every later route that adds a global channel (R3, R6.1′, R7.1).
* **Outcome**: KILLED at G0 (2026-11-10).
* **Kill rule**: Gate 0: is the route known, and does it fix the reason the known version is not standard practice?
* **Evidence**: SCALE (arXiv:2604.25775) is this construction with affected-site caching and Woodbury updates, and Lrux (arXiv:2602.05255) supplies the update kernels. SCALE's own $16\times16$, doping $1/8$ energies sit about $3$–$4\times10^{-3}t$ per site above transformer and deep-convolution backflow of the same paper, larger than the $10^{-3}t$ per-site scale that separates competing orders in the Hard Core.
* **Why this kills it**: the construction is published, its adoption barrier is the accuracy loss caused by locality, and the route contains nothing that addresses that loss.
* **What the kill does not rule out**: local backflow plus a mechanism that carries long-range correlation at the same cost class; R3, R6.1′, R6.1″, R7.1 and R7.3 are such variants.
* **Strongest case against the kill**: SCALE's gap could be an optimisation effect rather than a limit of locality; not raised by a critic, unresolved, and irrelevant to the novelty verdict.
* **Checked by**: round-1 per-leaf critic (2026-11-11), which accepted the gate kills.
* **Lesson**: route-specific — a novelty kill of one published construction.

### R2 — Readout-rank exact ratios for transformer backflow (Route 2) (KILLED)
* **The idea**: In transformer backflow each orbital row is a fixed orbital plus a token embedding $t_x\in\mathbb{R}^d$ times a readout matrix, so the change of the whole matrix under any hop has rank at most $d+1$. With the readout times the inverse cached, every ratio is a $(d+1)$-dimensional determinant, exact, with no change to the family.
* **Lineage**: initial batch (round 1).
* **Outcome**: KILLED at G1 (2026-11-10).
* **Kill rule**: Gate 1: does the claimed cost survive the full operation count, including the network forward pass per connected configuration?
* **Evidence**: one changed token changes every token after the first attention layer, so each of the $\mathcal{O}(zN_e)$ connected configurations needs a forward pass of cost $\Theta(V^2d)$, giving $C_E=\Theta(N_e^3d)$. In the published run (Gu et al., arXiv:2507.02644) $d=256\ge N_e=224$, so the rank bound is vacuous as well.
* **Why this kills it**: the determinant algebra is no longer the bottleneck; the forward pass alone is at least cubic, which is the barrier the leaf exists to remove (F1, P3).
* **What the kill does not rule out**: attention variants whose per-hop forward change is local or low-rank; R7.4 tried the linear-attention version.
* **Strongest case against the kill**: a cached key-value forward could cut the forward cost; not raised by a critic; answered by the fact that in a multi-layer transformer every later-layer token depends on the changed token, so caching does not avoid recomputing $\mathcal{O}(V)$ tokens.
* **Checked by**: round-1 per-leaf critic (2026-11-11).
* **Lesson**: route-specific — the attention forward pass, not the determinant, sets the cost.

### R3 — Local plus global-bottleneck backflow, LGB-BF (Route 3) (KILLED)
* **The idea**: Each orbital row is a mean-field orbital plus a full-rank local term from a one-layer convolution plus a gated product of $K$ global channels pooled from all local features. A hop changes the rows within range $r$ fully and all other rows only through a rank-$2K$ change, so a carried projected Green's function gives exact ratios at $C_E=\mathcal{O}(N_e^2)$ while the global channels carry long-range information.
* **Lineage**: initial batch (round 1); the round-2 autopsy mutated it into R2.1 and its global channel into the Liu–Clark block of R3.4.
* **Outcome**: KILLED at G0 (2026-11-11, by the round-1 per-leaf critic at Gates 0 and 2).
* **Kill rule**: Gate 0 (is the family new, or a subfamily of a known one?) and Gate 2 (can the planned test fail against the Foil?).
* **Evidence**: the pooled $\tanh$ global term is a strict subfamily of the Liu–Clark rank-$r$ correction (arXiv:2311.09450), whose coefficient block is an arbitrary function of the configuration and which already underperforms full NNBF; the local part is SCALE. The planned 24-hour test measured only the timing slope of $C_E$ on random weights, which the Foil also meets ($C_E=\mathcal{O}(N_e^2)$).
* **Why this kills it**: the family adds no expressive mechanism beyond two published ones, and the test could not separate it from the Foil, so even a PASS would have shown nothing.
* **What the kill does not rule out**: a global channel at least as rich as the Liu–Clark block (R2.1, R3.4) and an accuracy metric; both were adopted as boundary conditions B1 and B2 of round 2.
* **Strongest case against the kill**: the combination (full-rank local plus global bottleneck) might train better than either component; not raised by a critic; unresolved, but a combination of known parts needs an accuracy test to claim anything, which this route did not have.
* **Checked by**: round-1 per-leaf critic (2026-11-11).
* **Lesson**: route-specific — a subfamily of a known weaker family plus an unfalsifiable test.

### R4 — Unbiased stochastic ratio estimator for unrestricted NNBF (Route 4) (KILLED)
* **The idea**: The local energy is linear in the amplitude ratios, so an unbiased estimator of each ratio $\det(I+A^{-1}\Delta)$ would keep the energy and gradients unbiased. Estimate the log-determinant with stochastic Chebyshev probes and exponentiate it without bias by Russian-roulette truncation, avoiding the $\mathcal{O}(N_e^3)$ determinant per connection.
* **Lineage**: initial batch (round 1); cross-field translation from randomised numerical linear algebra.
* **Source analogy**: randomised linear algebra estimates $\log\det$ of large matrices by a Chebyshev expansion and Hutchinson trace probes (Han, Malioutov and Shin, arXiv:1503.06394); it works because the log-determinant is a trace, hence additive and unbiasedly estimable. Correspondence: $\log\det(I+A^{-1}\Delta)$ is the log-ratio. Translation gap: the local energy needs the ratio itself, and an unbiased exponential of a noisy log has variance exponential in its magnitude.
* **Outcome**: KILLED at G1 (2026-11-10).
* **Kill rule**: Gate 1: does the route respect the complexity bound F1 and the variance properties of the local energy?
* **Evidence**: forming $\Delta(\mathbf n')$ for unrestricted NNBF already costs $\Omega(N_e^2)$ per connection, so $C_E=\Omega(N_e^3)$ before any estimator runs; the signed Russian-roulette series has variance growing like $\exp(\mathcal{O}(\lVert\log(I+X)\rVert))$, which diverges near nodes ($\rho\to0$) and for large ratios.
* **Why this kills it**: it misses the cost target by the matrix-output bound and destroys the zero-variance property exactly where the local-energy variance is set.
* **What the kill does not rule out**: multi-proposal or sign-stratified estimators (master note on P2.2′) and estimators for restricted families with low-rank $\Delta$.
* **Strongest case against the kill**: control variates could tame the variance; not raised by a critic; unresolved, but the F1 cost bound alone suffices.
* **Checked by**: round-1 per-leaf critic (2026-11-11); also in the master global anti-pattern "single-proposal stochastic replacement of an exact non-stoquastic sum".
* **Lesson**: L12 (`library.md`)

### R2.1 — Split-capacity hidden-fermion backflow, SC-HFDS (Route 2.1) (KILLED)
* **The idea**: Use the Liu–Clark hidden-fermion correction $\det(\Phi_0+\Phi_hC(\mathbf n))$ with a narrow "sign trunk" network for $C$ and spend most of the parameters on a separate positive amplitude network $e^{J}$, on the hypothesis that the known deficit of low-rank corrections is amplitude, not sign. Carried tables make each ratio $\mathcal{O}(N_e)$, so $C_E=\mathcal{O}(N_e^2)$.
* **Lineage**: Operation A from R3 after its kill at G0 (round 2); after its own kill, its carried-table component was mutated into R3.3 and its frame into R3.2.
* **Outcome**: KILLED at G0 (2026-11-11, round-2 per-leaf critic).
* **Kill rule**: Gate 0: is it a relabel of known constructions?
* **Evidence**: HFDS times a Jastrow already exists (Liu–Clark; HFPS takes a Jastrow from the same network, arXiv:2507.10705), and the ratio is the Lrux low-rank kernel. The round-2 test was also loose: rank $r=2$ against $N_\sigma\in\{4,5\}$ electrons per spin, so the restriction did not bite.
* **Why this kills it**: every component is published, and the only new claim (deficit is amplitude) was not testable on the round-2 clusters.
* **What the kill does not rule out**: a sum of such terms (R2.2) and the pairing frame (R3.2).
* **Strongest case against the kill**: the capacity split is a claim no paper had measured; not answered except by noting that a measured claim on an untestable instance has no value.
* **Checked by**: round-2 per-leaf critic (2026-11-11).
* **Lesson**: route-specific — a relabel.

### R2.2 — Shared-reference low-rank multi-determinant, SK-LR (Route 2.2) (MERGED)
* **The idea**: Sum $K$ hidden-fermion terms, each a configuration-dependent low-rank correction over one shared reference determinant, $\psi=e^J\prod_\sigma\det A_{0\sigma}\sum_kw_k\prod_\sigma\det(I_r+C_{k\sigma}(\mathbf n)Y_{k\sigma})$. One carried inverse serves all terms, so $C_E=\mathcal{O}(N_e^2)$, while the sum gives more nodal freedom than one term (a $K$-secant of the minor map, by Cauchy–Binet).
* **Lineage**: Operation B, cross-field translation from the table method of continuum quantum Monte Carlo (round 2); retained unchanged as R3.1 with a new test in round 3.
* **Source analogy**: multi-Slater–Jastrow QMC evaluates many excited determinants that share one reference with one inverse, quadratic in the electron number and linear in the number of excitations (Clark, Morales, McMinis, Kim and Scuseria, J. Chem. Phys. 135, 244105, 2011); it works because the excitations and CI coefficients are fixed. Correspondence: excitations are the $K$ low-rank terms, CI coefficients are the network outputs $C_k(\mathbf n)$. Translation gap: the coefficients here depend on the configuration, and a sum of terms is not size-consistent (found later, R3.1).
* **Outcome**: MERGED into R3.1 (2026-11-11).

### R2.3 — Low-rank-response AGP (Route 2.3) (KILLED)
* **The idea**: Move the hidden-fermion idea into the pairing frame: the amplitude is a determinant of a pair function $F_0+PQ(\mathbf n)^{\top}$ restricted to occupied up and down sites, which contains projected BCS (the Foil). A one-sided low-rank correction keeps each ratio at $\mathcal{O}(N_er^2)$, so the local energy stays quadratic.
* **Lineage**: Operation B.1 (frame change from particle–hole to particle–particle), round 2.
* **Outcome**: KILLED at G0 (2026-11-11).
* **Kill rule**: Gate 0: is it known?
* **Evidence**: it is the hidden-fermion Pfaffian state (HFPS, arXiv:2507.10705, applied in arXiv:2511.07566) restricted to $S_z=0$ singlet pairing; HFPS already has a configuration-dependent rank-$\tilde N$ block, a Jastrow and low-rank updates. The two-sided version is cubic, because of a new–new contraction block of cost $\mathcal{O}(N_e^2r)$ per hop.
* **Why this kills it**: the one-sided form is a published family, and the two-sided form misses the cost target.
* **What the kill does not rule out**: a sum of terms over the pairing reference (R3.2) and local hidden rows in the pairing frame (R7.1).
* **Strongest case against the kill**: the carried-table ratio is cheaper than HFPS's own; recorded under R2.1 and R3.2, not a reason to keep this card.
* **Checked by**: round-2 per-leaf critic (2026-11-11).
* **Lesson**: route-specific — a restriction of HFPS.

### R2.4 — Thouless rotation into an active virtual window (Route 2.4) (KILLED)
* **The idea**: Write the orbitals as occupied mean-field orbitals plus a configuration-dependent mixing $\kappa(\mathbf n)$ with the $r$ virtual orbitals nearest the Fermi level (antinodal momenta), on the idea that the important corrections live in that window, so a rank-$r$ correction with physically chosen orbitals suffices.
* **Lineage**: Operation B.1 (momentum or natural-orbital frame), round 2.
* **Outcome**: KILLED at G0 (2026-11-11).
* **Kill rule**: Gate 0: is it a subfamily of a known or sibling route with no new mechanism?
* **Evidence**: it is R2.1 with the hidden orbitals frozen to the active virtual orbitals, i.e. a subfamily of HFDS; SR can already choose those orbitals.
* **Why this kills it**: freezing parameters removes freedom without adding a mechanism.
* **What the kill does not rule out**: physically initialised hidden orbitals as a training aid.
* **Strongest case against the kill**: a good fixed basis could ease optimisation; not raised by a critic; unresolved and outside the leaf's cost question.
* **Checked by**: round-2 per-leaf critic (2026-11-11).
* **Lesson**: route-specific — a frozen subfamily.

### R2.5 — Delayed-acceptance two-stage Metropolis (Route 2.5) (KILLED)
* **The idea**: Screen each Metropolis proposal with the cheap restricted family and evaluate the expensive full NNBF ratio only for proposals that survive the screen, keeping detailed balance through the second-stage correction. This lowers the cost per accepted move while sampling the exact full-family distribution.
* **Lineage**: Operation B.2 (active probing), cross-field translation from computational statistics, round 2; re-proposed as R13.6.
* **Source analogy**: delayed-acceptance MCMC (Christen and Fox, J. Comput. Graph. Stat. 14, 795, 2005) uses a cheap surrogate posterior to reject most proposals before the expensive likelihood; it works when the expensive evaluation is needed only for acceptance. Correspondence: posterior is $\lvert\psi\rvert^2$, surrogate is the restricted family. Translation gap: VMC also needs the local energy, which uses every connected ratio of the full family.
* **Outcome**: KILLED at G1 (2026-11-11).
* **Kill rule**: Gate 1: does the route meet the complexity bound F1 for the local energy?
* **Evidence**: $E_{\mathrm{loc}}$ needs all $\mathcal{O}(zN_e)$ full-family ratios, so $C_E=\Omega(N_e^3)$ is untouched; replacing them by surrogate ratios is biased.
* **Why this kills it**: it saves only on acceptance, which is not where the cost lies.
* **What the kill does not rule out**: delayed acceptance as a constant-factor tool where acceptance dominates.
* **Strongest case against the kill**: none raised; the cost argument is an operation count.
* **Checked by**: round-2 per-leaf critic (2026-11-11).
* **Lesson**: route-specific — the local energy, not acceptance, is the cost.

### R2.6 — Fast-multipole hierarchical backflow (Route 2.6) (KILLED)
* **The idea**: Near-field orbital rows are full-rank local backflow, while far-field influence enters through rank-$p$ channels per level of a quadtree, so the change of the orbital matrix under a hop has rank $\mathcal{O}(r^2+p\log V)$ and ratios stay cheap while every particle feels distant ones.
* **Lineage**: Operation B.3, cross-field translation from fast multipole methods, round 2; repaired as R6.4 (Tree-HFDS).
* **Source analogy**: the fast multipole method and hierarchical matrices (Greengard and Rokhlin, J. Comput. Phys. 73, 325, 1987; Hackbusch, Computing 62, 89, 1999) compress far-field interactions to low rank per level, which works because smooth kernels have low-rank far fields. Correspondence: near field is local rows, far field is pooled coarse channels. Translation gap: the backflow correction need not be smooth, and on exact-sum clusters everything is near field.
* **Outcome**: KILLED at G2 (2026-11-11).
* **Kill rule**: Gate 2 (is the far-field mechanism weaker than a known baseline?) and Gate 3 for testing (can the claim be tested within the Hard Core at affordable size?).
* **Evidence**: the far-field channels are pooled rank-$p$ channels, the structure the round-1 critic showed is a strict subfamily of the Liu–Clark correction; on every cluster that admits exact sums ($V\le16$) all sites lie in the near field.
* **Why this kills it**: its novel part is a known weak subfamily, and its claim cannot be measured where $\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}$ is exact.
* **What the kill does not rule out**: a hierarchy with nonlinear coarse features (R6.4) tested by variational Monte Carlo at $8\times8$ or larger.
* **Strongest case against the kill**: the hierarchy's value is precisely at large size; accepted, which is why the kill is scoped to exact-sum testing.
* **Checked by**: round-2 per-leaf critic (2026-11-11).
* **Lesson**: L39 (`library.md`)

### R3.1 — SK-LR, shared-reference low-rank sum, round-3 test (Route 3.1) (KILLED)
* **The idea**: $\psi=e^{J}\prod_\sigma\det A_{0\sigma}\sum_{k=1}^Kw_k\prod_\sigma\det(I_r+C_{k\sigma}(\mathbf n)Y_{k\sigma})$, with $Y=A_0^{-1}\Phi_h[\mathrm{occ}]$ a carried table and $C_k$ the output of a narrow sign trunk; a positive amplitude network $e^J$ takes the rest of the parameters. Every ratio is a small determinant from carried tables, so $C_E=\mathcal{O}(N_e^2)$, and the hypothesis was that the sum keeps projected-NNBF sign quality at fixed parameter count.
* **Lineage**: R2.2 retained (round 3), restated in round 5 as a parameter-split claim after the critic showed rank alone is not binding.
* **Source analogy**: the table method of multi-determinant QMC (Clark et al., J. Chem. Phys. 135, 244105, 2011), as in R2.2; the gap that killed it is size consistency.
* **Outcome**: KILLED at G4 (2026-11-16, retroactive T0 argument, upheld by the round-6 per-leaf critic).
* **Kill rule**: Gate 4 (scaling and relevance): does the gain survive growing system size, i.e. is the sign capacity size-consistent at fixed width?
* **Evidence**: T0 lemma, corrected by the round-6 critic: restrict $M$ decoupled blocks to two states of opposite block sign; the target sign is the parity of $M$ bits, which has polynomial-threshold degree $M$ (Minsky–Papert). With a block-separable or locally connected trunk, each term is a polynomial of degree at most 2 (at most 4) in block functions, and so is any sum of $K$ terms, so parity is out of reach for $M\ge5$ at every $K$; a dense trunk needs width $\Omega(N_e)$, which makes $C_E=\Omega(N_e^3)$. The finite-size measurement (job 526080, $4\times4$ OBC $t$–$t'$–$J$ instances 0 and 3, $6+6$ and $5+5$ electrons, 3 seeds, 1200 projected SR steps, $P\approx1.5$–$1.7\times10^3$) was AMBIGUOUS: $\rho_s=\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}/\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}(\text{projected NNBF})=0.97$ and 0.87, but the oracle-spin ratio 1.30 and 1.16 sat in the ambiguous band; cost check PASS (594 times faster than brute force per connection at $N_e=896$).
* **Why this kills it**: the leaf's goal is sub-cubic cost with no accuracy loss at scale; a family whose sign capacity at fixed width cannot represent independent block signs loses accuracy as $V$ grows, and fixing that costs at least the Foil's exponent.
* **What the kill does not rule out**: the measured ED-size fact (a width-6 trunk with four rank-1 terms nearly matches projected NNBF on 16 sites, 236–594 times cheaper per connection); products instead of sums (R6.2) and local hidden rows (R6.1′).
* **Strongest case against the kill**: real ground states are not parities of decoupled blocks, so the worst case may not bind; the critic answered that size consistency is the minimum requirement for any extensive claim, and that the ED gain was largely a point-group-projection finite-size bonus (projection cut NNBF's sign error from 0.091 to $0.0125t$).
* **Checked by**: round-6 per-leaf critic (2026-11-16, Minor 1 corrected the lemma's citation); cross-node critic 2026-11-16.
* **Lesson**: L53 (`library.md`)

### R3.2 — SK-AGP, shared pairing-reference low-rank sum (Route 3.2) (KILLED)
* **The idea**: The R3.1 sum moved onto the Foil's pairing reference: $\psi=e^{J}\det B_0\sum_kw_k\det(I_r+C_k[:,\mathrm{occ}_\downarrow]B_0^{-1}\Phi_{h,k}[\mathrm{occ}_\uparrow])$, with $B_0$ the pair-function matrix of projected BCS. With $r=0$ it is exactly the Foil; carried pairing tables give $C_E=\mathcal{O}(N_e^2)$, and the sum adds sign freedom on top of the Foil's frame.
* **Lineage**: Operation B.1 on the killed R2.1 (frame change to the pairing frame), round 3.
* **Outcome**: KILLED at G4 (2026-11-16, retroactive T0, upheld by the round-6 critic).
* **Kill rule**: Gate 4, as R3.1: is the sign capacity size-consistent at fixed width?
* **Evidence**: the R3.1 parity argument applies unchanged, because $\det B_0$ (a product over decoupled blocks) and $e^J$ (positive) cannot supply the block-parity sign. Job 526080: AMBIGUOUS, $\rho_s=0.75$ and 0.82, but $\gamma=\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}/\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}(\text{Slater}\times\text{Jastrow})=0.62$ on instance 3, inside the ambiguous band; cost PASS (236 times faster per connection).
* **Why this kills it**: same inference as R3.1.
* **What the kill does not rule out**: the pairing frame itself, which R7.1 reused with local hidden rows.
* **Strongest case against the kill**: as R3.1; the per-instance PASS on instance 0 shows the frame helps at ED size; answered as R3.1.
* **Checked by**: round-6 per-leaf critic (2026-11-16).
* **Lesson**: L53 (`library.md`)

### R3.3 — Image-table projected SK-LR (Route 3.3) (KILLED)
* **The idea**: Symmetry projection averages the amplitude over the $\lvert G\rvert$ images of the configuration under the lattice group. With a reference whose column span is group-invariant, one carried inverse serves all images, and only $\lvert G\rvert K$ extra table columns are needed, so projection would cost little extra.
* **Lineage**: Operation A on R2.1's carried-table component, aimed at the projection-cost wall (round 3); mutated into R6.3.
* **Outcome**: KILLED at G1 (2026-11-12).
* **Kill rule**: Gate 1: does the operation count, including the network, support the claim?
* **Evidence**: the network forward $C_k(g\mathbf n')$ costs $\mathcal{O}(H_sKrN_e)$ per image and connection, the same as separate tables, so the local energy is unchanged; with translations $\lvert G\rvert\propto V$ adds a factor $N_e$ either way.
* **Why this kills it**: the saving is in memory and the sweep only, not in the dominant term.
* **What the kill does not rule out**: an equivariant trunk whose image forwards are permutations of one forward (R6.3).
* **Strongest case against the kill**: none raised; it is an operation count.
* **Checked by**: round-3 per-leaf critic (2026-11-12).
* **Lesson**: route-specific — per-image forwards dominate.

### R3.4 — SCALE plus an unrestricted Liu–Clark block (Route 3.4) (KILLED)
* **The idea**: Repair LGB-BF (R3) by combining full-rank finite-range local rows (SCALE) with the unrestricted Liu–Clark hidden-fermion block, so the family contains both published families; the local part keeps updates cheap and the global block carries long-range correlation.
* **Lineage**: Operation A on R3 (round 3).
* **Outcome**: KILLED at G3 (2026-11-12, Gate 3 for testing).
* **Kill rule**: Gate 3 for testing: can the restriction be measured on an instance that keeps the Hard Core?
* **Evidence**: on every exact-sum cluster ($4\times4$) a range-1 patch with $t'$ bonds covers nearly every particle, so the "local" part is global and the restriction cannot bite; Gate 0 was also at risk as a sum of two published components.
* **Why this kills it**: a test that cannot separate the route from its foil cannot promote it.
* **What the kill does not rule out**: the same family tested by variational Monte Carlo at $8\times8$ or larger.
* **Strongest case against the kill**: the combination might still help at large size; accepted as scope, unresolved.
* **Checked by**: round-3 per-leaf critic (2026-11-12).
* **Lesson**: L39 (`library.md`)

### R6.1 — Global-kernel linear-readout backflow, GK-BF (Route 6.1) (KILLED)
* **The idea**: Orbital row $j$ is a mean-field orbital plus $K[x_j,:]H(\mathbf n)\Omega$, with local features $H$ from patches, a trainable site-to-site kernel $K$ and a shared readout $\Omega$. A hop changes $H$ on a few sites only, so the matrix changes by rank $1+b$, and the global kernel makes the correction long-range at $\mathcal{O}(N_e^2)$ local energy.
* **Lineage**: Operation A on R2.6 and R3.4 (round 6); mutated into R6.1′.
* **Outcome**: KILLED at G4 (2026-11-16, T0, found after its T1 run).
* **Kill rule**: Gate 4: is the sign capacity extensive (size-consistent) at fixed width?
* **Evidence**: by the determinant lemma $\det(A_0+G\Omega)=\det A_0\det(I_F+\Omega A_0^{-1}G)$ with $G=K[\mathrm{occ},:]H$, so the family is one $F\times F$ hidden-fermion factor; decoupled blocks share one $F$-dimensional readout space, so $F$ must grow with the number of blocks.
* **Why this kills it**: a fixed-size factor cannot multiply independent block corrections, the round-6 extensivity boundary condition.
* **What the kill does not rule out**: one hidden row per site with site-dependent readouts (R6.1′), which is size-consistent.
* **Strongest case against the kill**: none raised; it is an identity.
* **Checked by**: round-6 per-leaf critic (2026-11-16).
* **Lesson**: L53 (`library.md`)

### R6.1′ — Local-hidden-row HFDS with a plus-shaped patch, LH-HFDS (Route 6.1′) (KILLED)
* **The idea**: One hidden fermion per site $m$, whose hidden row $C_m(\mathbf n)=h_m(\mathbf n)\Omega_m$ depends only on the features of a small patch around $m$, coupled to the physical orbitals by a learned global linear map $K$: $A=\Phi_0[\mathrm{occ}]+K[\mathrm{occ},:]C(\mathbf n)$. A hop changes only the $b$ hidden rows whose patch it touches, so ratios are $(1+b)$-dimensional determinants, $C_E=\mathcal{O}(N_e^2)$, and decoupled blocks factorise.
* **Lineage**: Operation A on R6.1 (round 6); its reported-only $3\times3$ arm became R6.1″; its frame change became R7.1 and its resolvent extension R7.3.
* **Outcome**: KILLED at T2 (2026-11-16, job 698229).
* **Kill rule**: pre-registered T2 rule (round 7): "KILL if $\rho_N\ge1.5$, or if $\rho_F>0.85$ (the route does not beat the one-site lattice-backflow foil)", with $\rho_F=\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}(\texttt{lh\_plus})/\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}(\texttt{lh\_site})$ and $\rho_N$ the ratio to parameter-matched NNBF.
* **Evidence**: instance 4 ($5\times3$ OBC $t$–$t'$–$J$, $6+6$ electrons), seed 11, 75 projected SR steps, $P\approx1626$: $\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}$ 0.0655 (route), 0.0755 (one-site foil), 0.0880 (NNBF), so $\rho_F=0.868$ and $\rho_N=0.744$; single seed, no error bar. Round 8b had also given KILL-COST (uncertain): cost per decorrelated sample against NNBF of width 6 at $L=16$, $R(16)=0.96\pm0.42$.
* **Why this kills it**: the plus patch does not beat one-site features (a known long-range lattice backflow class, Tocchio–Becca) by the pre-registered margin, so the patch nonlinearity adds no demonstrated sign quality.
* **What the kill does not rule out**: larger patches (R6.1″), other frames (R7.1), and longer training; the 75-step horizon measures learning speed.
* **Strongest case against the kill**: a single seed at 75 steps cannot resolve 0.868 from 0.85; unresolved, but the rule was pre-registered and the route's successor R6.1″ was tested instead.
* **Checked by**: orchestrator applied the rule (2026-11-16); the Round 11 per-leaf critic (2026-11-16) read the result and did not contest it.
* **Lesson**: L33 (`library.md`)

### R6.1″ — LH-HFDS with a $3\times3$ patch (Route 6.1″) (KILLED)
* **The idea**: R6.1′ with a $3\times3$ feature patch: each site's hidden row sees nine sites, a hop changes at most $b=14$ hidden rows, and the local energy stays $\mathcal{O}(N_e^2)$ with a constant about 1.75 to 3 times larger; the wider patch was the best arm of job 698229 ($\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}=0.0388$ against NNBF's 0.0880).
* **Lineage**: R6.1′'s reported-only arm, promoted to a route with its own pre-registered T2 (round 9).
* **Outcome**: KILLED at CRITIC (2026-11-16, Round 11 closure critic, after a T2 PROMOTE by rule).
* **Kill rule**: Round 11 critic: a cost-reduction route survives only with a cost advantage per decorrelated sample, at equal accuracy, against the cheapest correct same-frame cubic baseline; short-horizon parameter-matched ratios may kill, never promote.
* **Evidence**: job 699528, instance 4, seed 22, 75 steps: $\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}$ 0.0222 (route), 0.0408 (plus patch), 0.0376 (NNBF $H=16$, matched in operations per amplitude), 0.0099 (NNBF $H=48$, reported only), so $\rho_N=0.28$, $\rho_C=0.59$, $\rho_F=0.54$, PROMOTE by rule. The $H=48$ NNBF is 3.4 times faster per step and 2.2 times lower in sign error. Operation-count crossover against the dense same-frame baseline lies beyond $L=32$ for $b=14$. NNBF reaches 0.0159 at 1200 steps against 0.088 at 75, so 75-step ratios measure learning speed.
* **Why this kills it**: the only evidence for the route is a learning-speed ratio, the practical comparison at equal wall time reverses it, and it has no cost case at reachable sizes, so it carries none of the leaf's claim.
* **What the kill does not rule out**: a converged, equal-wall-time accuracy comparison at $L\ge16$ by variational Monte Carlo; the $H=48$ NNBF is itself a sector-fitting artefact ($P/d_{S\chi}=0.70$).
* **Strongest case against the kill**: the T2 was pre-registered and passed; the critic answered that the T2 rule tested a quantity the leaf does not claim, and kept the verdict as "T2 PROMOTE (learning speed at matched $P$)".
* **Checked by**: Round 11 per-leaf critic (2026-11-16); cross-node critic (2026-11-16) adopted the anti-pattern.
* **Lesson**: L33 (`library.md`)

### R6.2 — Product of local rank-1 determinant-ratio factors, PLR (Route 6.2) (KILLED)
* **The idea**: Replace SK-LR's sum by a product over sites of local factors $1+h_m(\mathbf n)\cdot Z[:,m]$, each equal to a one-hidden-fermion determinant ratio over the reference, $\det(A_0+\phi_mc_m^{\top})/\det A_0$. A product of block factors factorises over decoupled blocks, so the sign capacity is size-consistent, and all $V$ factors refresh in $\mathcal{O}(VF)$ per hop.
* **Lineage**: Operation B.3, cross-field translation from coupled-cluster theory (round 6).
* **Source analogy**: coupled cluster is size-extensive while truncated configuration interaction is not, because the exponential (product) form factorises over non-interacting fragments (Bartlett and Musiał, Rev. Mod. Phys. 79, 291, 2007). Correspondence: SK's sum of terms is truncated CI; PLR's product is the exponential form. Translation gap: each factor is normalised by $\det A_0$, so the product divides by the reference determinant once per site.
* **Outcome**: KILLED at G1 (2026-11-16, round-6 per-leaf critic; an earlier timing-guard kill was voided).
* **Kill rule**: Gate 1: is the amplitude a well-behaved fermionic function (no singularities that break sampling)?
* **Evidence**: per spin $\psi_{\mathrm{PLR}}=\det(A_0)^{1-M}\prod_m\det(A_0+\phi_mc_m^{\top})$, a pole of order $M-1$ at every reference node; the critic's check measured $\lvert\psi\rvert\propto\varepsilon^{-35.11}$ against the predicted $\varepsilon^{-35}$ at $M=V=36$, $\max\log_{10}\lvert\prod_mf_m\rvert$ of $+22$ at $L=6$ and $+78$ at $L=8$ over 800 random blocks, and a T1 local energy near $-9\times10^{20}$ at $L=8$. It is also dominated by R6.1′, which puts the same factors in one determinant and is pole-free.
* **Why this kills it**: under $\lvert\psi\rvert^2$ sampling the chain is drawn to the reference nodes and the local energy has unbounded variance, with an extensive pole order.
* **What the kill does not rule out**: product forms that are polynomials in bounded entries (boundary condition B7.2), such as R6.1′.
* **Strongest case against the kill**: random weights exaggerate the poles; the order of the pole is structural, so training cannot remove it.
* **Checked by**: round-6 per-leaf critic (2026-11-16).
* **Lesson**: L53 (`library.md`)

### R6.3 — Translation-equivariant trunk with shared image tables (Route 6.3) (KILLED)
* **The idea**: With a translation-equivariant sign trunk, the network outputs for all symmetry images of a configuration are permutations of one forward pass, so symmetry projection would need no extra network evaluations and only extra carried tables per image.
* **Lineage**: Operation A on R3.3 (round 6).
* **Outcome**: KILLED at G4 (2026-11-16, T0).
* **Kill rule**: Gate 4: does the gain survive system size, or is it a finite-size bonus?
* **Evidence**: the carried tables are still one set per image, so $C_E=\mathcal{O}(\lvert G\rvert N_e^2)$, which is cubic with translations ($\lvert G\rvert\propto V$); with the point group only, the gain is one global quantum number worth $\mathcal{O}(1/V)$ per site, and a fully equivariant ansatz with an invariant reference is already symmetric.
* **Why this kills it**: either the cost is cubic or the accuracy gain vanishes per site.
* **What the kill does not rule out**: point-group projection as an ED-size accuracy tool.
* **Strongest case against the kill**: none raised.
* **Checked by**: round-6 per-leaf critic (2026-11-16).
* **Lesson**: L10 (`library.md`)

### R6.4 — Hierarchical local-hidden-row HFDS, Tree-HFDS (Route 6.4) (KILLED)
* **The idea**: R6.1′ plus one hidden fermion per block of a $2^\ell\times2^\ell$ block hierarchy, each with nonlinear block features computed from its children. A hop changes the local rows plus $\mathcal{O}(\log V)$ ancestor rows, so $C_E=\mathcal{O}(N_e^2F(b+\log V))$, and coarse rows are nonlinear in whole-block content, which a purely local patch lacks.
* **Lineage**: Operation A on R6.1′'s sharpest gap, and translation repair of R2.6 (its pooled linear far field replaced by nonlinear block features), round 6.
* **Source analogy**: fast multipole and hierarchical matrices (Greengard and Rokhlin 1987; Hackbusch 1999), as for R2.6; the repair replaces linear pooled far-field channels (the B2 weakness) with nonlinear block features. The translation gap that remained is testability: exact-sum clusters have almost no hierarchy.
* **Outcome**: KILLED at G3 (2026-11-16, Gate 3 for testing, round-6 critic Major 3).
* **Kill rule**: Gate 3 for testing: can the added mechanism be measured on an exact-sum instance against a foil that isolates it?
* **Evidence**: coarse levels exist only while $2^\ell$ divides the side length; on instance 4 ($5\times3$) there is none, so the T2 would test plain R6.1′, and on $4\times4$ the root row is one global nonlinear hidden unit, which is NNBF's mechanism. T1 had passed (exactness $2.4\times10^{-15}$, timing slope 0.66).
* **Why this kills it**: no affordable exact-sum test can attribute a gain to the hierarchy, the R2.6 situation.
* **What the kill does not rule out**: the hierarchy tested by variational Monte Carlo at $8\times8$ or larger against NNBF with variance-extrapolated energies.
* **Strongest case against the kill**: the hierarchy matters only at large size; accepted, which is why the kill is scoped to exact sums.
* **Checked by**: round-6 per-leaf critic (2026-11-16).
* **Lesson**: L39 (`library.md`)

### R7.1 — Local-feature hidden-fermion pairing state, LH-HFPS (Route 7.1) (SHELVED)
* **The idea**: R6.1′ moved into the Foil's pairing frame: $\psi=e^{J}\det(F_0[\mathrm{occ}_\uparrow,\mathrm{occ}_\downarrow]+K[\mathrm{occ}_\uparrow,:]C(\mathbf n)[:,\mathrm{occ}_\downarrow])$ with a singlet pair function $F_0$, one patch-local hidden row per site and a learned linear coupling $K$, plus a two-body density Jastrow for pair-number size consistency. Hops change a row, a column and $b$ hidden rows of an augmented matrix, so ratios are small determinants and $C_E=\mathcal{O}(N_e^2)$.
* **Lineage**: Operation B.1 (frame change), round 7, a critic seed; its wave function was re-implemented by the 11c compiled engine (`full`) and the Round 13 Schur engine (R13.1); its sublattice variants are R11.1 and R11.2.
* **Outcome**: SHELVED (2026-11-16: the 1200-step T3 was built, pre-flighted and HELD by the orchestrator; the route was DOWNGRADED to an accuracy tool without a cost case).
* **Why shelved**: the T3 (instances 0, 3 and 4, three seeds, 1200 SR steps, arms route, one-site foil and dense HFPS) needs about 820 CPU-hours, far outside this brainstorm's low-compute constraint; the Round 8c gate had fired (cost per decorrelated sample against dense HFPS at $L=16$: $R_D=0.99\pm0.56$, KILL-COST, uncertain), so even a T3 PASS would not carry the leaf's cost claim. Later engines confirmed the missing cost case for this wave function: 11c compiled `full` gave $R_D(16)=1.78\pm0.52$ and the Schur engine $3.50\pm0.85$ at $L=16$ and $2.02\pm0.80$ at $L=24$, all below the bars. The T2 PROMOTE (job 699118: $\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}$ 0.0126 against 0.0170 for the one-site pairing foil and 0.0880 for NNBF at 75 steps) is learning speed: NNBF reaches 0.0159 at 1200 steps, so $\rho_N\approx0.80$ against converged NNBF.
* **What would unshelve it**: a size or engine at which dense HFPS is measurably more expensive per decorrelated sample by a factor of at least 3 (the operation model puts that at $L\approx28$), or a re-scoping of the question as pairing-frame accuracy, which belongs to P1 and P4 rather than to this leaf; the package is in `tests/P2.1_subcubic_amplitudes_r10/`.

### R7.2 — Two-layer local trunk, LH2 (Route 7.2) (KILLED)
* **The idea**: Feed R6.1′'s hidden rows from a two-layer local network, so each hidden row is nonlinear over a radius-$2r$ receptive field while the number of rows changed by a hop stays a lattice constant, keeping $C_E=\mathcal{O}(N_e^2)$ and adding nonlinear range.
* **Lineage**: Operation A on R6.1′'s sharpest gap (round 7), a critic seed.
* **Outcome**: KILLED at G0 (2026-11-16, Gate 0 inside the portfolio and Gate 3 for testing).
* **Kill rule**: Gate 0 within the portfolio (is it a separate route or a parameter of an existing one?) and Gate 3 for testing (B7.3: the mechanism must bite on instance 4 against a foil that isolates it).
* **Evidence**: the changed-row count and cost class depend only on the receptive field, which a one-layer patch of the same radius matches; two plus-shaped layers reach Manhattan radius 2, covering 11 of the 15 sites of instance 4 from its centre.
* **Why this kills it**: it is a point on R6.1′'s patch-size axis, and at exact-sum size its features are nearly global, so a PASS would show only that near-global features help.
* **What the kill does not rule out**: a deeper local trunk tested by variational Monte Carlo at large size, together with R6.4.
* **Strongest case against the kill**: a two-layer trunk is more parameter-efficient than a wide patch; accepted but outside the cost question.
* **Checked by**: not separately reviewed; the Round 11 per-leaf critic (2026-11-16) read the leaf from Round 6 on and did not contest it.
* **Lesson**: L39 (`library.md`)

### R7.3 — Sparse hidden–hidden HFDS, SH-HFDS (Route 7.3) (KILLED)
* **The idea**: R6.1′ plus a configuration-dependent sparse hidden–hidden block $I+L(\mathbf n)$, with $L$ coupling nearest-neighbour hidden rows through local features. By the Schur complement the effective backflow contains $(I+L)^{-1}=\sum_k(-L)^k$, products of local features along lattice paths, so the correction is non-local and nonlinear (string-like, as for a hole moving in a spin background) while a hop still changes only $1+b$ rows.
* **Lineage**: Operation B.3, cross-field translation from Gaussian Markov random fields (round 7).
* **Source analogy**: in Gaussian Markov random fields a sparse precision matrix yields a dense covariance while every conditional stays local (Rue and Held, Gaussian Markov Random Fields, 2005); it works because the model carries the sparse matrix, never its dense inverse. Correspondence: precision is $I+L$, covariance is the resolvent. Translation gap: the determinant needs the augmented inverse of dimension $N+V$, whose update dominates the sweep.
* **Outcome**: KILLED at CRITIC (2026-11-16, Round 11 closure critic, after a T2 PROMOTE by rule).
* **Kill rule**: as R6.1″: a cost-reduction route survives only with a per-sample cost advantage at equal accuracy against the cheapest same-frame cubic baseline; short-horizon parameter-matched ratios cannot promote.
* **Evidence**: job 699305, instance 4, seed 11, 75 steps: $\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}$ 0.0523 (route), 0.0655 (best local-hidden-row foil), 0.0880 (NNBF), so $\rho_N=0.594$ and $\rho_F=0.798$, PROMOTE by rule; the route was 8 times slower per step than NNBF (30.4 s against 3.7 s). Round 8b cost per decorrelated sample against NNBF of width 6 at $L=16$: $R(16)=1.47\pm0.60$, DOWNGRADE (uncertain).
* **Why this kills it**: the accuracy evidence is learning speed at matched parameters, and the cost evidence shows no advantage at $L\le16$; the route carries no part of the leaf's claim.
* **What the kill does not rule out**: a compiled engine without Green's tables (the 11c lesson), which was never built for this family, and a converged comparison at $L\ge16$.
* **Strongest case against the kill**: the critic's "update bottleneck" reasoning used a cost constant that Round 11c later showed was an artefact of carrying unnecessary tables, so the cost half of this kill is weaker than stated; unresolved, and the accuracy half stands.
* **Checked by**: Round 11 per-leaf critic (2026-11-16).
* **Lesson**: L33 (`library.md`)

### R7.4 — Linear-attention backflow with a global summary (Route 7.4) (KILLED)
* **The idea**: Orbital rows $\Phi_0[x_j]+\phi(q_j)^{\top}SW$ with a global summary $S=\sum_k\phi(k_k)v_k^{\top}$ of all tokens, as in linear attention. A hop changes $S$ by rank $\mathcal{O}(1)$, so ratios stay cheap while every row sees the whole configuration.
* **Lineage**: Operation B.3, cross-field translation from machine learning (round 7).
* **Source analogy**: linear attention (Katharopoulos et al., ICML 2020, arXiv:2006.16236) replaces softmax attention by a kernel feature map so that a global key–value summary updates by rank one per token; it works because the summary is linear in the tokens. Translation gap: a linear readout of a fixed-size summary has bounded rank, which is the GK-BF trap.
* **Outcome**: KILLED at G4 (2026-11-16, T0).
* **Kill rule**: Gate 4: is the sign capacity size-consistent at fixed width, within the F1 cost bound?
* **Evidence**: a linear readout of $S$ makes the global correction rank at most $\dim\phi$, one fixed-size hidden-fermion factor shared by all blocks (as R6.1); a nonlinear readout changes every row per hop, which is the F1 bound $C_E=\Omega(N_e^3)$.
* **Why this kills it**: either branch fails a frozen boundary condition.
* **What the kill does not rule out**: attention with local key–value windows feeding local hidden rows.
* **Strongest case against the kill**: none raised; it is an identity plus the F1 bound.
* **Checked by**: not separately reviewed; the Round 11 per-leaf critic (2026-11-16) read the leaf from Round 6 on and did not contest it.
* **Lesson**: L53 (`library.md`)

### R11.1 — Sublattice hidden rows, checkerboard `half` at $F=4$ (Round 11b, 11c, 12, 12b) (KILLED)
* **The idea**: Keep R7.1's pairing-frame wave function but place hidden rows only on the checkerboard sublattice ($v_h=V_h/V=1/2$), so the carried augmented inverse shrinks from dimension $N+V$ to $N+V/2$. The accepted-move update, which dominates the sweep, scales as the square of that dimension, so the route becomes several times cheaper than dense HFPS while staying size-consistent.
* **Lineage**: reopening-ledger ingredient (the Round 11 Saturation Diagnostic named "fewer hidden rows than sites" as the structural falsifier); cost-tested in 11b (Python, diagnostic stands) and 11c (compiled, falsified the diagnostic); its larger-$F$ mutation is R12.3.
* **Outcome**: KILLED at T1 (2026-11-16, job 704752).
* **Kill rule**: Round 12 T1 rule, unchanged in Round 12b: "KILL the variant if $\rho>1.25$ at 2 or more counted sizes", with $\rho=\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}(\text{variant})/\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}(\texttt{full})$ at fixed $F=4$, unprojected exact-force SR trained to a plateau, and a size counted only if converged with $\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}(\texttt{full})\ge10^{-3}t$.
* **Evidence**: cost (11c, compiled, untrained parameters, $L=16$): $R_D=5.05\pm1.27$ times cheaper per decorrelated sample than dense HFPS. Accuracy, OBC $t$–$t'$–$J$ toys with $V=8$, 12, 14, 15, seed 11: $\rho=2.44$, 1.59, 2.07 and 1.80, all four counted.
* **Why this kills it**: removing half of the hidden rows loses sign quality by a factor of 1.6 to 2.4 at every size, so the cost gain is bought with accuracy, against the leaf's goal.
* **What the kill does not rule out**: the same placement with more features per row (R12.3), and placements that depend on the configuration (R13.2).
* **Strongest case against the kill**: at fixed $F$ the variant also has 30 to 50% fewer parameters, so the test mixes row count with parameter count; the Round 12d matched-parameter comparison (B) was designed to separate them and has not run.
* **Checked by**: orchestrator applied the pre-registered aggregate (2026-11-16); no per-leaf critic yet.
* **Lesson**: L28 (`library.md`)

### R11.2 — Sublattice hidden rows, `quarter` at $F=4$ (Round 11b, 11c, 12, 12b) (KILLED)
* **The idea**: As R11.1 with hidden rows only on sites whose two coordinates are both even ($v_h=1/4$), shrinking the carried augmented inverse further, to dimension $N+V/4$, and making the sweep about ten times cheaper than dense HFPS; 25% of the sites then lie in no feature patch.
* **Lineage**: reopening-ledger ingredient, as R11.1.
* **Outcome**: KILLED at T1 (2026-11-16, job 704752).
* **Kill rule**: the Round 12 T1 rule quoted in R11.1, plus the Round 12c margin rule: "if the ladder KILLs with $\rho>5$ at 2 or more counted sizes, larger $F$ could not plausibly bring it under the kill bar".
* **Evidence**: cost (11c, $L=16$): $R_D=10.26\pm2.45$. Accuracy, same toys as R11.1: $\rho=9.92$, 1.86, 6.15 and 2.75 at $V=8$, 12, 14, 15, all counted; $\rho>5$ at two sizes.
* **Why this kills it**: the sign loss is large at every size and exceeds the factor of 4 that any capacity step in this family has bought, so no larger-$F$ rescue is attempted.
* **What the kill does not rule out**: configuration-dependent placement (R13.2).
* **Strongest case against the kill**: as R11.1 (fewer parameters at fixed $F$); the factor-4 margin rule is a heuristic from one observed step; unresolved.
* **Checked by**: orchestrator applied the aggregate (2026-11-16); no per-leaf critic yet.
* **Lesson**: L28 (`library.md`)

### R12.3 — Checkerboard `half` at larger hidden width $F$ (Round 12 option 3; Rounds 12c and 12d) (KILLED)
* **The idea**: Buy back the sign capacity that R11.1 lost by giving each checkerboard hidden row more features ($F=16$ instead of 4). The expensive accepted-move update depends on the matrix dimension $N+V_h$, not on $F$, which enters only the cheap ratio terms, so the cost advantage over dense HFPS should largely survive while the rows become more expressive.
* **Lineage**: Operation A on R11.1 after its T1 kill (Round 12 decision tree, option 3); cost-gated in 12c, accuracy-tested in 12d (job 706240, cancelled at the pause) and completed at $V=15$ in Round 14 (job 731409). No successor yet; generation round 15 is in progress.
* **Outcome**: KILLED at T1 (2026-11-17, job 731409).
* **Kill rule**: Round 12d comparison A, written before any scored run: with $\rho(V)=\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}(\texttt{half\_F16})/\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}(\texttt{full\_F4})$ on the counted sizes, KILL by the trend clause if $\rho$ rises strictly over the counted sizes and $\rho(V_{\max})>1.15$ (marginal, critic first, if $\rho(V_{\max})<1.5$); otherwise PASS-TO-T2 at unmatched parameters.
* **Evidence**: OBC $t$–$t'$–$J$ toys ($t'=-0.25t$, $J=0.4t$), unprojected SR to a plateau, seed 11, all four sizes converged. $\rho=0.038$, 0.886, 1.110 and 1.915 at $V=8$, 12, 14 and 15 (jobs 706240 and 731409); at $V=15$, $\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}=2.83\times10^{-3}$ for `half_F16` against $1.48\times10^{-3}$ for `full_F4`. Cost (12c, compiled, untrained parameters, $L=16$, against dense HFPS): $R_D=5.34\pm1.47$ at $F=16$, GATE-PASS.
* **Why this kills it**: The ratio rises strictly with size and its largest-size value, 1.915, is above both the 1.15 kill line and the 1.5 marginal line, so the pre-registered trend clause fires outright. Since `half_F16` already has 1.6 to 1.8 times the parameters of `full_F4`, a matched-parameter comparison could only make it worse, and quadrupling $F$ delayed the capacity loss of checkerboard rows by about two sites (from $\rho=2.07$ at $V=14$ at $F=4$ to 1.92 at $V=15$ at $F=16$) without removing it.
* **What the kill does not rule out**: other ways to keep fewer than one hidden row per site with more capacity per row (for example hole-gated rows at $F=8$, R13.7, shelved); trained-VMC accuracy at $L\ge16$, where no test exists; projected training; more seeds (one seed per size).
* **Strongest case against the kill**: one seed per size, and $V=8$ is sector fitting (820 parameters against a 560-dimensional space), so the trend rests on three counted non-trivial sizes with no seed spread. Answered by the margin: $\rho(15)=1.915$ is 0.77 above the kill line and the rise from $V=14$ (1.110) is larger than any seed effect seen in this leaf's earlier ladders. Unresolved: a second seed was not run.
* **Checked by**: pre-registered rule applied by the orchestrator, 2026-11-17 (not marginal, so no critic referral was required by the rule); per-leaf critic, 2026-11-17, had upheld the rule as stated before the result.
* **Lesson**: L28 (`library.md`): fewer than one hidden row per site loses sign capacity on 12- to 15-site clusters; this card shows the loss persists at $F=16$.

### R13.1 — Schur-complement engine for the all-sites route, SCH-full (Round 13.1) (KILLED)
* **The idea**: Keep R7.1's wave function exactly but carry only the physical $N\times N$ inverse $A^{-1}$ and a table $Q=A^{-1}K[\mathrm{up},:]$, forming the few needed hidden entries on demand, instead of the $(N+V)$-dimensional augmented inverse. Each accepted move then costs about $2kN(N+V_h)$ instead of $2k(N+V)^2$, predicted 3.2 times cheaper, with ratios still exact.
* **Lineage**: Operation A on the 11c engine (Round 13), with the selected-inversion idea from sparse direct solvers; mutated into R13.1′.
* **Outcome**: KILLED at T1 (2026-11-16).
* **Kill rule**: pre-registered Round 13 T1 cost rule: "PASS-cost if $R_D(16)-\sigma\ge3$; KILL-cost otherwise", where $R_D$ is the cost per decorrelated sample of dense HFPS (`dense2`) over the route's.
* **Evidence**: $L=16$, compiled, untrained parameters: $R_D=3.50\pm0.85$, so $R_D-\sigma=2.66$; it beats the 11c engine on the same wave function by $1.81\pm0.34$; exactness $3.3\times10^{-14}$ against the 11c ratios.
* **Why this kills it**: the margin over the cubic baseline is not resolved at $1\sigma$, so the all-sites route has no demonstrated cost case at $L=16$ even with the best engine.
* **What the kill does not rule out**: the engine as the cheapest implementation of the all-sites wave function, and larger $L$ (R13.1′).
* **Strongest case against the kill**: the central value 3.50 is above 3 and the error is dominated by $\tau_{\mathrm{int}}$ noise on untrained parameters; unresolved, and the pre-registered rule demanded resolution.
* **Checked by**: no per-leaf critic yet (Round 13 has not been reviewed).
* **Lesson**: L47 (`library.md`)

### R13.1′ — Schur engine scored at $L=24$ (Round 13.1′) (KILLED)
* **The idea**: The same engine and wave function as R13.1, scored where the class difference should show: per sweep the route is $\mathcal{O}(N_e^3)$ and dense HFPS $\mathcal{O}(N_e^4)$, so the cost ratio should grow roughly linearly in $N_e$ and clear a higher bar at $L=24$ than at $L=16$.
* **Lineage**: Operation A on R13.1 after its kill (Round 13, amendment A1).
* **Outcome**: KILLED at T1 (2026-11-16).
* **Kill rule**: amendment A1: "PASS-cost if $R_D(24)-\sigma\ge4.5$; KILL otherwise".
* **Evidence**: $L=24$: dense HFPS sweep 1.57 s with $\tau_{\mathrm{int}}=0.83\pm0.27$; route 0.404 s with $\tau_{\mathrm{int}}=1.60\pm0.36$; $R_D(24)=2.02\pm0.80$; the low-noise sweep-time ratio is 3.89 (3.54 at $L=16$), also below 4.5.
* **Why this kills it**: the ratio barely grows from $L=16$ to 24, because the dense LU runs more efficiently as $N$ grows, so the asymptotic class gain arrives too slowly to matter at reachable sizes.
* **What the kill does not rule out**: $L\ge32$, and a GPU setting with different kernel efficiencies.
* **Strongest case against the kill**: the larger $\tau$ of the route at $L=24$ may be noise; even the sweep-time ratio alone (3.89) misses 4.5, so the kill does not rest on $\tau$.
* **Checked by**: no per-leaf critic yet.
* **Lesson**: L47 (`library.md`)

### R13.2 — Hole-gated hidden rows, `hg` (Round 13.2) (KILLED)
* **The idea**: Activate a hidden row only on empty sites: $\psi=e^{J}\det(F_0+\sum_{m:n_m=0}K[:,m]h_m\Omega_m)[\mathrm{up},\mathrm{dn}]$. At doping $\delta$ there are only $\delta V$ active rows (32 at $L=16$), so the carried matrix and the update shrink sharply, while rows sit where the physics (moving holes) needs them; the sum is order-free and extensive, hence size-consistent.
* **Lineage**: Operation A (configuration-dependent placement) on R11.1 and R11.2 after their kills (Round 13); its correspondence with vertex insertion in continuous-time impurity QMC was noted only in the Analogy Map; R13.7 was its gated larger-$F$ mutation.
* **Outcome**: KILLED at T2 (2026-11-17, scored from the partial data of cancelled job 707185; the kill clause's inputs are complete).
* **Kill rule**: Round 13 decision tree for the ladder, which applies the Round 12 rule over sizes 20 to 25: "KILL (two counted sizes with $\rho>1.25$): `hg` is dead, and with it hole-only placement".
* **Evidence**: cost (Round 13 T1, $L=16$): $R_D=4.45\pm1.04$, the only P2.1 route to pass a cost gate on a new wave function at $1\sigma$. Accuracy, OBC $t$–$t'$–$J$ toys, seed 11, $F=4$, against `full_F4`: $\rho=0.83$ at $V=8$, 1.37 at $V=12$ ($1.09\times10^{-2}$ against $7.95\times10^{-3}$), 2.04 at $V=15$ ($3.02\times10^{-3}$ against $1.48\times10^{-3}$), all converged; $V=14$ unconverged (cancelled). Aggregate output: KILL at 2 counted sizes.
* **Why this kills it**: the pre-registered kill clause needs two counted sizes above 1.25 and has them; the missing size cannot reverse it; the ratios grow with $V$ beyond $V=8$, so the $V=8$ win does not extend to clusters where `full` keeps a real sign error.
* **What the kill does not rule out**: hole-gated rows with more features (R13.7, shelved), combinations with fixed rows (R13.3 was killed on cost), and larger seeds; the evidence is a single seed.
* **Strongest case against the kill**: the job was cancelled, the kill comes from a partial array scored after the pause, and the $V=8$ point ($\rho=0.83$) is in a near-sector-fitting regime that cannot vouch for either side; the per-leaf critic checked this and upheld the kill; the kill clause is monotone in added sizes, so completing the array could not reverse it, but a second seed could move $\rho$ at $V=12$ (1.37) near the bar.
* **Checked by**: per-leaf critic, 2026-11-17: upheld; aggregate re-run (rho 0.83/1.37/2.04); n_eff matched to full_F4, both plateau-converged, hg trained longer than the reference; the kill clause is monotone in added sizes; single seed is the only open scope.
* **Lesson**: L28 (`library.md`)

### R13.3 — Checkerboard plus hole-gated rows, `cbh` (Round 13.3) (KILLED)
* **The idea**: Hidden rows on the checkerboard always, and on the other sublattice when that site is empty, so the family contains `half` (R11.1) and adds rows where holes move. Its active-row count lies between `half` and the all-sites route, aiming to recover capacity at intermediate cost.
* **Lineage**: Operation A combining the levers of R11.1 and R13.2 (Round 13).
* **Outcome**: KILLED at T1 (2026-11-16).
* **Kill rule**: Round 13 T1 cost rule: "PASS-cost if $R_D(16)-\sigma\ge3$; KILL-cost otherwise".
* **Evidence**: $L=16$: $R_D=1.71\pm0.43$, $R_D-\sigma=1.27$; the `half` arm on the same Schur engine was slower than `full_S` (0.056 s against 0.035 s per sweep), because the gathered active-row loops are scalar code.
* **Why this kills it**: it is not cheaper than dense HFPS by the pre-registered factor, so it has no cost case.
* **What the kill does not rule out**: the same family with a matrix–matrix gather for the active rows, which would remove the scalar-loop penalty.
* **Strongest case against the kill**: the kill is partly an implementation artefact (scalar loops), recorded in the leaf; unresolved, and the route would also inherit `half`'s capacity loss.
* **Checked by**: no per-leaf critic yet.
* **Lesson**: L47 (`library.md`)

### R13.4 — Shared low-rank hidden couplings, LR (Round 13.4) (KILLED)
* **The idea**: Factor the hidden-row coupling as $KC=\sum_\rho U_\rho(\mathbf n)\bar\Omega_\rho$ with $R$ shared basis rows, so the carried matrix has dimension $N+R$ instead of $N+V$ and the update shrinks, while every site still contributes through $U=K\bar H(\mathbf n)$.
* **Lineage**: Operation A (Round 13, option a).
* **Outcome**: KILLED at G4 (2026-11-16, T0).
* **Kill rule**: Gate 4 (operation count at size) and Gate 2 (is it beaten by a sibling foil?).
* **Evidence**: a move changes $U$ by $K[:,S]\Delta\bar H_S$, rank $b$ as in `full`, so the update rank stays $b+2$; size consistency needs $R\propto V$, so the saving over R13.1 is at most $(N+V)/(N+R)$, 1.5 times at $R=V/2$.
* **Why this kills it**: it saves at most a factor 1.5 on one term against R13.1, which has the same rank and full capacity.
* **What the kill does not rule out**: low-rank couplings with an update rank that does not grow with $b$.
* **Strongest case against the kill**: none raised.
* **Checked by**: no per-leaf critic yet.
* **Lesson**: route-specific — dominated by a sibling engine.

### R13.5 — Local sign factor on the pairing reference, LSF (Round 13.5) (KILLED)
* **The idea**: $\psi=e^J\det F_0[\mathrm{up},\mathrm{dn}]\prod_mg_m(\mathbf n)$, where each $g_m$ is a real patch-local factor that may change sign; each ratio costs $\mathcal{O}(N)$, and the product is size-consistent, so local sign corrections ride on the Foil's frame almost for free.
* **Lineage**: Operation B (Round 13).
* **Outcome**: KILLED at G0 (2026-11-16).
* **Kill rule**: Gate 0: is it known, with its adoption barrier unaddressed?
* **Evidence**: it is the correlator-product-state times Slater (or pairing) ansatz (Changlani et al., Phys. Rev. B 80, 245116, 2009), already killed in P1.2 by the master's post-critic pass.
* **Why this kills it**: a published family whose limitation (local sign factors cannot fix non-local nodal errors) the route does not address.
* **What the kill does not rule out**: sign factors that depend on non-local features.
* **Strongest case against the kill**: none raised.
* **Checked by**: no per-leaf critic yet; consistent with the master's P1.2 kill.
* **Lesson**: route-specific — a known family.

### R13.6 — Delayed acceptance with `half` as surrogate, DA (Round 13.6) (KILLED)
* **The idea**: Screen proposals with the cheap `half` wave function (R11.1) and evaluate the expensive all-sites ratio only for proposals that pass, so most of the sweep runs at `half`'s cost while the chain samples the all-sites distribution exactly.
* **Lineage**: Operation B (Round 13), a re-proposal of R2.5's delayed-acceptance idea with a new surrogate.
* **Outcome**: KILLED at G4 (2026-11-16, T0).
* **Kill rule**: Gate 4: operation count of the full sweep at size.
* **Evidence**: every accepted move still pays the all-sites update, and on the 11c engine accepted-move work is about 70% of the sweep, so the saving is bounded by the 30% ratio share, at most a factor 1.4; the local energy still needs all full ratios.
* **Why this kills it**: the bound is far below the factor-3 cost bar.
* **What the kill does not rule out**: delayed acceptance for families where ratios, not updates, dominate.
* **Strongest case against the kill**: none raised.
* **Checked by**: no per-leaf critic yet.
* **Lesson**: route-specific — the update, not the ratio, dominates here.

### R13.7 — Hole-gated rows at $F=8$ (Round 13.7) (SHELVED)
* **The idea**: If hole-gated rows (R13.2) lose capacity at $V\ge12$, give each active row more features ($F=8$): the number of active rows stays $\delta V$, so the expensive update is unchanged and the cost rises only through the cheap $F$ terms (predicted about 1.1 times R13.2, $R_D(16)\approx4.0$).
* **Lineage**: Operation A on R13.2's open size (Round 13, generated after the results; gated, not run).
* **Outcome**: SHELVED (G2 predicted outcome, 2026-11-17). Recorded as a T0 kill by the documentation pass; overturned by the per-leaf critic, because an unmet run condition is not a refutation.
* **Why shelved**: Round 13 pre-registered the accuracy ladder for this route "as a fourth arm only if 13.2 KILLs with every triggering $\rho\le1.5$". R13.2's triggering ratios are 1.37 and 2.04 (partial job 707185); 2.04 exceeds 1.5, so by the pre-registered margin argument a width increase was predicted not to recover a factor-2 sign loss at $V=15$, and the step (cost run plus an accuracy ladder of about 3 tasks of at most 2 CPU-h each) was not worth its cost under G2. The prediction is weaker than it looked: R12.3's $F=4\to16$ change recovered `half` from $\rho=2.07$ to 1.11 at $V=14$, a width recovery of about the size the margin argument rules out.
* **What would unshelve it**: R12.3 passing comparison A at $V=15$ (not met: job 731409 killed R12.3 at $\rho(15)=1.915$, 2026-11-17), or comparisons B and C showing that width recovers sign capacity at matched parameters (not run).
* **Checked by**: per-leaf critic, 2026-11-17: overturned -> SHELVED (G2 predicted outcome); an unmet run condition is not a refutation, and R12.3's F=4->16 recovery (rho 2.07->1.11 at V=14) weakens the margin prediction. Unshelve if R12.3 passes at V=15 or B/C show width recovers capacity at matched parameters.
* **Lesson**: untested; the margin heuristic it was shelved by is route-specific.

### R15.1 — Polished re-score of checkerboard rows at $F=16$ (Round 15.1) (LIVE)
* **The idea**: Test whether R12.3's $V=15$ loss is an optimiser artefact rather than a capacity limit. Both arms were scored at the best point of a noisy SR trajectory, and `half_F16`, with 1.7 times the parameters, sat further above its late-trajectory floor (0.009 against 0.003). Polishing both arms deterministically with exact gradients from their saved best states removes the SR sampling noise, so the remaining sign-error ratio measures the two families, not how far each noisy optimiser happened to get (L33).
* **Lineage**: Operation A on R12.3 after its T1 kill by the Round 12d trend clause (job 731409), Round 15; a revival in the sense of L51, so it has its own pre-registered test and does not overturn the R12.3 verdict. Gates at T0: G0 PASS (a measurement of an in-leaf family), G1 PASS (same ansatz, exact sums), G2 handled by polishing the foil `full_F4` the same way, G3 PASS, G4 inherits R12.3's cost case ($R_D(16)=5.34\pm1.47$). Test: load the saved best parameters of both arms at $V=15$ (instance 25), minimise the exact energy by L-BFGS (at most 300 iterations or the CPU cap), rerun the unchanged budget step for $\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}$. Pre-registered: KILL if $\rho_{\mathrm{pol}}(15)>1.25$; if $\le1.15$, polish $V=12$ and 14 the same way and apply the Round 12d rule to the four polished ratios; between, NOT RESOLVED (per-leaf critic). Guards: each polish must lower its arm's exact energy; an arm still falling by more than $2\times10^{-4}t$ over its last 50 iterations makes the result INCONCLUSIVE. Predicted KILL, $p\approx0.7$; a 5-iteration probe moved $\rho$ from 1.915 to about 1.78.
* **Outcome**: LIVE (T0 passed; T1 running as gated job 742029, `tests/P2.1_subcubic_amplitudes_r15b/`, cap 0.42 CPU-h).

### R15.2 — Checkerboard rows with a radius-2 receptive field (Round 15.2) (KILLED)
* **The idea**: If `half` fails at $V=15$ because no hidden row sees an odd-centred patch, give each even-site row features from its own plus patch and from the four neighbouring odd-centred plus patches (a radius-2 diamond of 13 sites). The row count stays $V/2$, so the carried dimension stays $n=N+V/2$, while each row now sees every feature that the all-sites route's rows see.
* **Lineage**: Operation A on R12.3, Round 15 (the $V=15$ data say width is not the missing ingredient; receptive field might be). A sibling of R7.2 (two-layer local trunk) restricted to the checkerboard. No successor.
* **Outcome**: KILLED at G4 (2026-11-17, T0).
* **Kill rule**: Gate 4 (operation count at size): is the predicted $R_D(16)$ high enough to clear $R_D(16)-\sigma\ge3$ at this leaf's typical $\sigma\approx1.0$ to 1.3, and is it below the measured cost of a foil with full capacity?
* **Evidence**: receptive-field lemma: a hop changes every hidden row whose receptive field contains a touched site. Counted on the square lattice with the 8b proposal (nearest-neighbour and diagonal hops equally likely): plus-patch `half` has $b=4.0$ (matching the 12c measurement of 4.0), plus-patch `full` $b=8$ (measured $k=9.6$), radius-2 `half` $b=0.5\cdot9+0.25\cdot12+0.25\cdot6=9.0$, so $k\approx9.8$, the same as `full`. Converting with two two-term models fitted to the measured 11c costs at $L=16$ (`full` $R_D=1.78$, `half` $R_D=5.05$): $c\propto\alpha n^2k+\beta n^2$ gives $R_D(16)\approx4.2$; $c\propto\alpha n^2k+\gamma$ gives 3.3. Both are below the 4.3 needed to clear the bar at $\sigma=1.3$, and the scalar feature loops grow with a 13-site receptive field (Round 12c measured that feature loops cost 4 to 5 times their operation share), which only lowers them.
* **Why this kills it**: once the receptive field is wide enough to restore the missing features, the update rank equals the all-sites route's, and the only remaining saving is the $n^2$ factor; the predicted cost lands at or below R13.1's measured $3.50\pm0.85$, a foil that has the full capacity and already failed the bar.
* **What the kill does not rule out**: placement that adapts to the configuration with $b$ held near 4 (R15.6, since killed at T1), and a GPU or batched engine with different kernel speeds.
* **Strongest case against the kill**: the first model's central value (4.2) is within 0.1 of the threshold, and both models are two-point fits; unresolved, but even a pass would need an accuracy test that L39 limits to kill-only at $V\le15$ (a radius-2 diamond covers 13 of 15 sites), so a T1 pass could not promote it.
* **Checked by**: self-gated by the round-15 route generator (2026-11-17); no per-leaf critic yet.
* **Lesson**: L58 (`library.md`): in patch-local hidden-row families the update rank is the number of rows whose receptive field contains a touched site, so thinning rows while widening receptive fields saves only the matrix dimension.

### R15.3 — Schur engine for the all-sites route scored at $L=32$ (Round 15.3) (KILLED)
